F_{1}:=\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\operatorname{\mathbb{Z}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}}}(\mathbf{A}^{1},E_{1}),F)
lies in
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
by
Lemma
1.5
(1)
.

∎

2.11
.

We recall some standard terminology.
Let
(X,D)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}_{ls}
,
Y\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
, and let
f:Y\to X
be a
k
-morphism of finite type.
We say
D
is transversal to
f
, if for any number of irreducible components
D_{1},\ldots,D_{r}

of the SNCD
|D|
, the morphism
f
intersects the scheme-theoretic intersection

D_{1}\cap\ldots\cap D_{r}
transversally
(i.e., the scheme-theoretic inverse image
f^{-1}(D_{1}\cap\ldots\cap D_{r})
is smooth over
k
and of codimension

r
in
Y
). Note that
f
is always transversal to the empty divisor.

If
f
is a closed immersion we also say
Y
and
D
intersect transversally
.
Since
X
is of finite type over a perfect field, this is equivalent to say, that for any point
x\in Y\cap D

we find a regular sequence of parameters
t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}\in\mathcal{O}_{X,x}
,
such that
\mathcal{O}_{Y,x}=\mathcal{O}_{X,x}/(t_{1},\ldots,t_{s})
and the irreducible components of
|D|
containing
x

are in
\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal{O}_{X,x}
given by
V(t_{s+1}),\ldots,V(t_{r})
, with
1\leq s\leq r\leq n
.

Theorem 2.12
.

Let
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
and
\mathcal{X}=(X,D)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}_{ls}
.
Assume there is a smooth irreducible component
D_{0}
of
|D|
which has multiplicity 1 in
D
.
Let
Z\subset X
be a smooth closed subscheme which is contained in
D_{0}
and intersects
|D-D_{0}|
transversally.
Let
\rho:Y\to X
be the blow-up in
Z
.
Then the natural map

F_{\mathcal{X}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}R\rho_{*}F_{(Y,\rho^{*}D)}

is an isomorphism in the derived category of abelian Nisnevich sheaves on
X
.

The proof is given in
2.16
.
The key point is to understand the case of the blow-up of
\mathbf{A}^{2}
in the origin with
D_{0}
a line,
which is established in the next Lemma. Here, after some preliminary steps, we are reduced to prove the vanishing of the
cohomology of the pushforward of
F
along the projection from the blow-up to the exceptional divisor.
This is where the modulus descent, i.e., Proposition
2.5
, is crucially used.

Lemma 2.13
.

Let
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
and
\mathcal{X}=(X,D)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}_{ls}
. Let
\rho:Y\to\mathbf{A}^{2}
be the blow-up in the origin
0\in\mathbf{A}^{2}

and let
L
be a line containing
0
. Then

R^{i}\rho_{X*}F_{(Y,\rho^{*}L)\otimes\mathcal{X}}=0,\quad\text{for all }i\geq 1,

where
\rho_{X}:=\rho\times{\operatorname{id}}_{X}:Y\times X\to\mathbf{A}^{2}\times X
is the base change of
\rho
.

Proof.

We can assume
X
is henselian local
and

L=V(x)\subset\mathbf{A}^{2}=\operatorname{Spec}k[x,y].

Set

\mathcal{F}:=F_{(Y,\rho^{*}L)\otimes\mathcal{X}};

it is a Nisnevich sheaf on
Y\times X
. For
i\geq 1
, the higher direct images

R^{i}\rho_{X*}\mathcal{F}
are supported in
0\times X
whence

H^{j}(\mathbf{A}^{2}_{X},R^{i}\rho_{X*}\mathcal{F})=0,\quad\text{for all }i,j\geq 1,

and

R^{i}\rho_{X*}\mathcal{F}=0\Longleftrightarrow H^{0}(\mathbf{A}^{2}_{X},R^{i}\rho_{X*}\mathcal{F})=0.

Furthermore,
\rho_{X*}\mathcal{F}=F_{(\mathbf{A}^{2},L)\otimes\mathcal{X}}
, since
(Y,\rho^{*}L)\otimes\mathcal{X}\cong(\mathbf{A}^{2},L)\otimes\mathcal{X}
in

\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
(see
[
KMSY21a
, 1]
). Hence by Lemma
2.10

H^{i}(\mathbf{A}^{2}_{X},\rho_{X*}\mathcal{F})=H^{i}(\mathbf{A}^{2}_{X},F_{(\mathbf{A}^{2},L)\otimes\mathcal{X}})=0.

Thus the Leray spectral sequence yields

(2.13.1)

H^{0}(\mathbf{A}_{X}^{2},R^{i}\rho_{X*}\mathcal{F})=H^{i}(Y\times X,\mathcal{F}),\quad i\geq 0,

and we have to show, that this group vanishes for
i\geq 1
.
Write

Y=\operatorname{Proj}k[x,y][S,T]/(xT-yS)\subset\mathbf{A}^{2}\times\mathbf{P}^{1},

and denote by

\pi:Y\times X\hookrightarrow\mathbf{A}^{2}\times\mathbf{P}^{1}_{X}\to\mathbf{P}^{1}_{X}=\operatorname{Proj}\mathcal{O}_{X}[S,T]

the morphism induced by projection.
In order to show that (
2.13.1
) vanishes, we can project along
\pi
and use the Leray spectral sequence

H^{i-j}(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{X},R\pi^{j}_{*}\mathcal{F})\Rightarrow H^{i}(Y\times X,\mathcal{F})

to reduce the problem to showing that

(2.13.2)

H^{i}(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{X},R^{j}\pi_{*}\mathcal{F})=0,\quad i\geq 1,j\geq 0.

The terms
R^{j}\pi_{*}\mathcal{F}
for
j\geq 1
are easy to handle using Lemma
2.10
. Indeed,
set
s=S/T
and write

\mathbf{P}^{1}\setminus\{\infty\}=\mathbf{A}^{1}_{s}:=\operatorname{Spec}k[s],\quad\mathbf{P}^{1}\setminus\{0\}=\operatorname{Spec}k[\tfrac{1}{s}].

Set
U:=\mathbf{A}^{1}_{s}\times X
and
V:=(\mathbf{P}^{1}\setminus\{0\})\times X
and

\mathcal{U}:=(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{s},0)\otimes\mathcal{X},\quad\mathcal{V}:=(\mathbf{P}^{1}\setminus\{0\})\otimes\mathcal{X}.

We have

\pi^{-1}(U)=\mathbf{A}^{1}_{y}\times U,\quad\pi^{-1}(V)=\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x}\times V,

and the restriction of
\pi
to these open subsets is given by projection.
Furthermore by construction,

(2.13.3)

\mathcal{F}_{|\pi^{-1}(U)}=F_{(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{y},0)\otimes\mathcal{U}},\quad\mathcal{F}_{|\pi^{-1}(V)}=F_{(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x},0)\otimes\mathcal{V}}.

Thus Lemma
2.10
(in the case
n=1
) yields

R^{j}\pi_{*}\mathcal{F}=0,\quad j\geq 1.

It remains to show

(2.13.4)

H^{i}(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{X},\pi_{*}\mathcal{F})=0,\quad i\geq 1.

Set

(2.13.5)

F_{1}:=\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}({\operatorname{\mathbb{Z}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}}}(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x},0),F).

Note that
F_{1}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
by Lemma
1.5
(1)
.
Let
j:V\hookrightarrow\mathbf{P}^{1}_{X}
be the open immersion. Its base change along
\pi
induces a morphism

(2.13.6)

\iota:(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x},0)\otimes\mathcal{V}\to(Y,\rho^{*}L)\otimes\mathcal{X}\quad\text{in }\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}.

This yields an exact sequence of Nisnevich sheaves on
\mathbf{P}^{1}_{X}

0\to\pi_{*}\mathcal{F}\xrightarrow{\pi_{*}(\iota^{*})}j_{*}F_{1,\mathcal{V}}\to\Gamma\to 0,

defining
\Gamma
; here the first map is injective by the semipurity of
F
.
Since
\Gamma
is supported on
0\times X
we obtain for
i\geq 2

\displaystyle H^{i}(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{X},\pi_{*}\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle=H^{i}(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{X},j_{*}F_{1,\mathcal{V}})

\displaystyle=H^{i}(V,F_{1,\mathcal{V}}),

\displaystyle\text{by Lem \ref{lem:comp-SNCD}},

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\text{by Lem \ref{lem:higher-HI}}.

It remains to prove the vanishing (
2.13.4
) for
i=1
. This will occupy the rest of the proof.
Let

a\colon Y\times X\to\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x}\times\mathbf{P}^{1}\times X

be induced by the base change of the closed immersion
Y\hookrightarrow\mathbf{A}^{2}\times\mathbf{P}^{1}
followed by the
base change of the projection
\mathbf{A}^{2}\to\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x}
.
The map
a
induces a morphism

(2.13.7)

\alpha:(Y,\rho^{*}L)\otimes\mathcal{X}\to(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x},0)\otimes\mathbf{P}^{1}_{\mathcal{X}}\quad\text{in }\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}},

where
\mathbf{P}^{1}_{\mathcal{X}}:=\mathbf{P}^{1}\otimes\mathcal{X}
, and
which precomposed with
\iota
from (
2.13.6
) yields the morphism

(2.13.8)

\alpha\iota:(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x},0)\otimes\mathcal{V}\to(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x},0)\otimes\mathbf{P}^{1}_{\mathcal{X}},

induced by the open immersion
\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x}\times V\hookrightarrow\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x}\times\mathbf{P}^{1}_{X}
. This gives a factorization

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{1,\mathbf{P}^{1}_{\mathcal{X}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}\mathcal{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{*}F_{1,\mathcal{V}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}(\alpha^{*})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}(\iota^{*})}

where the diagonal morphism is injective, by
[
Sai20a
, Thm 3.1(2)]
and the semipurity of
F_{1}
.
This implies that the morphism labeled
\pi_{*}(\alpha^{*})
is injective too.

Similarly,
the embedding
\mathcal{V}\to(\mathbf{P}^{1},0)\otimes\mathcal{X}
, induces another injective morphism

F_{1,(\mathbf{P}^{1},0)\otimes\mathcal{X}}\to j_{*}F_{1,\mathcal{V}}
.

In total, we obtain the following commutative diagram

(2.13.9)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F_{1,\mathbf{P}^{1}_{\mathcal{X}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{*}(\alpha^{*})}
\textstyle{\pi_{*}\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{*}(\iota^{*})}
\textstyle{{\underline{\Sigma}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F_{1,\mathbf{P}^{1}_{\mathcal{X}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{*}((\alpha\iota)^{*})}
\textstyle{j_{*}F_{1,\mathcal{V}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\underline{\Lambda}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F_{1,\mathbf{P}^{1}_{\mathcal{X}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F_{1,(\mathbf{P}^{1},0)\otimes\mathcal{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\underline{\Lambda}}(0)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0,}

with exact rows, defining the cokernels
{\underline{\Sigma}}
,
{\underline{\Lambda}}
and
{\underline{\Lambda}}(0)
, as well as the map
\varphi
.
Applying
R\Gamma(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{X},-)
yields

\textstyle{\Sigma\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\partial_{1}}
\textstyle{H^{1}(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{X},F_{1,\mathbf{P}^{1}_{\mathcal{X}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{X},\pi_{*}\mathcal{F})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\to 0}
\textstyle{\Lambda\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial_{2}}
\textstyle{H^{1}(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{X},F_{1,\mathbf{P}^{1}_{\mathcal{X}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{X},j_{*}F_{1,\mathcal{V}})\to 0}
\textstyle{\Lambda(0)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{X},F_{1,\mathbf{P}^{1}_{\mathcal{X}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{X},F_{1,(\mathbf{P}^{1},0)\otimes\mathcal{X}})=0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

with exact rows and in which the
\partial_{i}
are the connecting homomorphisms
and where

\Sigma:=H^{0}(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{X},{\underline{\Sigma}}),\quad\Lambda:=H^{0}(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{X},{\underline{\Lambda}}),\quad\Lambda(0):=H^{0}(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{X},{\underline{\Lambda}}(0)).

The group
H^{1}(\mathbf{P}^{1},F_{1,(\mathbf{P}^{1},0)\otimes\mathcal{X}})
vanishes by the cube invariance of cohomology, see
[
Sai20a
, Thm 9.3]
, thus
\partial_{2|\Lambda(0)}
is surjective, the vanishing (
2.13.4
) for
i=1
will follow, if we can show

(2.13.10)

\Lambda(0)\subset\varphi(\Sigma).

Note that
{\underline{\Sigma}}
,
{\underline{\Lambda}}
, and
{\underline{\Lambda}}(0)
have support in
0\times X\subset U
, so we can compute the global sections on
U
instead of
\mathbf{P}^{1}
to show (
2.13.10
). Now, since
H^{1}(U,F_{1,\mathcal{U}})=0
, by Lemma
2.10
, unravelling the definitions we obtain
from (
2.13.3
) and (
2.13.9
) with
G:=F(-\otimes\mathcal{X})
and

\mathbf{A}^{1}_{s}=\mathbf{P}^{1}\setminus\{\infty\}
the following descriptions:

\Sigma=\frac{G((\mathbf{A}^{1}_{y},0)\otimes(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{s},0))}{\alpha^{*}G((\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x},0)\otimes\mathbf{A}^{1}_{s})},

\Lambda=\frac{G((\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x},0)\otimes(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{s}\setminus\{0\},\emptyset))}{G((\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x},0)\otimes\mathbf{A}^{1}_{s})},

(2.13.11)

\Lambda(0)=\frac{G((\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x},0)\otimes(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{s},0))}{G((\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x},0)\otimes\mathbf{A}^{1}_{s})}.

By
[
Sai20a
, Lem 5.9]
we have isomorphisms (see Notation
2.1
)

(2.13.12)

\frac{G(\overline{\square}^{(1)}_{x}\otimes(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{s},0))}{G((\mathbf{P}^{1}_{x},\infty)\otimes(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{s},0))}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\frac{G((\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x},0)\otimes(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{s},0))}{G(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x}\otimes(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{s},0))},

(2.13.13)

\frac{G(\overline{\square}^{(1)}_{x}\otimes\overline{\square}^{(1)}_{s})}{G(\overline{\square}^{(1)}_{x}\otimes(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{s},\infty))}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\frac{G(\overline{\square}^{(1)}_{x}\otimes(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{s},0))}{G(\overline{\square}^{(1)}_{x}\otimes\mathbf{A}^{1}_{s})}.

Write
j
for the open immersion
(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x},0)\hookrightarrow\overline{\square}^{(1)}_{x}
. The base change of
j^{*}
induces a commutative diagram

(2.13.14)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G((\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x},0)\otimes\mathbf{A}^{1}_{s})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G((\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x},0)\otimes(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{s},0))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda(0)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G(\overline{\square}^{(1)}_{x}\otimes\mathbf{A}^{1}_{s})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G(\overline{\square}^{(1)}_{x}\otimes(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{s},0)).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{*}}

The horizontal composite morphism is zero by (
2.13.11
), hence the kernel of the diagonal arrow contains
G(\overline{\square}^{(1)}_{x}\otimes\mathbf{A}^{1}_{s})
. Next, note that from (
2.13.12
) we get the surjective morphism

(2.13.15)

G(\overline{\square}^{(1)}_{x}\otimes(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{s},0))\oplus G(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x}\otimes(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{s},0))\to G((\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x},0)\otimes(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{s},0))\to 0.

Combining (
2.13.15
), (
2.13.13
) and (
2.13.14
) we get a surjection

(2.13.16)

G(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x}\otimes(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{s},0))\oplus G(\overline{\square}^{(1)}_{x}\otimes\overline{\square}^{(1)}_{s})\rightarrow\!\!\!\!\!\rightarrow\Lambda(0).

Note that
the pullback of the open immersion
\pi^{-1}(V)\hookrightarrow Y\times X
along
\pi^{-1}(U)\hookrightarrow Y\times X

induces the open immersion

\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x}\times(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{s}\setminus\{0\})\times X\to\mathbf{A}^{1}_{y}\times\mathbf{A}^{1}_{s}\times X

which is induced by base change from the
k[s]
-linear map

k[y,s]\mapsto k[x,s,1/s],\quad y\mapsto x/s.

It gives the following two morphisms in
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}

\iota_{1}:(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x},0)\otimes(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{s}\setminus\{0\})\otimes\mathcal{X}\to(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{y},0)\otimes(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{s},0)\otimes\mathcal{X}.

\iota_{2}:(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x},0)\otimes(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{s}\setminus\{0\})\otimes\mathcal{X}\to\overline{\square}^{(1)}_{y}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(2)}_{s}\otimes\mathcal{X}.

Furthermore, consider the base change of the map (
2.5.1
)

\psi:\overline{\square}^{(1)}_{y}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(2)}_{s}\otimes\mathcal{X}\to\overline{\square}^{(1)}_{x}\otimes\overline{\square}^{(1)}_{s}\otimes\mathcal{X},

which is induced by
x\mapsto ys
; it restricts to

\psi_{1}:(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{y},0)\otimes(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{s},0)\to\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x}\otimes(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{s},0).

In particular,
\psi_{1}\circ\iota_{1}
is induced by the open immersion

\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x}\setminus\{0\}\times\mathbf{A}^{1}_{s}\setminus\{0\}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x}\times\mathbf{A}^{1}_{s}\setminus\{0\}

and
\psi\circ\iota_{2}
is induced by the identity on

\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x}\setminus\{0\}\times\mathbf{A}^{1}_{s}\setminus\{0\}
.
Consider the following diagram

\textstyle{G(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x}\otimes(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{s},0))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{1}^{*}}
\textstyle{G((\mathbf{A}^{1}_{y},0)\otimes(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{s},0))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{r_{1}}
\textstyle{\Sigma\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{G((\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x},0)\otimes(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{s}\setminus\{0\},\emptyset))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{r_{2}}
\textstyle{\Lambda}
\textstyle{G(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x}\otimes(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{s},0))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{r_{3}}
\textstyle{\Lambda(0).\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Here the maps
r_{i}
are the natural maps into the quotients;
the diagram commutes by definition of the morphisms involved.
Hence

(2.13.17)

\operatorname{Im}(G(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x}\otimes(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{s},0))\to\Lambda(0))\subset\varphi(\Sigma).

Consider now the following diagram

\textstyle{G(\overline{\square}^{(1)}_{y}\otimes\overline{\square}^{(1)}_{s})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{r_{1}}
\textstyle{\Sigma\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{G(\overline{\square}^{(1)}_{x}\otimes\overline{\square}^{(1)}_{s})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi^{*}}
\textstyle{G(\overline{\square}^{(1)}_{y}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(2)}_{s})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{2}^{*}}
\textstyle{G((\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x},0)\otimes(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{s}\setminus\{0\},\emptyset))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{r_{2}}
\textstyle{\Lambda}
\textstyle{G(\overline{\square}^{(1)}_{x}\otimes\overline{\square}^{(1)}_{s})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{r_{3}}
\textstyle{\Lambda(0).\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Here the maps
r_{1}
and
r_{3}
are induced by restriction followed by the quotient map using (
2.13.12
) and (
2.13.13
);
the two squares and the triangle on the lower left commute by definition of the morphisms involved;
the map
\psi^{*}
factors via the dotted arrow in the diagram, by Proposition
2.5
.
This shows

\operatorname{Im}(G(\overline{\square}^{(1)}_{x}\otimes\overline{\square}^{(1)}_{s})\to\Lambda(0))\subset\varphi(\Sigma),

which together with (
2.13.17
) and (
2.13.16
)
implies (
2.13.10
). This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Lemma 2.14
.

Let the assumptions and notations be as in Theorem
2.12
. Assume additionally
{\operatorname{codim}}(Z,X)\leq 2
.
Then Theorem
2.12
holds.

Proof.

There is nothing to prove for
{\operatorname{codim}}(Z,X)=1
, we therefore consider the case
{\operatorname{codim}}(Z,X)=2
.
Since
(Y,\rho^{*}D)\cong(X,D)
in
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
we have

\rho_{*}F_{(Y,\rho^{*}D)}\cong F_{(X,D)}
.
Thus it remains to show the vanishing

(2.14.1)

R^{i}\rho_{*}F_{(Y,\rho^{*}D)}=0,\quad\text{for all }i\geq 1.

The question is Nisnevich local around the points in
Z
.
Let
z\in Z
be a point and consider the regular henselian local ring
A=\mathcal{O}_{X,z}^{h}
.
For
V\subset X
set
V_{(z)}:=V\times_{X}\operatorname{Spec}A
.
Denote by
D^{\prime}\subset X
the closed subscheme defined by
D-D_{0}
.
By assumption we find a regular system of local parameters
x,y,t_{1}\ldots,t_{s}
of
A
,
such that
Z_{(z)}=V(x,y)
,
D_{0,(z)}=V(x)
, and

D^{\prime}_{(z)}=V(t_{1}^{n_{1}}\cdots t_{r}^{n_{r}})
, for some
r\leq s
and
n_{i}\geq 1
.
Let
K\hookrightarrow A
be a coefficient field over
k
; we obtain an isomorphism

K\{x,y,t_{1},\ldots,t_{s}\}\xrightarrow{\simeq}A.

Let
\rho_{1}:\widetilde{\mathbf{A}^{2}}\to\mathbf{A}^{2}
be the blow-up in
0
.
By the above the blow-up in
Z

\rho:(Y,\rho^{*}D)\to(X,D)

is Nisnevich locally around
z
over
k
isomorphic to the morphism

(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}^{2}},\rho_{1}^{*}(x))\otimes(\mathbf{A}_{K}^{s},(\prod_{i=1}^{r}t_{i}^{n_{i}}))\to(\mathbf{A}^{2},(x))\otimes(\mathbf{A}_{K}^{s},(\prod_{i=1}^{r}t_{i}^{n_{i}})),

which is induced by base change from
\rho_{1}
.
Hence the vanishing (
2.14.1
) follows from Lemma
2.13
.
∎

Lemma 2.15
.

Let
X
be a finite type
k
-scheme and
Z_{0}\subset Z_{1}\subset X
closed subschemes.
Let
\rho:X^{\prime}\to X
be the blow-up of
X
in
Z_{0}
and let
\rho^{\prime}:X^{\prime\prime}\to X^{\prime}
be the blow-up
of
X^{\prime}
in the strict transform
\tilde{Z}_{1}
of
Z_{1}
.
Furthermore, let
\sigma:Y^{\prime}\to X
be the blow-up in
Z_{1}
and let
\sigma^{\prime}:Y^{\prime\prime}\to Y^{\prime}

be the blow-up of
Y^{\prime}
in
\sigma^{-1}(Z_{0})
. Then there is an isomorphism

\textstyle{X^{\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\rho\rho^{\prime}}
\textstyle{Y^{\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma\sigma^{\prime}}
\textstyle{X.}

Proof.

Recall the following general fact: Let
\mathcal{I},\mathcal{J}\subset\mathcal{O}_{X}
be two coherent ideal sheaves.
Then the blow-up
\tilde{X}\to X
of
X
in
\mathcal{I}\cdot\mathcal{J}
is equal to the composition

X_{2}\xrightarrow{\pi_{2}}X_{1}\xrightarrow{\pi_{1}}X
, where
\pi_{1}
is the blow-up in
\mathcal{I}
and
\pi_{2}
is the blow-up in

\pi_{1}^{-1}\mathcal{J}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{X_{1}}
. This is proven using the universal property of blow-ups, see, e.g.,

[
Sta19
,
Tag 080A
]
.

Here denote by
\mathcal{I}_{i}\subset\mathcal{O}_{X}
the ideal sheaves of
Z_{i}
. We have
\mathcal{I}_{1}\subset\mathcal{I}_{0}
.
Let
\pi:\tilde{X}\to X
be the blow-up of
X
in
\mathcal{I}_{1}\cdot\mathcal{I}_{0}
.
By the remark above,
\pi
is isomorphic as
X
-scheme to
\sigma\sigma^{\prime}
.
Furthermore, note that
\rho^{\prime}
is also equal to the blow-up of
X^{\prime}
in
\rho^{-1}(Z_{1})
.
Indeed, the ideal sheaf of
\rho^{-1}(Z_{1})
is equal to

\rho^{-1}\mathcal{I}_{1}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}=\mathcal{I}_{E}\cdot\tilde{\mathcal{I}}_{1}
, where
\mathcal{I}_{E}
is the ideal sheaf of the exceptional divisor of

\rho
and
\tilde{\mathcal{I}}_{1}
is the ideal sheaf of
\tilde{Z}_{1}
;
since
\mathcal{I}_{E}
is invertible, the blow-ups of
X^{\prime}
in
\tilde{\mathcal{I}}_{1}
and in
\rho^{-1}\mathcal{I}_{1}\cdot\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}

are isomorphic. Thus by the remark above the
X
-scheme
\rho\rho^{\prime}
is isomorphic to
\pi
as well.
∎

2.16
.

Proof of Theorem
2.12
.

The proof is by induction on
c={\operatorname{codim}}(Z,X)
, the induction start for
c\leq 2
being Lemma
2.14
.
Assume
c>2
. The question is local on
X
. Hence we can assume
X=\operatorname{Spec}A
and that there is a regular sequence

y_{1},\ldots,y_{c},t_{1},\ldots,t_{r}\in A
such that
Z=V(y_{1},\ldots,y_{c})
,
D_{0}=V(y_{1})
and
D^{\prime}=D-D_{0}=V(t_{1}^{n_{1}}\cdots t_{r}^{n_{r}})
, for
some
n_{i}\geq 1
. Set
Z_{2}:=V(y_{1},y_{2})
. Let
\rho:Y\to X
be the blow-up in
Z
and denote by
\tilde{Z}_{2}
the strict transform of
Z_{2}
.
Then
\rho^{*}D
has SNC support with the strict transform
\tilde{D}_{0}
of
D_{0}
being a smooth component containing
\tilde{Z}_{2}
.
Furthermore,
\tilde{Z}_{2}
intersects
\rho^{*}D-\tilde{D}_{0}
transversally and
{\operatorname{codim}}(\tilde{Z}_{2},Y)=2
.
Let
\rho^{\prime}:Y^{\prime}\to Y
be the blow-up in
\tilde{Z}_{2}
. By Lemma
2.14
we find

(2.16.1)

R\rho_{*}F_{(Y,\rho^{*}D)}\cong R(\rho\rho^{\prime})_{*}F_{(Y^{\prime},(\rho\rho^{\prime})^{*}D)}.

Let
\sigma:W\to X
be the blow-up in
Z_{2}
and set
Z_{c-1}:=\sigma^{-1}(Z)
.
Then
\sigma^{*}D
has SNC support with the exceptional divisor
E
being a smooth component containing
Z_{c-1}
.
Furthermore
Z_{c-1}
intersects the strict transform of
D
transversally and
{\operatorname{codim}}(Z_{c-1},W)=c-1
.
Let
\sigma^{\prime}:W^{\prime}\to W
be the blow-up in
Z_{c-1}
. By Lemma
2.14
and induction we find

F_{(X,D)}\cong R\sigma_{*}F_{(W,\sigma^{*}D)}\cong R(\sigma\sigma^{\prime})_{*}F_{W^{\prime},(\sigma\sigma^{\prime})^{*}D}.

Thus the statement follows from Lemma
2.15
and (
2.16.1
).∎

2.3.
(\mathbf{P}^{n},\mathbf{P}^{n-1})
-invariance of cohomology

We follow the basic strategy of
[
KS20
, Lem 10]
. See also
[
BPØ
, Prop 7.3.1]
.

Lemma 2.17
.

Let
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
and
\mathcal{X}=(X,D)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}_{ls}
.
Let
x\in\mathbf{P}^{n}
be a
k
-rational point and
L\subset\mathbf{P}^{n}
a hyperplane.
Denote by
\rho:Y\to\mathbf{P}^{n}
the blow-up in
x
.
Denote by
q:Y\times X\to E\times X
the base change
of the morphism
Y\to E
which parametrizes the lines in
\mathbf{P}^{n}
through
x
.
Then the pullback

q^{*}:F_{(E,L^{\prime})\otimes\mathcal{X}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}Rq_{*}F_{(Y,\rho^{*}L)\otimes\mathcal{X}}

is an isomorphism, where
L^{\prime}=\tilde{L}\cap E
,
with
\tilde{L}\subset Y
the strict transform of
L
.
(Note
L^{\prime}=\emptyset
, if
x\not\in L
.)

Proof.

Note that the projection morphism
Y\to E
makes
Y
into a
\mathbf{P}^{1}
-bundle over
E
and induces a morphism

(Y,\rho^{*}L)\otimes\mathcal{X}\to(E,L^{\prime})\otimes\mathcal{X}
.
The latter morphism locally over
E
has the form of the projection
{\overline{\square}}\otimes\mathcal{W}\to\mathcal{W}
, for some
\mathcal{W}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
.
Indeed, over an affine neighborhood
U\subset E
intersecting (resp. not intersecting)
L^{\prime}
,
the modulus pair
\mathcal{W}
can be taken to be
(U,L^{\prime}\cap U)\otimes\mathcal{X}
(resp.
(U,\emptyset)\otimes\mathcal{X}
).
In both cases the divisor
\{\infty\}\times U\times X
on
\mathbf{P}^{1}\times U\times X
is the restriction of the exceptional divisor to
q^{-1}(U)=\mathbf{P}^{1}\times U\times X
.
Thus the statement follows from the cube-invariance of cohomology, see
[
Sai20a
, Thm 9.3]
.
∎

Theorem 2.18
.

Let
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
. Let
L\subset\mathbf{P}^{n}
be a hyperplane and
\mathcal{X}=(X,D)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}_{ls}
.
Then the pullback

F_{\mathcal{X}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}R\pi_{*}F_{(\mathbf{P}^{n},L)\otimes\mathcal{X}},

along the projection
\pi:\mathbf{P}^{n}_{X}\to X
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

The case
n=1
is
[
Sai20a
, Thm 9.3]
.
Assume
n\geq 2
. Let
x\in\mathbf{P}^{n}
be a
k
-rational point,
L\subset\mathbf{P}^{n}
a hyperplane with
x\in L
,
and
\rho:Y\to\mathbf{P}^{n}
the blow-up in
x
.
Then
R\rho_{*}F_{(Y,\rho^{*}L)\otimes\mathcal{X}}=F_{(\mathbf{P}^{n},L)\otimes\mathcal{X}}
by
Theorem
2.12
.
Thus the statement follows from Lemma
2.17
and induction.
∎

Corollary 2.19
.

Let
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
and
\mathcal{X}=(X,D)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}_{ls}
.
Let
V
be a vector bundle on
X
and denote by

\pi:\mathbf{P}(V):=\operatorname{Proj}({\operatorname{Sym}}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(V))\to X

the structure map. Then
\pi^{*}
induces an isomorphism

\pi^{*}:F_{\mathcal{X}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\pi_{*}F_{(\mathbf{P}(V),\pi^{*}D)}.

Proof.

The question is local on
X
, hence we can assume that
V
is trivial of rank
n+1
.
Let
L\subset\mathbf{P}^{n}
be a hyperplane and consider

F_{\mathcal{X}}\xrightarrow{\pi^{*}}\pi_{*}F_{\mathbf{P}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{X}}\hookrightarrow\pi_{*}F_{(\mathbf{P}^{n},L)\otimes\mathcal{X}}.

The second map is injective by semipurity and
[
Sai20a
, Thm 3.1(2)]
;
the composition is an isomorphism by Theorem
2.18
, hence
so is the first map.
∎

3.
Smooth blow-up formula

Theorem 3.1
.

Let
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
and
\mathcal{X}=(X,D)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}_{ls}
.
Let
Z\subset X
be a smooth closed subscheme which intersects
D
transversally.
Consider the following cartesian diagram

\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{E}}
\scriptstyle{i_{E}}
\textstyle{\tilde{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\textstyle{X,}

in which
\rho
is the blow-up of
X
along
Z
. Set

\tilde{\mathcal{X}}=(\tilde{X},D_{|\tilde{X}}),\quad\mathcal{Z}=(Z,D_{|Z}),\quad\mathcal{E}=(E,D_{|E}).

Then there is a distinguished triangle in the bounded derived category of Nisnevich sheaves of abelian groups

D^{b}(X_{{\operatorname{Nis}}})

F_{\mathcal{X}}\xrightarrow{\rho^{*}\oplus(-i^{*})}R\rho_{*}F_{\tilde{\mathcal{X}}}\oplus i_{*}F_{\mathcal{Z}}\xrightarrow{i_{E}^{*}+\,\rho_{E}^{*}}i_{*}R\rho_{E*}F_{\mathcal{E}}\to F_{\mathcal{X}}[1].

Proof.

The first part of the argument is similar to the proof of
[
Gro85
, IV.1.1.]
.
We have to show that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\mathcal{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\rho_{*}F_{\tilde{\mathcal{X}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{*}F_{\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{*}R\rho_{E*}F_{\mathcal{E}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{E}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{E}^{*}}

is homotopy cartesian in
D^{b}(X_{{\operatorname{Nis}}})
. To this end it suffices to show that the following maps are isomorphisms:

(3.1.1)

\rho_{E}^{*}:F_{\mathcal{Z}}\to\rho_{E*}F_{\mathcal{E}},

(3.1.2)

\rho^{*}:F_{\mathcal{X}}\to\rho_{*}F_{\tilde{\mathcal{X}}},

(3.1.3)

i_{E}^{*}:R^{j}\rho_{*}F_{\tilde{\mathcal{X}}}\to i_{*}R^{j}\rho_{E*}F_{\mathcal{E}},\quad j\geq 1.

The map (
3.1.1
) is an isomorphism by Corollary
2.19
, since
E
is a projective bundle over
Z
.
The question for the other two isomorphisms is Nisnevich local.
Since Z and D intersect transversally we can assume that

\mathcal{X}=(\mathbf{A}^{n},\emptyset)\otimes\mathcal{Z}
with
\mathcal{Z}=(Z,D_{Z})\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}_{ls}

and that
\tilde{X}
is the blow up of
X=\mathbf{A}^{n}\times Z
at
\{0\}\times Z

(cf. the proof of Lemma
2.14
).
Write
\mathbf{A}^{n}=\mathbf{P}^{n}\setminus L
and let
Y
be the blow-up of
0\in\mathbf{P}^{n}
and denote by

E_{0}
the exceptional divisor. Note that
L
is embedded isomorphically into
Y
, not intersecting
E_{0}
.

We obtain the diagram

\textstyle{E_{0}\times Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\bar{\imath}_{E}}
\scriptstyle{\bar{\rho}_{E}}
\textstyle{Y\times Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q}
\scriptstyle{\bar{\rho}}
\textstyle{E_{0}\times Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\bar{\rho}_{E}}
\textstyle{Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\bar{\imath}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{P}^{n}\times Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{Z,}

where
\bar{\imath}:Z=\{0\}\times Z\hookrightarrow\mathbf{P}^{n}\times Z
is the closed immersion,

\bar{\rho}
is the base change of the blow-up,
\pi
and
\bar{\rho}_{E}
are the projections and

q
is as in Lemma
2.17
.
It remains to show that the following maps are isomorphisms

(3.1.4)

\bar{\rho}^{*}:F_{(\mathbf{P}^{n},L)\otimes\mathcal{Z}}\to\bar{\rho}_{*}F_{(Y,L)\otimes\mathcal{Z}},

(3.1.5)

\bar{\imath}_{E}^{*}:R^{j}\bar{\rho}_{*}F_{(Y,L)\otimes\mathcal{Z}}\to\bar{\imath}_{*}R^{j}\bar{\rho}_{E*}F_{E_{0}\otimes\mathcal{Z}},\quad j\geq 1.

Indeed, the restriction of these two isomorphisms to
\mathbf{A}^{n}_{Z}=\mathbf{P}^{n}_{Z}\setminus L_{Z}
yields the isomorphisms
(
3.1.2
) and (
3.1.3
).

The map (
3.1.4
) is an isomorphism away from
0\times Z
.
Since source and target of
R\pi_{*}\eqref{thm:bus5}
are both isomorphic to
F_{\mathcal{Z}}

by Theorem
2.18
and Lemma
2.17
, (
3.1.4
) is an isomorphism everywhere.
Similarly, (
3.1.5
) is an isomorphism if
R\pi_{*}\eqref{thm:bus6}
is.
To show the latter, first observe that we have

(3.1.6)

R^{a}\pi_{*}R^{b}\bar{\rho}_{*}(F_{(Y,L)\otimes Z})=0,\quad\text{for }a\neq 0.

Indeed, if
b\geq 1
, then
R^{b}\bar{\rho}_{*}F_{(Y,L)\otimes\mathcal{Z}}
has support in
0\times Z
;
if
b=0
the cohomology for
a\geq 1
vanishes by (
3.1.4
) and Theorem
2.18
.
Now
R\pi_{*}\eqref{thm:bus6}
is equal to the composition

\pi_{*}R^{j}\bar{\rho}_{*}F_{(Y,L)\otimes\mathcal{Z}}\cong R^{j}\bar{\rho}_{E*}Rq_{*}F_{(Y,L)\otimes\mathcal{Z}}\cong R^{j}\bar{\rho}_{E*}F_{E_{0}\otimes\mathcal{Z}},

where the first isomorphism follows from (
3.1.6
) and the Leray spectral sequence and the second isomorphism holds by Lemma
2.17
.
This completes the proof.

∎

4.
Twists

4.1.
A tensor formula for homotopy invariant sheaves

Lemma 4.1
.

(Bloch-Gieseker)
Assume
k
infinite of exponential characteristic
p\geq 1
.
Let
X
be an integral quasi-projective
k
-scheme and
D
a Cartier divisor on
X
.
Let
n\geq 1
be an integer with
(n,p)=1
.
Then there exists a finite and surjective morphism
\pi:Y\to X
and a Cartier divisor
E
on
Y

such that the following properties hold:

(1)

Y
is integral, normal and
\pi^{-1}(X_{\rm sm})
is a smooth open subscheme of
Y
,
where
X_{\rm sm}
is the smooth locus of
X
;

(2)

\pi^{*}D=nE
;

(3)

\deg(\pi)
divides a power of
n
;

(4)

if
D
is effective, then so is
E
.

Proof.

The proof is a slight modification of
[
BG71
, Lem 2.1]
.
First note that
(4)
follows from
(2)
and
(1)
.
Also, it suffices to prove the statement for
D
a very ample divisor.
Let
i:X\hookrightarrow\mathbf{P}^{N}:=\mathbf{P}
be an immersion such that
\mathcal{O}(D)=i^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{P}}(1)
.
By Bertini’s theorem (see, e.g.,
[
Jou83
, I, Cor 6.11]
) we find hyperplanes

H_{0},\ldots,H_{N}\subset\mathbf{P}
such that all the intersections
H_{i_{0}}\cap\ldots\cap H_{i_{r}}

and
H_{i_{0}}\cap\ldots\cap H_{i_{r}}\cap X_{\rm sm}
are transversal (or empty), for all

\{i_{0},\ldots,i_{r}\}\subset\{0,\ldots,N\}
and all
0\leq r\leq N
.
Let
Y_{i}
be a linear polynomial defining
H_{i}
, so that

\mathbf{P}=\operatorname{Proj}k[Y_{0},\ldots,Y_{N}]
.
Let
\Pi:\mathbf{P}\to\mathbf{P}
be the
k
-morphism defined by
Y_{i}\mapsto Y_{i}^{n}
,
i=0,\ldots,N
.
Note that
\Pi
is finite of degree
n^{N}
and it is étale over
\mathbf{P}\setminus\cup_{i}H_{i}
.
Form the cartesian diagram

\textstyle{X^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Pi}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\textstyle{\mathbf{P}.}

Then
X^{\prime}\times_{X}X_{\rm sm}
is smooth: this can be checked after base change to the algebraic closure of
k
and
then the argument is the same as in the second and third paragraph in the proof of
[
BG71
, Lem 2.1]

(the choice of the
H_{i}
is crucial here).
Let
X^{\prime\prime}\subset X^{\prime}
be an irreducible component (with reduced scheme structure) and denote by
Y
the normalization
of
X^{\prime\prime}
and by
\pi:Y\to X
the composition

Y\to X^{\prime\prime}\hookrightarrow X^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\pi^{\prime}}X

and by
E=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{P}}(1)_{|Y}
the pullback of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{P}}(1)
along

Y\to X^{\prime\prime}\hookrightarrow X^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i^{\prime}}}{{\hookrightarrow}}\mathbf{P}.

Then
\pi:Y\to X
and
E
satisfy the conditions of the statement.
∎

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
F,G\in{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}
.
Let

(4.2.1)

\underline{\omega}^{*}F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}\underline{\omega}^{*}G\to\underline{\omega}^{*}(F\otimes_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}G)

be the morphism in
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
, which is induced by adjunction from the isomorphism

\underline{\omega}_{!}(\underline{\omega}^{*}F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}\underline{\omega}^{*}G)\cong(\underline{\omega}_{!}\underline{\omega}^{*}F)\otimes_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\underline{\omega}_{!}\underline{\omega}^{*}G)\cong F\otimes_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}G.

Then we obtain a surjection in
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}NST}}

{\underline{a}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(\eqref{lem:surjT1}):{\underline{a}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}\underline{\omega}^{*}G)\rightarrow\!\!\!\!\!\rightarrow{\underline{a}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}(F\otimes_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}G)).

Proof.

Denote by
H_{l}
(resp.
H_{r}
) the source (resp. target) of (
4.2.1
) and take
\mathcal{X}=(X,D)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
.
By definition,
\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}
(resp.
\otimes_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}
) is the Day convolution of the tensor product on
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
(resp. on
\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}
), so that we have the following presentations
(which also hold for general
(X,D)
, cf.
[
SV00
, §2]
)

H_{l}(X,D)=\left(\bigoplus_{\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{Z}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}}F(\mathcal{Y}^{o})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}G(\mathcal{Z}^{o})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y}\otimes\mathcal{Z})\right)/R_{l},

where for
\mathcal{Y}=(\overline{Y},Y_{\infty})
we set
\mathcal{Y}^{o}=\overline{Y}\setminus Y_{\infty}
, and where
R_{l}

is the subgroup generated by the elements

f^{*}a\otimes g^{*}b\otimes h-a\otimes b\otimes(f\otimes g)\circ h,

where
\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{Y}^{\prime},\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
,
a\in F(\mathcal{Y}^{o})
,
b\in G(\mathcal{Z}^{o})
,

f\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}(\mathcal{Y}^{\prime},\mathcal{Y})
,
g\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}(\mathcal{Z}^{\prime},\mathcal{Z})
, and
h\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}\otimes\mathcal{Z}^{\prime})
. Similarly,

H_{r}(X,D)=\left(\bigoplus_{Y,Z\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}}F(Y)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}G(Z)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(X\setminus D,Y\times Z)\right)/R_{r},

where
R_{r}
is the subgroup generated by

f^{*}a\otimes g^{*}b\otimes h-a\otimes b\otimes(f\times g)\circ h,

where
Y,Y^{\prime},Z,Z^{\prime}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
,
a\in F(Y)
,
b\in G(Z)
,
f\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(Y^{\prime},Y)
,
g\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(Z^{\prime},Z)
, and

h\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(X\setminus D,Y^{\prime}\times Z^{\prime})
.

Let
\sum_{i}a_{i}\otimes b_{i}\otimes\gamma_{i}\in H_{r}(X,D)
, where
a_{i}\in F(Y_{i})
,
b_{i}\in G(Y_{i})
,
and
\gamma_{i}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(X\setminus D,Y_{i}\times Z_{i})
.
By
[
KMSY21a
, Thm 1.6.2]
we find a proper morphism
\rho:X^{\prime}\to X
inducing an isomorphism

X^{\prime}\setminus|\rho^{*}D|\xrightarrow{\simeq}X\setminus|D|
, such that the closure of any irreducible component
of
\gamma_{i}
in
X^{\prime}\times Y_{i}\times Z_{i}
is finite over
X^{\prime}
, for all
i
.
By (
1.2.4
) and Lemma
1.3
we are reduced to show the following:

Claim 4.2.1
.

Assume
X
is henselian local of geometric type (i.e.
X=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathcal{O}_{X,x}^{h})
for
X
integral quasi-projective
k
-scheme).
Let
V\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(X\setminus D,Y\times Z)
be a prime correspondence, such that the closure of
\overline{V}\subset X\times Y\times Z
of
V

is finite over
X
, and let
a\in F(Y)
,
b\in G(Z)
. Then the class of
a\otimes b\otimes V
in
H_{r}(X,D)

lies in the image of
H_{l}(X,D)\xrightarrow{\eqref{lem:surjT1}}H_{r}(X,D)
.

Let
V
be as above. Since the closure
\overline{V}\subset X\times Y\times Z
of
V
is integral and finite over
X
, it is local.
Denote by
v\in\overline{V}
the closed point and by
y\in Y
,
z\in Z
the images of
v
, respectively.
We get induced maps
\mathcal{O}_{Y,y}\to\Gamma(\overline{V},\mathcal{O}_{\overline{V}})
and
\mathcal{O}_{Z,z}\to\Gamma(\overline{V},\mathcal{O}_{\overline{V}})
.
Hence

V\subset(X\setminus D)\times U_{1}\times U_{2},

where
j_{1}:U_{1}\hookrightarrow Y
and
j_{2}:U_{2}\hookrightarrow Z
are open affines containing
y
and
z
, respectively.
Denote by
V^{\prime}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(X\setminus D,U_{1}\times U_{2})
the induced prime correspondence.
Then
V=(j_{1}\times j_{2})\circ V^{\prime}
and thus

a\otimes b\otimes V=j_{1}^{*}a\otimes j_{2}^{*}b\otimes V^{\prime}\quad\text{in }H_{r}(X,D).

Hence Claim
4.2.1
follows from the following:

Claim 4.2.2
.

Let
(X,D)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
, let
Y,Z
be smooth quasi-projective
k
-schemes,
let
V\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(X\setminus D,Y\times Z)
be a prime correspondence and
a\in F(Y)
,
b\in G(Z)
.
Then the class of
a\otimes b\otimes V
in
H_{r}(X,D)
lies in the image of

H_{l}(X,D)\xrightarrow{\eqref{lem:surjT1}}H_{r}(X,D)
.

We prove the claim. First we reduce to
k
infinite by a standard trick:
If
k
is finite denote by
k(\ell)
a
\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}
-Galois extension of
k
for a prime
\ell
;
by a trace argument the (diagonal) pullback

H_{r}(X,D)\to H_{r}(X_{k(\ell)},D_{k(\ell)})\times H_{r}(X_{k(\ell^{\prime})},D_{k(\ell^{\prime})})

is injective for
\ell\neq\ell^{\prime}
.

In the following we assume
k
infinite.
By assumption we find proper modulus pairs
\mathcal{Y}=(\overline{Y},Y_{\infty})

and
\mathcal{Z}=(\overline{Z},Z_{\infty})
such that
\overline{Y}
and
\overline{Z}
are projective and

Y=\overline{Y}\setminus|Y_{\infty}|
and
Z=\overline{Z}\setminus|Z_{\infty}|
.
Since
V
is closed in
X\setminus|D|\times\overline{Y}\times\overline{Z}
we find an integer
n_{0}

such that
V\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}((X,n_{0}D),\mathcal{Y}\otimes\mathcal{Z})
.
Choose
n\geq n_{0}
with
(n,p)=1
.
By Lemma
4.1
we find a modulus pair

\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}=(\overline{Y}^{\prime},Y_{\infty}^{\prime})
together with a finite and surjective morphism

\bar{\pi}_{Y,n}:\overline{Y}^{\prime}\to\overline{Y}
such that
\deg\bar{\pi}_{Y,n}
divides a power of
n

and
\bar{\pi}_{Y,n}^{*}(Y_{\infty})=nY^{\prime}_{\infty}
, similarly for
\overline{Z}
.
Denote by
\pi_{Y,n}:Y^{\prime}\to Y
the induced finite and surjective morphism in
\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
and
by
\pi_{Y,n}^{t}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(Y,Y^{\prime})
the correspondence induced by the transpose of the graph.
In
H_{r}(X,D)
we obtain

\displaystyle\deg(\pi_{Y,n})\deg(\pi_{Z,n})\cdot(a\otimes b\otimes V)

\displaystyle=\pi_{Y,n*}\pi_{Y,n}^{*}a\otimes\pi_{Z,n*}\pi_{Z,n}^{*}b\otimes V

\displaystyle=(\pi_{Y,n}^{t})^{*}\pi_{Y,n}^{*}a\otimes(\pi_{Z,n}^{t})^{*}\pi_{Z,n}^{*}b\otimes V

\displaystyle=\pi_{Y,n}^{*}a\otimes\pi_{Z,n}^{*}b\otimes(\pi_{Y,n}^{t}\times\pi_{Z,n}^{t})\circ V.

Observe that the components of
(\pi_{Y,n}^{t}\times\pi_{Z,n}^{t})\circ V\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(X\setminus D,Y^{\prime}\times Z^{\prime})
,
are the irreducible components of

V\times_{Y\times Z}(Y^{\prime}\times Z^{\prime})=({\operatorname{id}}_{X\setminus|D|}\times\pi_{n,Y}\times\pi_{n,Z})^{-1}(V).

Let
W
be such a component, it comes with a finite and surjective map
W\to V
.
Denote by
\overline{V}\subset X\times\overline{Y}\times\overline{Z}
and
\overline{W}\subset X\times\overline{Y}^{\prime}\times\overline{Z}^{\prime}

the closure of
V
and
W
, respectively, and denote by
\tilde{V}\to\overline{V}
and
\tilde{W}\to\overline{W}

the normalizations. Since
\overline{W}
is contained in
\overline{V}\times_{\overline{Y}\times\overline{Z}}(\overline{Y}^{\prime}\times\overline{Z}^{\prime})
, the
natural maps from
\tilde{W}
to
\overline{Y}
and
\overline{Z}
factor via a morphism
\tilde{W}\to\tilde{V}
.
We obtain

nD_{|\tilde{W}}\geq n_{0}D_{|\tilde{W}}\geq Y_{\infty|\tilde{W}}+Z_{\infty|\tilde{W}}=nY^{\prime}_{\infty|\tilde{W}}+nZ^{\prime}_{\infty|\tilde{W}},

where the second inequality follows from
V\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}((X,n_{0}D),\mathcal{Y}\otimes\mathcal{Z})
.
Hence

(\pi_{Y,n}^{t}\times\pi_{Z,n}^{t})\circ V\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}((X,D),\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}\otimes\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}).

It follows that

\delta_{n}\cdot(a\otimes b\otimes V)
lies in the image of

H_{l}(X,D)\to H_{r}(X,D)
, where
\delta_{n}:=\deg(\pi_{Y,n})\deg(\pi_{Z,n})
.
Choose
r\geq n_{0}
with
(r,p)=1=(r,n)
.
Since
\delta_{n}
divides a power of
n
and
\delta_{r}
divides a power
of
r
we find integers
s,t
with

a\otimes b\otimes V=s\delta_{n}\cdot(a\otimes b\otimes V)+t\delta_{r}\cdot(a\otimes b\otimes V).

This proves Claim
4.2.2
and hence also the lemma.
∎

Proposition 4.3
.

For
F_{1},\dots,F_{n}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
, consider the map

(4.3.1)

\underline{\omega}^{*}F_{1}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}\cdots\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}\underline{\omega}^{*}F_{n}\to\underline{\omega}^{*}(F_{1}\otimes_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}\cdots\otimes_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}F_{n})\to\underline{\omega}^{*}(F_{1}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}}\cdots\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}}F_{n}),

where the first map is induced by (
4.2.1
) and the associativity of

\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}
and
\otimes_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}
and the second map is induced by the natural surjective map (cf. (
1.6.2
))

F_{1}\otimes_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}\cdots\otimes_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}F_{n}\to h_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{\mathbf{A}^{1}}(F_{1}\otimes_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}\cdots\otimes_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}F_{n}):=F_{1}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}}\cdots\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}}F_{n},

where we use the notation from
1.4
and
\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}}
denotes the monoidal structure on
\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
defined by Voevodsky.
Then,(
4.3.1
) induces an isomorphism

(4.3.2)

h^{{\overline{\square}},{\rm sp}}_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}F_{1}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}\cdots\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}\underline{\omega}^{*}F_{n})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\underline{\omega}^{*}(F_{1}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}}\cdots\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}}F_{n}).

Proof.

We begin by recalling from
[
MS
, Prop. 3.2]
that for
F,G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
, the formula
F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}}G=\tau_{!}h_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{{\overline{\square}},{\rm sp}}(\tau^{*}F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}\tau^{*}G)
defines a symmetric monoidal structure on
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
.
Next, note that
\underline{\omega}^{*}H\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
for
H\in\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
by

[
KSY
, Lem. 2.3.1]
and
[
KMSY21b
, Prop. 6.2.1b)]
. Moreover

\displaystyle h^{{\overline{\square}},{\rm sp}}_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}F_{1}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}\underline{\omega}^{*}F_{2})

\displaystyle=h^{{\overline{\square}},{\rm sp}}_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}(\tau_{!}\omega^{*}F_{1}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}\tau_{!}\omega^{*}F_{2})

\displaystyle=\tau_{!}h^{{\overline{\square}},{\rm sp}}_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}(\omega^{*}F_{1}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}\omega^{*}F_{2})

\displaystyle=\tau_{!}h^{{\overline{\square}},{\rm sp}}_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}(\tau^{*}(\underline{\omega}^{*}F_{1})\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}\tau^{*}(\underline{\omega}^{*}F_{2}))=\underline{\omega}^{*}F_{1}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}}\underline{\omega}^{*}F_{2},

for every
F_{1},F_{2}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
. Here, the isomorphisms follow from (
1.1.3
) and the exactness of
\tau_{!}
.

We now observe that the functor
\underline{\omega}^{*}
is lax monoidal from
{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}
to
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
(this follows from the fact that
\underline{\omega}^{*}
is right adjoint to
\underline{\omega}_{!}
, which is strict monoidal by construction). By applying
h^{{\overline{\square}},{\rm sp}}_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}
to (
4.3.1
), we obtain the functorial map (
4.3.2
), which we can rewrite for
n=2
as

(4.3.3)

\underline{\omega}^{*}F_{1}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}}\underline{\omega}^{*}F_{2}\to\underline{\omega}^{*}(F_{1}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}}F_{2}).

In particular,
\underline{\omega}^{*}
restricts to a lax symmetric monoidal functor from
\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
to
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
, and the statement of the Proposition is equivalent to the fact
\underline{\omega}^{*}
is in fact (strictly) monoidal, i.e. that the map (
4.3.3
) is an isomorphism (note that the identity for the tensor product is simply the constant sheaf
\mathbb{Z}
, and that
\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbb{Z}=\mathbb{Z}
). Since the tensor products in
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
and
\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
are in particular associative, it is enough to prove the claim when
n=2
.

By Lemma
4.2
, the map (
4.3.3
) (or, equivalently, (
4.3.2
)) is surjective.
On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle\underline{\omega}_{!}h^{{\overline{\square}},{\rm sp}}_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}F_{1}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}\underline{\omega}^{*}F_{2})

\displaystyle=a^{V}_{\operatorname{Nis}}\underline{\omega}_{!}{\underline{h}}^{{\overline{\square}}}_{0}(\underline{\omega}^{*}F_{1}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}\underline{\omega}^{*}F_{2})^{{\rm sp}}

\displaystyle=a^{V}_{\operatorname{Nis}}\underline{\omega}_{!}{\underline{h}}^{{\overline{\square}}}_{0}\tau_{!}(\omega^{*}F_{1}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}\omega^{*}F_{2})

\displaystyle=a^{V}_{\operatorname{Nis}}\underline{\omega}_{!}\tau_{!}h^{{\overline{\square}}}_{0}(\omega^{*}F_{1}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}\omega^{*}F_{2})

\displaystyle=a^{V}_{\operatorname{Nis}}\omega_{!}h^{{\overline{\square}}}_{0}(\omega^{*}F_{1}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}\omega^{*}F_{2}),

where the first equality follows from the definition of
h^{{\overline{\square}},{\rm sp}}_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}
(cf. (
1.4.2
)) and
\underline{\omega}_{!}{\underline{a}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}=a^{V}_{\operatorname{Nis}}\underline{\omega}_{!}
(cf. (
1.2.2
)), and the second holds by the fact
\underline{\omega}_{!}A^{{\rm sp}}=\underline{\omega}_{!}A
for
A\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
and
\underline{\omega}^{*}=\tau_{!}\omega^{*}
(cf. (
1.1.3
)) and the monoidality of
\tau_{!}
, and the third follows from
{\underline{h}}^{{\overline{\square}}}_{0}(\tau_{!}B)=\tau_{!}h^{\square}_{0}(B)
for
B\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
, where
h^{\square}_{0}(B)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
is the maximal cube invariant quotient of
B
defined by the same way as (
1.4.3
), and the last holds by
\underline{\omega}_{!}\tau_{!}=\omega_{!}
(cf. (
1.1.3
)).
Thus
\underline{\omega}_{!}\eqref{prop:T1}
is an isomorphism by
[
RSY22
, Thm 5.3]
in view of
\omega^{*}F=\tilde{F}
(see
[
RSY22
, (3.14.5)]
) by
[
KSY
, Lem. 2.3.1]
.
Since both sides of (
4.3.2
) are semipure the map (
4.3.2
) is injective as well.
∎

4.2.
Definition and basic properties of twists

Definition 4.4

(see
[
MS
, §2]
)
.

Let
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
. We define
\gamma^{n}F
and
F(n)
,
n\geq 0
, recursively by

\gamma^{0}F:=F,\quad\gamma^{1}F:=\gamma F:=\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m},F),\quad\gamma^{n}F:=\gamma(\gamma^{n-1}F)

and

F(0):=F,\quad F(1):=h^{{\overline{\square}},{\rm sp}}_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}(F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m}),\quad F(n):=F(n-1)(1).

Corollary 4.5
.

Let
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
. Then
\gamma^{n}F
,
F(n)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
, for all
n\geq 0
.
Furthermore,

(4.5.1)

\gamma^{n}F=\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}((\overline{\square}^{(1)}_{{\operatorname{red}}})^{\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}n},F)=\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}K^{M}_{n},F),

and

(4.5.2)

F(n)=h^{{\overline{\square}},{\rm sp}}_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}(F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\overline{\square}^{(1)}_{{\operatorname{red}}})^{\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}n})=h^{{\overline{\square}},{\rm sp}}_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}(F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}\underline{\omega}^{*}K^{M}_{n}),

where

(4.5.3)

\overline{\square}^{(1)}_{{\operatorname{red}}}:=\operatorname{Coker}({\operatorname{\mathbb{Z}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}}}(\{1\})\to{\operatorname{\mathbb{Z}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}}}(\mathbf{P}^{1},0+\infty))\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}^{\tau}

and
K^{M}_{n}
is the improved Milnor
K
-theory from
[
Ker10
]
(there denoted by
\hat{K}^{M}_{n}
).

Proof.

For a proper modulus pair
\mathcal{X}
we have
\tau_{!}\tau^{*}{\operatorname{\mathbb{Z}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}}}(\mathcal{X})={\operatorname{\mathbb{Z}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}}}(\mathcal{X})
. It follows that

\overline{\square}^{(1)}_{{\operatorname{red}}}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}^{\tau}
.
By Lemma
2.4
we have
h^{{\overline{\square}},{\rm sp}}_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}(\overline{\square}^{(1)}_{{\operatorname{red}}})=\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m}
.
Thus

(4.5.4)

\gamma F=\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\overline{\square}^{(1)}_{\operatorname{red}},F).

Indeed (we drop the index
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
from
\operatorname{Hom}
and
\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}
)

\displaystyle\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}(\overline{\square}^{(1)}_{\operatorname{red}},F)(\mathcal{X})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Hom}({\operatorname{\mathbb{Z}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}}}(\mathcal{X})\otimes\overline{\square}^{(1)}_{\operatorname{red}},F)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Hom}(\overline{\square}^{(1)}_{\operatorname{red}},\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}({\operatorname{\mathbb{Z}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}}}(\mathcal{X}),F))

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Hom}(h^{{\overline{\square}},{\rm sp}}_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}(\overline{\square}^{(1)}_{\operatorname{red}}),\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}({\operatorname{\mathbb{Z}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}}}(\mathcal{X}),F))

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}(h^{{\overline{\square}},{\rm sp}}_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}(\overline{\square}^{(1)}_{\operatorname{red}}),F)(\mathcal{X})

\displaystyle=\gamma F(\mathcal{X}),

where the third equality holds by Lemma
1.5
(1)
,
(2)
.
This implies the first equality in (
4.5.1
) and also that

\gamma^{n}F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
, for all
n\geq 0
, by Lemma
1.5
(1)
.
For the second equality in (
4.5.1
), first note that it follows from
[
Ker10
]

and results by Voevodsky (see
[
RSY22
, 5.5]
), that we have

(4.5.5)

K^{M}_{n}\cong\mathbf{G}_{m}^{\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}}n}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}.

Hence by Proposition
4.3
and
[
MS
, Lem 1.14(iii)]
, we obtain

(4.5.6)

\underline{\omega}^{*}K^{M}_{n}=h_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{{\overline{\square}},{\rm sp}}((\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}^{m})^{\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}n})=h_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{{\overline{\square}},{\rm sp}}((\overline{\square}^{(1)}_{{\operatorname{red}}})^{\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}n}).

Thus the second equality in (
4.5.1
) follows from the adjunction (
1.4.2
).
The equalities in (
4.5.2
) follow similarly.
∎

Remark 4.6
.

By Corollary
4.5
the twist
\gamma^{n}F
(resp.
F(n)
)
agrees with the definition in
[
MS
, (2.3)]
(resp.
[
MS
, after Prop 3.2]
).

Remark 4.7
.

Let
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
and
\mathcal{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}_{ls}
. By (
4.5.4
) and
[
Sai20a
, Lem 5.9]
we have

\gamma^{1}F(\mathcal{X})=\frac{F((\mathbf{P}^{1},0+\infty)\otimes\mathcal{X})}{F((\mathbf{P}^{1},\infty)\otimes\mathcal{X})}=\frac{F((\mathbf{A}^{1},0)\otimes\mathcal{X})}{F(\mathbf{A}^{1}\otimes\mathcal{X})}.

4.8
.

For later use in section
8
we define a certain maps induced by adjuntion.
Let
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
. For
n\geq 0
we have an adjunction map

(4.8.1)

F\to\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}K^{M}_{n},F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}\underline{\omega}^{*}K^{M}_{n})

which sends
a\in F(\mathcal{X})
to (we drop the subscript
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
)

a\otimes{\operatorname{id}}\in\operatorname{Hom}({\operatorname{\mathbb{Z}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}}}(\mathcal{X})\otimes\underline{\omega}^{*}K^{M}_{n},F\otimes\underline{\omega}^{*}K^{M}_{n}),

where we identify an element
a\in F(\mathcal{X})
with the map
a:{\operatorname{\mathbb{Z}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}}}(\mathcal{X})\to F
.
Composing (
4.8.1
) with the map induced by the natural map

F\otimes\underline{\omega}^{*}K^{M}_{n}\to h_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{{\overline{\square}},{\rm sp}}(F\otimes\underline{\omega}^{*}K^{M}_{n})

Corollary
4.5
yields a map

(4.8.2)

\kappa_{n}:F\to\gamma^{n}(F(n))=:\gamma^{n}F(n)

which by Remark
4.6
coincides with the morphism
[
MS
, (3.5)]
.
Note that
\kappa_{0}
is the identity and that for
m,n\geq 0
the following diagram commutes

(4.8.3)

\textstyle{F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\kappa_{m+n}}
\scriptstyle{\kappa_{m}}
\textstyle{\gamma^{m+n}(F(m+n))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\gamma^{m}(F(m))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma^{m}\kappa_{n}}
\textstyle{\gamma^{m}\gamma^{n}(F(m)(n)).}

5.
Cup product with Chow cycles with support

5.1.
Milnor K-theory and intersection theory with supports

Everything in this subsection is well-known, however we give some explanations for lack of reference.

5.1
.

Recall that a
family of supports
on a scheme
X
is a non-empty collection
\Phi
of closed subsets of
X

which is stable under taking finite unions and closed subsets. The main examples are the family

\Phi_{Z}
, for a closed subset
Z\subset X
, which consists of all the closed subsets in
Z
, the family
\Phi^{\geq c}

of all closed subsets of codimension
\geq c
, and
the family
\Phi^{\rm prop}_{X/S}
, for a morphism
X\to S
, which consists of all closed subsets in
X

which are proper over
S
. If
F
is a sheaf on
X
and
\Phi
is a family of support, then

\Gamma_{\Phi}(X,F)=\{s\in F(X)\mid{\rm supp}(s)\in\Phi\}=\varinjlim_{Z\in\Phi}\Gamma_{Z}(X,F),

and
{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}(F)(U)=\Gamma_{\Phi\cap U}(U,F)
, for an open
U\subset X
.
For a morphism
f:Y\to X
we denote by
f^{-1}\Phi
the smallest family with supports on
Y

containing all closed subsets of the form
f^{-1}(Z)
,
Z\in\Phi
.

Let
X
be
k
-scheme. We denote by
{\operatorname{CH}}_{i}(X)
the Chow group of
i
-dimensional cycles on
X
. If
X
is equidimensional of dimension
d
, we denote by
{\operatorname{CH}}^{i}(X)
the Chow group of
i
-codimensional cycles on
X
, i.e.
{\operatorname{CH}}^{i}(X)={\operatorname{CH}}_{d-i}(X)
. If
\Phi
is a family of supports on
X
, we set

(5.1.1)

{\operatorname{CH}}^{i}_{\Phi}(X)=\varinjlim_{Z\in\Phi}{\operatorname{CH}}_{d-i}(Z),

where the transition maps in the directed limit are given by pushforward along closed immersions.
Note that for a closed subset
Z\subset X
we have

{\operatorname{CH}}^{i}_{Z}(X):={\operatorname{CH}}^{i}_{\Phi_{Z}}(X)={\operatorname{CH}}_{d-i}(Z),

in particular
{\operatorname{CH}}^{i}_{X}(X)={\operatorname{CH}}^{i}(X)
. The notation
{\operatorname{CH}}^{i}_{Z}(X)
is not superfluous since
if
Z
is singular the pullback along the refined Gysin homomorphism as in
[
Ful98
, §6]

relies on the embedding
Z\hookrightarrow X
.

5.2
.

We recall some facts on the relation between Milnor
K
-theory and intersection theory.
Let
K^{M}_{i}
be the improved Milnor
K
-sheaf from
[
Ker10
]
. Its restriction to
\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
is homotopy invariant
and hence for
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
its restriction to
({\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}/X)
is a Nisnevich sheaf denoted by
K^{M}_{i,X}
and
we have

(5.2.1)

R\varepsilon_{*}K^{M}_{i,X}=\varepsilon_{*}K^{M}_{i,X},

where
\varepsilon:X_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}\to X_{{\operatorname{Zar}}}
denotes the canonical morphism of sites, see
[
Voe00b
, Thm 3.1.12]
.

If
Z
is a finite-type
k
-scheme, we denote by
C_{\bullet}(i)(Z)
the degree
i

(homological) Gersten complex of
K^{M}_{*,Z}
(e.g.
[
Ros96
, 5.]
), i.e.,

C_{n}(i)(Z)=\bigoplus_{z\in Z_{(n)}}K^{M}_{n+i}(z),

and the differentials are induced by the tame symbol.
(For the tame symbol we use the sign convention from
[
Ros96
, p. 328]
.)
Recall that the formation
Z\mapsto C_{\bullet}(i)(Z)
is covariant functorial with respect to proper maps and
contravariant functorial with respect to quasi-finite flat maps,
see
[
Ros96
, Prop (4.6)]
.
The assignment
U\mapsto C_{\bullet}(i)(U)
defines a complex of sheaves
on
Z_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
which we denote by
C_{\bullet,Z}(i)
.
If
Z
is equidimensional of dimension
e
, then we define

(5.2.2)

C^{n}_{Z}(i):=C_{e-n,Z}(i-e)

and obtain the cohomological degree
i
Gersten complex
C^{\bullet}_{Z}(i)
the global sections of which we also
denote by
C^{\bullet}(i)(Z)
.

In the following we assume
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
is equidimensional.
By
[
Ker10
, Prop 10(8)]
, the Gersten complex is a resolution on the Nisnevich site for the sheaf
K^{M}_{i,X}
, i.e.

K^{M}_{i,X}\xrightarrow{\simeq}C^{\bullet}_{X}(i)\quad\text{in }D(X_{\operatorname{Nis}}).

Note that
C^{\bullet}_{X}(i)
sits in cohomological degree
[0,i]
.
By (
5.2.1
) and since
\varepsilon_{*}C_{X}^{\bullet}(i)
is a flasque resolution of
\varepsilon_{*}K^{M}_{i,X}
,
we can use
C^{\bullet}_{X}(i)
to compute Nisnevich cohomology with supports of
K^{M}_{i,X}
.
If
\dim X=d
and
\imath:Z\hookrightarrow X
is a closed immersion, then

{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}C^{n}_{X}(i)=\imath_{*}C_{d-n,Z}(i-d).

This gives rise to Bloch’s formula (with support)

(5.2.3)

{\operatorname{CH}}^{i}_{\Phi}(X)=H^{i}_{\Phi,{\operatorname{Zar}}}(X,K^{M}_{i})=H^{i}_{\Phi,{\operatorname{Nis}}}(X,K^{M}_{i})=:H^{i}_{\Phi}(X,K^{M}_{i}),

where
\Phi
is a family of supports on
X
.

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
f:Y\to X
be a morphism between equidimensional smooth schemes and let
\Phi
be a family of
supports on
X
. The following diagram commutes

(5.3.1)

\textstyle{H^{i}_{\Phi}(X,K^{M}_{i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{*}}
(
5.2.3
)
\textstyle{{\operatorname{CH}}^{i}_{\Phi}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{*}}
\textstyle{H^{i}_{f^{-1}\Phi}(Y,K^{M}_{i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(
5.2.3
)
\textstyle{{\operatorname{CH}}^{i}_{f^{-1}\Phi}(Y),}

where the pullback on the right is induced by the refined Gysin homomorphism in
[
Ful98
, 6.6]

(see also
[
CR11
, 1.1.30]
)
and the pullback on the left is induced from the sheaf structure of
K^{M}_{i}
on the category of schemes.

Proof.

In
[
Ros96
, 12]
a morphism of complexes

(5.3.2)

I(f):C^{\bullet}(i)(X)\to C^{\bullet}(i)(Y)

is defined, depending on the choice of a coordination of the tangent bundle
TX
of
X
(see
[
Ros96
, §9]
for the definition of a coordination).
It is compatible with the pullback
f^{*}:K^{M}_{i}(X)\to K^{M}_{i}(Y)
, by
[
Ros96
, (12.3)Prop, (12.4)Cor]
.
Furthermore, if
u:U\to X
is étale, a coordination of
TX
induces by pullback a coordination of

TU
and hence it is direct to check that we have

u_{Y}^{*}\circ I(f)=I(f_{U})\circ u^{*}:C^{\bullet}(i)(X)\to C^{\bullet}(i)(Y_{U}),

where
u_{Y}:Y_{U}\to Y
is the base change of
u
along
f
and
f_{U}:Y_{U}\to U
is the base change of
f
along
u
.
It follows that the choice of a coordination on
TX
allows one to promote (
5.3.2
) to a morphism of complexes of sheaves on
X_{\operatorname{Nis}}

(5.3.3)

\mathcal{I}(f):C^{\bullet}(i)_{X}\to f_{*}C^{\bullet}(i)_{Y}

which is compatible with the pullback
f^{*}:K^{M}_{i,X}\to f_{*}K^{M}_{i,Y}
. In view of
5.2
, taking sections with support
\Gamma_{\Phi}(X,-)
and then cohomology, gives a map

H^{i}(\Gamma_{\Phi}(X,\mathcal{I}(f)))\colon H^{i}_{\Phi}(X,K^{M}_{i})\to H^{i}_{f^{-1}\Phi}(Y,K^{M}_{i})

that we identify with the left vertical map in (
5.3.1
).
Consider the following diagram of solid arrows

(5.3.4)

\textstyle{C^{i}(i)(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{I(f)}
\textstyle{C_{d-i}(i-d)(Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{I_{Z}(f)}
\textstyle{{\operatorname{CH}}_{d-i}(Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{!}}
\textstyle{C^{i}(i)(Y)}
\textstyle{C_{e-i}(i-e)(f^{-1}(Z))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\operatorname{CH}}_{e-i}(f^{-1}(Z)),}

where
Z\in\Phi
,
d=\dim X
,
e=\dim Y
, and
f^{!}
is the refined Gysin map from
[
Ful98
, 6.6]
.
It remains to show that there exists a dotted arrow
I_{Z}(f)
making the diagram commute.
Since the pushforward on
C^{\bullet}(i)
is compatible with the one on Chow groups, we can assume
that
Z
is integral with
\dim Z=d-i
, i.e.,
C_{d-i}(i-d)(Z)=\mathbb{Z}\cdot[Z]
.
By definition of
I(f)
and
f^{!}
it suffices to consider the case where
f=i:Y\hookrightarrow X
is a regular closed immersion
defined by a coherent ideal sheaf
J
. Denote by
N_{Y/X}=\operatorname{Spec}(\oplus_{n\geq 0}J^{n}/J^{n+1})
the normal bundle
over
Y
and fix a coordination
\tau
of
N_{Y/X}
in the sense of
[
Ros96
, 9., p.371]
.
Set
Z^{\prime}:=Z\times_{X}Y
and
N:=N_{Y/X}\times_{Y}Z^{\prime}
; the pullback of
\tau
along
Z^{\prime}\hookrightarrow Y
induces
a coordination
\tau^{\prime}
of
N
.
Denote by
C_{Z^{\prime}/Z}
the normal cone of
Z^{\prime}\hookrightarrow Z
and by
\nu:C_{Z^{\prime}/Z}\hookrightarrow N
the closed immersion induced by

J\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}_{Z}\rightarrow\!\!\!\!\!\rightarrow J\mathcal{O}_{Z}
.
Note that
C_{Z^{\prime}/Z}
has pure dimension
d-i
, see
[
Ful98
, B 6.6]
, and thus

C_{d-i}(i-d)(C_{Z^{\prime}/Z})=\bigoplus_{z\in(C_{Z^{\prime}/Z})^{(0)}}\mathbb{Z}.

With the notation from
[
Ros96
, 9., 11.]
we define
I_{Z}(f)
to be the composition

I_{Z}(f):C_{d-i}(i-d)(Z)\xrightarrow{J(Z,Z^{\prime})}C_{d-i}(i-d)(C_{Z^{\prime}/Z})\\
\xrightarrow{\nu_{*}}C_{d-i}(i-d)(N)\xrightarrow{r(\tau^{\prime})}C_{e-i}(i-e)(Z^{\prime}).

Let
D(Z,Z^{\prime})\to\mathbf{A}^{1}=\operatorname{Spec}k[t]
be the deformation scheme from
[
Ros96
, 10.]
, so that

D(Z,Z^{\prime})_{|\mathbf{A}^{1}\setminus\{0\}}=Z\times(\mathbf{A}^{1}\setminus\{0\})
and
D(Z,Z^{\prime})_{|0}=C_{Z^{\prime}/Z}
.
Then by definition (see
[
Ros96
, 11.]
)

J(Z,Z^{\prime})([Z])=\operatorname{div}_{D(Z,Z^{\prime})}(t)_{|C_{Z^{\prime}/Z}}=[C_{Z^{\prime}/Z}],

where
[C_{Z^{\prime}/Z}]
denotes the cycle associated to the scheme
C_{Z^{\prime}/Z}
,
[
Ful98
, 1.5]
.
Thus the map
J(Z^{\prime},Z)
corresponds to the specialization map

\sigma:{\operatorname{CH}}_{d-i}(Z)\to{\operatorname{CH}}_{d-i}(C_{Z^{\prime}/Z})
from
[
Ful98
, 5.2]
. Therefore, the above definition of
I_{Z}(f)
makes
the square on the right in (
5.3.4
) commutative, by the alternative description of
i^{!}
on the Chow side
in
[
Ful98
, 6.2, 2nd paragraph on p. 98]
. We subdivide the left square as follows:

(5.3.5)

\textstyle{C_{d-i}(i-d)(Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\nu_{*}\circ J(Z,Z^{\prime})}
\textstyle{C_{d-i}(i-d)(N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{r(\tau^{\prime})}
\textstyle{C_{e-i}(i-e)(Z^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C^{i}(i)(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{J(X,Y)}
\scriptstyle{I(f)}
\textstyle{C^{i}(i)(N_{Y/X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{r(\tau)}
\textstyle{C^{i}(i)(Y),}

the vertical maps are all induced by pushforward along the respective closed immersion.
It follows directly from the definition of the maps
r(\tau)
and
r(\tau^{\prime})
in
[
Ros96
, (9.1)-(9.4)]
, that
the right square of (
5.3.5
) commutes. For the left square note that

D(Z,Z^{\prime})
is an integral and closed subscheme of
D(X,Y)
, hence it is the closure of

Z\times(\mathbf{A}^{1}\setminus\{0\})
in
D(X,Y)
; furthermore
D(Z,Z^{\prime})\cap N_{Y/X}=D(Z,Z^{\prime})\cap N=C_{Z^{\prime}/Z}
.
Thus by definition

J(X,Y)([Z])=[C_{Z^{\prime}/Z}]\quad\text{in }C^{i}(i)(N_{Y/X}).

This yields the commutativity of the left square in (
5.3.5
).
∎

5.4
.

In
[
Ros96
, 14.1]
a cross product

C^{p}(i)(X)\times C^{q}(j)(Y)\to C^{p+q}(i+j)(X\times Y),\quad(a,b)\mapsto a\times b

is defined by sending
(a_{x},b_{y})
, where
a_{x}\in K^{M}_{i-p}(x)
,
x\in X^{(p)}
, and
b_{y}\in K^{M}_{j-q}(y)
,

y\in Y^{(q)}
, to

(5.4.1)

\oplus_{z\in(x\times y)^{(0)}}l_{z}\,a_{x|z}\cdot b_{y|z},

where
x\times y
denotes the fiber product of
k
-schemes,

l_{z}
denotes the length of the local ring of
x\times y
at
z
and

a_{x|z}\in K^{M}_{i-p}(z)
denotes the pullback of
a_{x}
to
z
and similar with
b_{y|z}
.
Note that
z\in(x\times y)^{(0)}
implies
z\in(X\times Y)^{(p+q)}
.
By
[
Ros96
, (14.4)]
we have

(5.4.2)

d(a\times b)=(da)\times b+(-1)^{i-p}(a\times db),

where
d
denotes the differential of the Gersten complex.
(There seems to be a typo in the formula in
loc. cit.
:
the
(-1)^{n}
in that formula should be a
(-1)^{n+p}
as follows from what is said in the proof and

[
Ros96
, R3f, R3d]
; this formula is for the homological notation, if one translates to
the cohomological notation via (
5.2.2
) one obtains (
5.4.2
).)
We have to modify the cross product to obtain a morphism of complexes (with the usual sign convention for
a tensor product of complexes). For
a\in C^{p}(i)(X)
and
b\in C^{q}(j)(Y)
we set

(5.4.3)

a\boxtimes b:=(-1)^{i(q+j)}a\times b.

Then we obtain

d(a\boxtimes b)=(da)\boxtimes b+(-1)^{p}\,a\boxtimes db.

Thus
\boxtimes
induces a morphism of complexes

(5.4.4)

\boxtimes:{\rm tot}(C^{\bullet}(i)(X)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}C^{\bullet}(j)(Y))\to C^{\bullet}(i+j)(X\times Y),

which via the augmentation from Milnor
K
-theory is compatible with

(5.4.5)

K^{M}_{i}(X)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}K^{M}_{j}(Y)\to K^{M}_{i+j}(X\times Y),\quad a\otimes b\mapsto\pi_{X}^{*}a\cdot\pi_{Y}^{*}b,

with
\pi_{X}:X\times Y\to X
the projection.
In degree
i+j
the map (
5.4.4
) is given by

(\bigoplus_{x\in X^{(i)}}\mathbb{Z}\cdot x)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}(\bigoplus_{y\in Y^{(j)}}\mathbb{Z}\cdot y)\to\bigoplus_{z\in X\times Y^{(i+j)}}\mathbb{Z}\cdot z,

x\otimes y\mapsto\bigoplus_{z\in(x\times y)^{(0)}}(-1)^{i(j+j)}l_{z}\cdot z=\bigoplus_{z\in(x\times y)^{(0)}}l_{z}\cdot z.

Hence for families of support
\Phi
on
X
and
\Psi
on
Y
the following diagram commutes

(5.4.6)

\textstyle{{\operatorname{CH}}^{i}_{\Phi}(X)\times{\operatorname{CH}}^{j}_{\Psi}(Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\boxtimes}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{{\operatorname{CH}}^{i+j}_{\Phi\times\Psi}(X\times Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{H^{i}_{\Phi}(X,K^{M}_{i})\times H^{j}_{\Psi}(Y,K^{M}_{j})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\boxtimes}
\textstyle{H^{i+j}_{\Phi\times\Psi}(X\times Y,K^{M}_{i+j}),}

where the upper horizontal map is the exterior product of cycles, see
[
Ful98
, 1.10]
and

\Phi\times\Psi
denotes the smallest family of supports containing
Z_{1}\times Z_{2}
,
for all
Z_{1}\in\Phi
and
Z_{2}\in\Psi
.
We note that if
\tau:X\times Y\to Y\times X
is the switching morphism, then

(5.4.7)

\tau_{*}(a\boxtimes b)=(-1)^{ij+pq}(b\boxtimes a),\quad a\in C^{p}(i)(X),b\in C^{q}(j)(Y),

as follows directly from (
5.4.1
) and (
5.4.3
).
The above and Lemma
5.3
implies that the intersection product with support

(5.4.8)

\Delta^{*}\circ\boxtimes:{\operatorname{CH}}^{i}_{\Phi}(X)\times{\operatorname{CH}}^{j}_{\Psi}(X)\to{\operatorname{CH}}^{i+j}_{\Phi\cap\Psi}(X),

from
[
Ful98
, 8]
corresponds via Bloch’s formula to

(5.4.9)

\Delta^{*}\circ\boxtimes:H^{i}_{\Phi}(X,K^{M}_{i})\times H^{j}_{\Psi}(X,K^{M}_{j})\to H^{i+j}_{\Phi\cap\Psi}(X,K^{M}_{i+j}),

where
\Phi\cap\Psi=\{Z_{1}\cap Z_{2}\mid Z_{1}\in\Phi,Z_{2}\in\Psi\}
.

5.2.
Cupping

5.5
.

Let
F,G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}NST}}
and let
X
be a
k
-scheme and
D
and
E
effective Cartier divisors on it, such that
(X,D)
,
(X,E)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
.
We recall that there is a natural morphism
of Nisnevich sheaves on
X

(5.5.1)

F_{(X,D)}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}G_{(X,E)}\to(F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}NST}}}G)_{(X,D+E)}

which is defined as follows:
For
U\to X
we have a surjection (see the proof of Lemma
4.2
)

\pi:\bigoplus_{\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{Z}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}}F(\mathcal{Y})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}G(\mathcal{Z})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}((U,(D+E)_{U}),\mathcal{Y}\otimes\mathcal{Z})\\
\rightarrow\!\!\!\!\!\rightarrow(F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}G)(U,(D+E)_{U}).

Composition with
\pi
gives then a morphism

(5.5.2)

F(U,D_{U})\otimes G(U,E_{U})\to(F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}G)(U,(D+E)_{U}),

a\otimes b\mapsto\pi(a\otimes b\otimes\Delta_{U}),

where
\Delta_{U}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}((U,(D+E)_{U}),(U,D_{U})\otimes(U,E_{U}))
is the diagonal (note that it is indeed an admissible correspondence). If we now compose (
5.5.2
)
with the value on
(U,(D+E)_{U})
of the natural map (the sheafification)

(F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}G)\to{\underline{a}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}(F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}G)=F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}NST}}}G

we get the desired map (
5.5.1
).

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
and
X,D,E
as in
5.5
. Assume
X
is connected.
Consider the map

(5.6.1)

(\gamma^{1}F(X,D))\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbf{G}_{m}(X\setminus E)\to F(X,D+E)

defined as composition

\displaystyle(\gamma^{1}F(X,D))\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbf{G}_{m}(X\setminus E)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\eqref{para:Tmap1}}(\gamma^{1}F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}NST}}}\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m})(X,D+E)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\rm adj.}F(X,D+E),

where the morphism ’adj.’ is induced by the counit of the adjunction

(-)\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m}\dashv{\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m},-)
.
Then the precomposition of (
5.6.1
)
with the natural map

F(\overline{\square}^{(1)}\otimes(X,D))\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{\mathbb{Z}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}}}(\overline{\square}^{(1)})(X,E)\to\gamma^{1}F(X,D)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbf{G}_{m}(X\setminus E),

stemming from (
4.5.4
) and Lemma
2.4
, is given by

F(\overline{\square}^{(1)}\otimes(X,D))\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{\mathbb{Z}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}}}(\overline{\square}^{(1)})(X,E)\to F(X,D+E),

a\otimes f\mapsto\Delta_{X}^{*}((f-\deg(f)\cdot s_{1})\otimes{\operatorname{id}}_{(X,D)})^{*}a,

where
s_{1}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}((X,E),{\overline{\square}}^{(1)})

is the graph of
X\to\operatorname{Spec}k=\{1\}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{P}^{1}
and

\Delta_{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}((X,D+E),(X,E)\otimes(X,D))
is the graph of the diagonal.

Proof.

Note that under the identification
{\operatorname{\mathbb{Z}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)})={\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{\rm red}\oplus\mathbb{Z}
,
see (
4.5.3
), the projection to the first factor is given by

{\operatorname{\mathbb{Z}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)})(X,E)\ni f\mapsto(f-\deg(f)\cdot s_{1})\in{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{{\operatorname{red}}}(X,E).

Since by Lemma
2.4
we have

h_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{{\overline{\square}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{{\operatorname{red}}})=\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m}
, the statement of the lemma
is direct from the explicit description of (
5.5.1
) in
5.5
.
∎

Lemma 5.7
.

Let
X
be a scheme and
Z\subset X
a closed subset
X
.
Let
A,B\in D(X_{{\operatorname{Nis}}})
and assume that the cohomology sheaves
H^{i}(B)
have support in
Z
, for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
.
Then the natural map

R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}(A\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}B)\xrightarrow{\simeq}A\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}B

is an isomorphism. In particular, for any
C\in D(X_{{\operatorname{Nis}}})
we obtain the canonical morphism

(5.7.1)

A\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}C\cong R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}(A\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}C)\to R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}(A\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}C).

Proof.

Denote by
j:U=X\setminus Z\hookrightarrow X
the open immersion.
By assumption
H^{i}(B_{|U})=H^{i}(B)_{|U}=0
, i.e.,
B_{|U}=0
in
D(X_{{\operatorname{Nis}}})
.
Therefore the statement follows from the distinguished triangle (see
[
Sta19
,
Tag 09XP
]
)

R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}(A\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}B)\to A\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}B\to Rj_{*}(A\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}B)_{|U}\xrightarrow{+1}

and the isomorphism
(A\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}B)_{|U}=A_{|U}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}B_{|U}
.
∎

5.8
.

Let
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
. Let
\mathcal{X}=(X,D)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
with
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
, let
\Phi
be a family of supports on
X
,
and
\alpha\in{\operatorname{CH}}^{i}_{\Phi}(X)
, see
5.1
.
We define the morphism

(5.8.1)

c_{\alpha}:(\gamma^{i}F)_{\mathcal{X}}[-i]\to R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}F_{\mathcal{X}}\quad\text{in }D(X_{{\operatorname{Nis}}})

as follows:
choose a representative
\tilde{\alpha}\in{\operatorname{CH}}^{i}_{Z}(X)
,
Z\in\Phi
, of
\alpha
;
by the identification

{\operatorname{CH}}^{i}_{Z}(X)=H^{i}_{Z}(X,K^{M}_{i})=\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{X_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{X},R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}K^{M}_{i,X})

the cycle

\tilde{\alpha}
induces a morphism in
D(X_{{\operatorname{Nis}}})
(again denoted by
\tilde{\alpha}
)

\tilde{\alpha}:\mathbb{Z}_{X}[-i]\to R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}K^{M}_{i,X}=R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}(\underline{\omega}^{*}K^{M}_{i})_{(X,\emptyset)}.

We define
c_{\alpha}
as the composition in
D(X_{\operatorname{Nis}})

\displaystyle(\gamma^{i}F)_{\mathcal{X}}[-i]

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{{\operatorname{id}}\otimes\tilde{\alpha}}(\gamma^{i}F)_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}K^{M}_{i,X}
