\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\eqref{lem:ten-supp1}}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}((\gamma^{i}F)_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}K^{M}_{i,X})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\rm els}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}((\gamma^{i}F)_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}K^{M}_{i,X})

\displaystyle\to R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}((\gamma^{i}F)_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}K^{M}_{i})_{(X,\emptyset)})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\eqref{para:Tmap1}}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}(\gamma^{i}F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}NST}}}\underline{\omega}^{*}K^{M}_{i})_{\mathcal{X}}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\rm adj}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}F_{\mathcal{X}},

where the map
els
is the enlarge-support-map,
the fourth map is induced by the quotient map
A\otimes^{L}B\to H_{0}(A\otimes^{L}B)=A\otimes B
,
and
adj
is induced by adjunction via Corollary
4.5
.
It is direct to check that the definition of
c_{\alpha}
does not depend on the choice of
\tilde{\alpha}
.

The morphism
c_{\alpha}
satisfies the following functorial properties.

Lemma 5.9
.

Let
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
. Let
\mathcal{X}=(X,D)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
with
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
, let
\Phi
be a family of supports on
X
.

(1)

We have
c_{\alpha+\beta}=c_{\alpha}+c_{\beta}
, for
\alpha,\beta\in{\operatorname{CH}}^{i}_{\Phi}(X)
.

(2)

Let
\Psi
be another family of supports containing
\Phi
. Denote
by the same letter
\imath
the natural maps
{\operatorname{CH}}_{\Phi}\to{\operatorname{CH}}_{\Psi}
and
R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}\to R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Psi}
.
Then
\imath c_{\alpha}=c_{\imath\alpha}
, for any
\alpha\in{\operatorname{CH}}^{i}_{\Phi}(X)
.

(3)

Let
\mathcal{Y}=(Y,E)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
. Let
f:Y\to X
be a morphism in
\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
, such that

E\geq f^{*}D
, and let
\alpha\in{\operatorname{CH}}^{i}_{\Phi}(X)
. Consider the pullback cycle
f^{*}\alpha\in{\operatorname{CH}}^{i}_{f^{-1}\Phi}(Y)

(see Lemma
5.3
). The following diagram commutes

\textstyle{(\gamma^{i}F)_{\mathcal{X}}[-i]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c_{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{f^{*}}
\textstyle{R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}F_{\mathcal{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{*}}
\textstyle{Rf_{*}(\gamma^{i}F)_{\mathcal{Y}}[-i]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c_{f^{*}\alpha}}
\textstyle{Rf_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{f^{-1}\Phi}F_{\mathcal{Y}}=R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}Rf_{*}F_{\mathcal{Y}}.}

(4)

For
\alpha\in{\operatorname{CH}}^{i}_{\Phi}(X)
,
\beta\in{\operatorname{CH}}^{j}_{\Psi}(X)

denote by
\alpha\cdot\beta\in{\operatorname{CH}}^{i+j}_{\Phi\cap\Psi}(X)
the intersection product of
\alpha
and
\beta
,
see (
5.4.8
).
The following diagram commutes

\textstyle{\gamma^{i}(\gamma^{j}F)_{\mathcal{X}}[-i][-j]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
Cor
4.5
\scriptstyle{c_{\alpha}[-j]}
\textstyle{R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}(\gamma^{j}F)_{\mathcal{X}}[-j]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c_{\beta}}
\textstyle{(\gamma^{i+j}F)_{\mathcal{X}}[-(i+j)]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c_{\alpha\cdot\beta}}
\textstyle{R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi\cap\Psi}F_{\mathcal{X}}.}

Proof.

(1)
and
(2)
are immediate to check.
For
(3)
we may assume
\Phi=\Phi_{Z}
, for some closed subset
Z\in X
.
It suffices to show the commutativity of the adjoint square,
which we can decompose into the following two diagrams (we write
G:=\gamma^{i}F
)

\textstyle{f^{-1}G_{\mathcal{X}}[-i]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{id}}\otimes\alpha}
\textstyle{1}
\textstyle{f^{-1}G_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes^{L}f^{-1}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}K^{M}_{i,X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{2}
\textstyle{f^{-1}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}(G_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes K^{M}_{i,X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G_{\mathcal{Y}}[-i]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{id}}\otimes f^{*}\alpha}
\textstyle{G_{\mathcal{Y}}\otimes^{L}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{f^{-1}Z}K^{M}_{i,Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{f^{-1}Z}(G_{\mathcal{Y}}\otimes K^{M}_{i,Y}),}

and

\textstyle{f^{-1}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}(G_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes K^{M}_{i,X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(
5.5.1
)
\textstyle{3}
\textstyle{f^{-1}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}(G\otimes\underline{\omega}^{*}K^{M}_{i})_{\mathcal{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rm adj.}
\textstyle{4}
\textstyle{f^{-1}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}F_{\mathcal{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{f^{-1}Z}(G_{\mathcal{Y}}\otimes K^{M}_{i,Y})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(
5.5.1
)
\textstyle{R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{f^{-1}Z}(G\otimes\underline{\omega}^{*}K^{M}_{i})_{\mathcal{Y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rm adj.}
\textstyle{R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{f^{-1}Z}G_{\mathcal{Y}},}

where the vertical maps are induced by pullback along
f:\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{X}
and for the first diagram
we use the canonical identification
f^{-1}A\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}f^{-1}B=f^{-1}(A\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}B)
.
The identity
Rf_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{f^{-1}Z}=R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}Rf_{*}
and the natural map

{\operatorname{id}}\to Rf_{*}f^{-1}
yield by adjunction a natural transformation

f^{-1}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}\to R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{f^{-1}Z}f^{-1}
; using this the commutativity of the square 2
is direct to check; furthermore
the proof of the commutativity of the squares 3 and 4 reduces to the case without support
(i.e.,
Z=X
), which is immediate to check.
The commutativity of square 1 follows from Lemma
5.3
.
For
(4)
we may assume
\Phi=\Phi_{Z}
and
\Psi=\Phi_{Z^{\prime}}
. Consider the following diagram

\textstyle{(\gamma^{i}\gamma^{j}F\otimes^{L}K_{i,Z})[-j]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{id}}\otimes\beta}
\textstyle{(\gamma^{i}\gamma^{j}F\otimes K_{i})[-j]_{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{i}}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{id}}\otimes\beta}
\textstyle{\gamma^{j}F[-j]_{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{id}}\otimes\beta}
\textstyle{\gamma^{i}\gamma^{j}F\otimes^{L}K_{i,Z}\otimes^{L}K_{j,Z^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{((\gamma^{i}\gamma^{j}F\otimes K_{i})\otimes^{L}K_{j,Z^{\prime}})_{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{i}}
\textstyle{(\gamma^{j}F\otimes^{L}K_{j,Z^{\prime}})_{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\gamma^{i}\gamma^{j}F\otimes^{L}(K_{i}\otimes K_{j})_{Z\cap Z^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu}
\textstyle{(\gamma^{i}\gamma^{j}F\otimes K_{i}\otimes K_{j})_{Z\cap Z^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\mu}
\textstyle{(\gamma^{j}F\otimes K_{j})_{Z\cap Z^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{j}}
\textstyle{\gamma^{i+j}F\otimes^{L}K_{i+j,Z\cap Z^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\gamma^{i+j}F\otimes K_{i+j})_{Z\cap Z^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{i+j}}
\textstyle{F_{Z\cap Z^{\prime}},}

in which we skip the indices
\mathcal{X}
and
X
, we write
K
instead of
K^{M}
,
we write
C_{Z}:=R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}C
, for
C\in D(X_{\operatorname{Nis}})
, the tensor products are over
\mathbb{Z}
, the map

\mu
is induced by multiplication
K^{M}_{i}\otimes K^{M}_{j}\to K^{M}_{i+j}
and Corollary
4.5
,
the maps
\varphi_{i}
are induced by the composition

(5.9.1)

(\gamma^{i}G)_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}K^{M}_{i,X}\xrightarrow{\eqref{para:Tmap1}}(\gamma^{i}G\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}NST}}}\underline{\omega}^{*}K^{M}_{i})_{\mathcal{X}}\xrightarrow{\rm adj}G,

for
G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
, and the unlabeled arrows are induced maps of the form

G\otimes^{L}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}H\xrightarrow{\eqref{lem:ten-supp1}}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}(G\otimes^{L}H)\to R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}(G\otimes H),

for sheaves
G,H
.
It is direct to check that this diagram commutes.
Thus it remains to show that the composition in
D(X_{\operatorname{Nis}})

(5.9.2)

\mathbb{Z}_{X}[-j-i]\xrightarrow{\alpha[-j]}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}K^{M}_{i,X}[-j]\xrightarrow{{\operatorname{id}}\otimes\beta}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}K^{M}_{i,X}\otimes^{L}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z^{\prime}}K^{M}_{i,X}\\
\to R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z\cap Z^{\prime}}(K^{M}_{i,X}\otimes K^{M}_{i,X})\to R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z\cap Z^{\prime}}K^{M}_{i+j,X}

is equal to the morphism induced by the intersection product
\alpha\cdot\beta\in{\operatorname{CH}}^{i+j}_{Z\cap Z^{\prime}}(X)
,
see (
5.4.8
).
Denote by
p_{i}:X\times X\to X
the projection to the
i
th factor and by
\Delta:X\hookrightarrow X\times X

the diagonal. Since
\Delta^{-1}p_{i}^{-1}={\operatorname{id}}_{X}
we obtain that the above composition is adjoint to

\displaystyle p_{1}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}_{X}[-i]\otimes^{L}p_{2}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}_{X}[-j]

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\alpha\otimes\beta}p_{1}^{-1}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}K^{M}_{i,X}\otimes^{L}p_{2}^{-1}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z^{\prime}}K^{M}_{j,X}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{p_{1}^{*}\otimes p_{2}^{*}}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z\times X}K^{M}_{i,X\times X}\otimes^{L}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{X\times Z^{\prime}}K^{M}_{j,X\times X}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\rm mult}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z\times Z^{\prime}}K_{i+j,X\times X}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\Delta^{*}}\Delta_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z\cap Z^{\prime}}K^{M}_{i+j,X}.

By the compatibility of (
5.4.4
) and (
5.4.5
) this composition maps in
D(X_{\operatorname{Nis}})

to the composition of complexes

p_{1}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}_{X}[-i]\otimes p_{2}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}_{X}[-j]\xrightarrow{\alpha\otimes\beta}p_{1}^{-1}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}C^{\bullet}_{X}(i)\otimes p_{2}^{-1}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z^{\prime}}C^{\bullet}_{Y}(j)\\
\xrightarrow{\boxtimes}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z\times Z^{\prime}}C^{\bullet}_{X\times X}(i+j)\xrightarrow{I(\Delta)}\Delta_{*}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z\cap Z^{\prime}}C^{\bullet}_{X}(i+j),

where
I(\Delta)
is a morphism as in (
5.3.3
) (and it is compatible with

\Delta^{*}:K^{M}_{X\times X,i+j}\to\Delta_{*}K^{M}_{X,i+j}
, see after (
5.3.3
)).
Thus it follows from the compatibility of (
5.4.8
) and (
5.4.9
)
that (
5.9.2
) is induced by the intersection product
\alpha\cdot\beta
.
∎

Lemma 5.10
.

Let
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
and
\mathcal{X}=(X,D)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
with
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
.
Let
E
be an effective Cartier divisor on
X
which we view as an element in
{\operatorname{CH}}^{1}_{|E|}(X)
.
Denote by
j:U=X\setminus|E|\hookrightarrow X
the open immersion and set
\mathcal{U}=(U,D_{|U})
.
Then the composition

H^{1}(c_{E}):(\gamma^{1}F)_{\mathcal{X}}\to R^{1}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{|E|}F_{\mathcal{X}}\cong j_{*}F_{\mathcal{U}}/F_{\mathcal{X}},

factors via the natural map (which is injective by the semipurity of
F
)

(5.10.1)

F_{(X,D+E)}/F_{\mathcal{X}}\hookrightarrow j_{*}F_{\mathcal{U}}/F_{\mathcal{X}}.

If
\nu:V\hookrightarrow X
is an open neighborhood of
|E|
, such that
E_{|V}=\operatorname{Div}(e)

is principal with
e\in\Gamma(V,\mathcal{O}_{V})
, then the induced map

(5.10.2)

(\gamma^{1}F)_{\mathcal{X}}\to F_{(X,D+E)}/F_{\mathcal{X}}\cong F_{(V,(D+E)_{|V})}/F_{(V,D_{|V})}

sends an element
a\in(\gamma^{1}F)(\mathcal{X})
, represented by
an element in
F(\overline{\square}^{(1)}\otimes\mathcal{X})
which restricts to
\tilde{a}\in F((\mathbf{A}^{1},0)\otimes\mathcal{X})
to the class
modulo
F(V,D_{|V})
of

(5.10.3)

\Delta_{V}^{*}(\Gamma_{e}\times\nu)^{*}\tilde{a}\in F(V,(D+E)_{|V}),

where
\Gamma_{e}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}((V,E_{|V}),(\mathbf{A}^{1},0))

is the graph of the morphism
e\in\mathbf{A}^{1}(V)
and
\Delta_{V}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}((V,(D+E)_{|V}),(V,E_{|V})\otimes(V,D_{|V}))

is induced by the diagonal.

Proof.

Set
G^{1}=\bigoplus_{x\in|E|^{(0)}}i_{x*}\mathbb{Z}
, where
i_{x}:\overline{x}\hookrightarrow X
is the closed immersion.
The complex
G:=[j_{*}\mathbf{G}_{m,U}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{Div}}G^{1}]

is a
{\underline{\Gamma}}_{|E|}
-acyclic resolution of
\mathbf{G}_{m,X}
on
X_{\operatorname{Nis}}
and hence

R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{|E|}\mathbf{G}_{m,X}=G^{1}[-1].

The map
\mathbb{Z}_{X}[-1]\to R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{|E|}\mathbf{G}_{m,X}
corresponding to
E\in{\operatorname{CH}}^{1}_{|E|}(X)
(see
5.8
)
is thus induced by the map of complexes
\varphi_{E}:\mathbb{Z}_{X}[-1]\to G^{1}[-1]
which sends
1\in\mathbb{Z}_{X}
to the
Weil divisor
[E]
. Set
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}:=(X,D+E)
. We obtain a commutative diagram

\textstyle{(\gamma^{1}F)_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbf{G}_{m,X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(
5.6.1
)
\textstyle{F_{\mathcal{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\gamma^{1}F)_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}j_{*}\mathbf{G}_{m,U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(
5.6.1
)
\textstyle{F_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{j_{*}F_{\mathcal{U}}.}

where the last horizontal arrow is injective by semipurity. This yields a map on the cokernels (taken vertically)

(\gamma^{1}F)_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}G^{1}\xrightarrow{\theta^{1}}F_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}/F_{\mathcal{X}}\hookrightarrow j_{*}F_{\mathcal{U}}/F_{\mathcal{X}}.

where the last morphism is injective again by semipurity. Set
C:=[F_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}\to F_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}/F_{\mathcal{X}}]
, then we obtain
a morphism of complexes
\theta
which fits in the commutative diagram of complexes

(5.10.4)

\textstyle{((\gamma^{1}F)_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbf{G}_{m,X})[0]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(
5.6.1
)
\textstyle{F_{\mathcal{X}}[0]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rm qis}
\textstyle{(\gamma^{1}F)_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta}
\textstyle{C.}

Consider the following diagram in
D(X_{\operatorname{Nis}})

\textstyle{(\gamma^{1}F)_{\mathcal{X}}[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c_{E}}
\textstyle{(\gamma^{1}F)_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{L}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{|E|}\mathbf{G}_{m,X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(*)}
\textstyle{R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{|E|}F_{\mathcal{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{|E|}((\gamma^{1}F)_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta}
\textstyle{R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{|E|}C}
\textstyle{(\gamma^{1}F)_{\mathcal{X}}[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{id}}\otimes\varphi_{E}}
\textstyle{(\gamma^{1}F)_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}G^{1}[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta^{1}}
\textstyle{F_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}/F_{\mathcal{X}}[-1],\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

where the map
(*)
is the composition

(\gamma^{1}F)_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{L}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{|E|}\mathbf{G}_{m,X}\xrightarrow{\eqref{lem:ten-supp1}}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{|E|}((\gamma^{1}F)_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{L}\mathbf{G}_{m,X})\\
\xrightarrow{\simeq}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{|E|}((\gamma^{1}F)_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}^{L}G)\xrightarrow{\rm nat.}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{|E|}((\gamma^{1}F)_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}G)

and, using that
G^{1}
and
F_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}/F_{\mathcal{X}}
have support in
|E|
, the lower vertical maps
are induced by the natural map
{\underline{\Gamma}}_{|E|}\to R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{|E|}
.
The upper half of the diagram commutes by the definition of
c_{E}
(see
5.8
)
and the commutativity of (
5.10.4
); the lower half of the diagram
commutes by the definition of the involved maps.
Thus
H^{1}(c_{E})
factors via (
5.10.1
).
If
V
is as in (
5.10.2
), then we can lift
[E]\in G^{1}(V)
to
e\in\Gamma(V,j_{*}\mathbf{G}_{m})
.
Therefore, formula (
5.10.3
) follows directly from the definition of
\theta^{1}
and
Lemma
5.6
, where we have to use the fact that the graph of the composition

s_{1}:V\to\operatorname{Spec}k=\{1\}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{A}^{1}
defines an element in
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}((V,\emptyset),(\mathbf{A}^{1},0))
and hence

\Delta_{V}^{*}(\Gamma_{s_{1}}\times\nu)^{*}\tilde{a}
vanishes modulo
F(V,D_{|V})
.
∎

Remark 5.11
.

Let
C_{*}(-)\colon{\rm Comp}^{+}(\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}})\to{\rm Comp}^{+}(\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}})
be the classical
\mathbf{A}^{1}
-fibrant replacement functor given by the Suslin complex
[
MVW06
, 2.14]
. When applied to
\mathbb{Z}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m}^{\wedge i})
, it gives an explicit model for the weight
i
motivic complex
\mathbb{Z}(i)[i]=C_{*}\mathbb{Z}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m}^{\wedge i})

[
MVW06
, 3.1]
. Let
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}NST}}
. By adjunction, we get an evaluation pairing

{\underline{\omega}}^{*}(\mathbb{Z}(i)[i])\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}{\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}}_{D(\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}NST}})}({\underline{\omega}}^{*}(\mathbb{Z}(i)[i]),F[0])\to F[0]

where we note that
{\underline{\omega}}^{*}(\mathbb{Z}(i)[i])
is still a bounded below complex of
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}NST}}
, since the functor
{\underline{\omega}}^{*}
is exact.
By taking
\mathcal{H}^{0}
in the above pairing, we get an induced map

{\underline{\omega}}^{*}\mathcal{H}^{0}((\mathbb{Z}(i)[i]))\otimes{\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}NST}}}({\underline{\omega}}^{*}\mathcal{H}^{0}(\mathbb{Z}(i)[i]),F)\to F

noting that
{\underline{\omega}}^{*}(\mathbb{Z}(i)[i])
is concentrated in non negative degrees, and since there is an isomorphism
{\underline{\omega}}^{*}\mathcal{H}^{0}(\mathbb{Z}(i)[i])\cong{\underline{\omega}}^{*}K^{M}_{i}
, this reads, for every
\mathcal{X}=(X,D)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
, as

K^{M}_{i,X}\otimes(\gamma^{i}F)_{\mathcal{X}}\to F.

By construction, it agrees with the cup product pairing (
5.9.1
). We will not use this extended version of the pairing in the rest of the paper.

6.
Projective bundle formula

6.1
.

Let
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
and
\mathcal{X}=(X,D)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}_{ls}
. Let
V
be a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module of rank
n+1
.
Denote by

\pi:P=\mathbf{P}(V)=\operatorname{Proj}({\operatorname{Sym}}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}V)\to X

the projection of the corresponding projective bundle and set
\mathcal{P}:=(P,\pi^{*}D)
.
Let
\xi:=c_{1}(\mathcal{O}_{P}(1))\in{\operatorname{CH}}^{1}(P)
be the first Chern class of the hyperplane
line bundle
\mathcal{O}_{P}(1)
and denote by
\xi^{i}\in{\operatorname{CH}}^{i}(P)
its
i
-fold self-intersection.
We denote by
\lambda^{i}_{V}
the composition in
D(X_{{\operatorname{Nis}}})

\lambda^{i}_{V}:(\gamma^{i}F)_{\mathcal{X}}[-i]\xrightarrow{\pi^{*}}R\pi_{*}(\gamma^{i}F)_{\mathcal{P}}[-i]\xrightarrow[\eqref{para:cyclecup1}]{c_{\xi^{i}}}R\pi_{*}F_{\mathcal{P}},\quad 0\leq i\leq n.

We thus get a map

(6.1.1)

\lambda_{V}=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\lambda^{i}_{V}:\bigoplus_{i=0}^{n}(\gamma^{i}F)_{\mathcal{X}}[-i]\to R\pi_{*}F_{\mathcal{P}}.

Lemma 6.2
.

Let
F
and
(X,D)
be as in
6.1
. Consider the projection
\pi:\mathbf{P}^{1}\times X\to X
.
Then

H^{1}(\lambda_{V}^{1}):(\gamma^{1}F)_{\mathcal{X}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}R^{1}\pi_{*}F_{\mathbf{P}^{1}\otimes\mathcal{X}}

is an isomorphism and
R^{i}\pi_{*}F_{\mathbf{P}^{1}\otimes\mathcal{X}}=0
, for all
i\geq 2
.

Proof.

Set
\mathcal{P}:=\mathbf{P}^{1}\otimes\mathcal{X}
and define
C^{1}
by the exact sequence

(6.2.1)

0\to F_{\mathcal{P}}\to\underbrace{F_{(\mathbf{P}^{1},0)\otimes\mathcal{X}}\to C^{1}}_{=:C}\to 0,

where the first map is injective by the semipurity of
F
. Since
C^{1}
has support in
0\times X
and

R^{i}\pi_{*}F_{(\mathbf{P}^{1},0)\otimes\mathcal{X}}=0
, for all
i\geq 1
, by the cube invariance of the cohomology,
[
Sai20a
, Thm 9.3]
, it follows that

C
is a
\pi_{*}
-acylic resolution of
F_{\mathcal{P}}
sitting in degree
[0,1]
. This proves the vanishing statement.
Furthermore, we claim that

\pi_{*}C^{1}=(\gamma^{1}F)_{\mathcal{X}}.

Indeed, for
U\to X
étale and
\mathcal{U}=(U,D_{U})
we have

(6.2.2)

(\gamma^{1}F)_{\mathcal{X}}(U)=\frac{F((\mathbf{A}^{1},0)\otimes\mathcal{U})}{F(\mathbf{A}^{1}\otimes\mathcal{U})}=\pi_{*}C^{1}(U),

where the first equality holds by Remark
4.7
;
the second equality holds by the following two observations:
since
C^{1}
is supported on
0\times X
, we have an exact sequence on
\mathbf{P}^{1}\times X

0\to j_{*}F_{\mathbf{A}^{1}\otimes\mathcal{X}}\to j_{*}F_{(\mathbf{A}^{1},0)\otimes\mathcal{X}}\to C^{1}\to 0,

where
j:\mathbf{A}^{1}\times X\hookrightarrow\mathbf{P}^{1}\times X
is the open immersion,
and by the Lemmas
2.9
and
2.10
we have

R^{1}\pi_{*}(j_{*}F_{\mathbf{A}^{1}\otimes\mathcal{X}})=R^{1}(\pi j)_{*}F_{\mathbf{A}^{1}\otimes\mathcal{X}}=0.

Furthermore, the map
\pi_{*}F_{\mathcal{P}}\to\pi_{*}F_{(\mathbf{P}^{1},0)\otimes\mathcal{X}}

is an equality by Corollary
2.19
and hence
(
6.2.1
), (
6.2.2
) and
R^{1}\pi_{*}F_{(\mathbf{P}^{1},0)\otimes\mathcal{X}}=0
imply

R^{1}\pi_{*}F_{\mathcal{P}}=\pi_{*}C^{1}=(\gamma^{1}F)_{\mathcal{X}}
.
It remains to show that
H^{1}(\lambda_{V}^{1}):(\gamma^{1}F)_{\mathcal{X}}\to R^{1}\pi_{*}F_{\mathcal{P}}=(\gamma^{1}F)_{\mathcal{X}}

realises such isomorphism. To this end, note that on
U\to X

we can identify
H^{1}(\lambda_{V})
by Lemma
5.10

with the composition

\gamma^{1}F(\mathcal{U})\xrightarrow{\pi^{*}}\gamma^{1}F(\mathbf{P}^{1}\otimes\mathcal{U})=\frac{F((\mathbf{A}^{1},0)\otimes\mathbf{P}^{1}\otimes\mathcal{U})}{F(\mathbf{A}^{1}\otimes\mathbf{P}^{1}\otimes\mathcal{U})}\\
\xrightarrow{\Delta_{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{U}}^{*}(\Gamma\times\nu)^{*}}\frac{F((\mathbf{A}^{1},0)\otimes\mathcal{U})}{F(\mathbf{A}^{1}\otimes\mathcal{U})}=\gamma^{1}F(\mathcal{U}),

where
\nu:\mathbf{A}^{1}_{U}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{P}^{1}_{U}
is the open immersion and
we identify
\xi
on
\mathbf{P}^{1}_{U}
with the class of the divisor
0\times U

and where
\Gamma
is the graph of the projection
\mathbf{A}^{1}\times U\to\mathbf{A}^{1}
.
Thus the equality

({\operatorname{id}}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}}\times\pi)\circ(\Gamma\times\nu)\circ\Delta_{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{U}}={\operatorname{id}}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{U}}

implies the statement.
∎

We are now ready to prove the projective bundle theorem in our setting.

Theorem 6.3
.

The map (
6.1.1
) is an isomorphism in
D(X_{{\operatorname{Nis}}})
.

Proof.

The question is local on
X
. Hence the statement follows for
n=1
from Lemma
6.2
.
We now assume
n\geq 2
and
P=\mathbf{P}^{n}_{X}
.
Consider the diagram

\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{E}}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{E}}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{E}}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\textstyle{P\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{X,}

where
\pi
is the projection,
i
a section of
\pi
,
\rho
the blow-up of
P
in
i(X)
,
i_{E}
is the closed immersion
of the exceptional divisor, and
q
is the standard map, which identifies

Y
with
\mathbf{P}(W)
, where
W:=\mathcal{O}_{E}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{E}(1)
.
Denote by
V
(resp.
V_{E}
) the trivial
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module of rank
n+1
(resp.
n
) defining
the projective bundle
P
(resp.
E
) over
X
(recall we work locally on
X
). We set
\mathcal{E}:=(E,D_{|E})
and
\mathcal{Y}:=(Y,D_{|Y})
.
By Corollary
2.19
we have

(6.3.1)

R\pi_{*}F_{\mathcal{P}}\cong F_{\mathcal{X}}\oplus\tau^{\geq 1}R\pi_{*}F_{\mathcal{P}},

where the map
F_{\mathcal{X}}=\pi_{*}F_{\mathcal{P}}\to R\pi_{*}F_{\mathcal{P}}
is split by the section
i
.
Thus applying
R\pi_{*}
to the exact triangle from Theorem
3.1
induced by the blow-up
Y\to P
,
we can split off
F_{\mathcal{X}}
to obtain the triangle on the bottom of the following diagram:

\textstyle{\oplus_{i=1}^{n}(\gamma^{i}F)_{\mathcal{X}}[-i]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\oplus_{i}\lambda^{i}_{V}}
\scriptstyle{(0,\lambda_{V_{E}}[-1])}
\textstyle{R\rho_{E*}F_{\mathcal{E}}\oplus R\rho_{E*}\gamma^{1}F_{\mathcal{E}}[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{id}}+0}
\scriptstyle{\lambda_{W}}
\textstyle{R\rho_{E*}F_{\mathcal{E}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\tau^{\geq 1}R\pi_{*}F_{\mathcal{P}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{*}_{1}}
\textstyle{R\pi_{*}R\rho_{*}F_{\mathcal{Y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{E}^{*}}
\textstyle{R\rho_{E*}F_{\mathcal{E}},}

where the map labeled by
\rho^{*}_{1}
is induced by (
6.3.1
) and
\rho^{*}
.
Note that the bottom triangle is actually split since
i_{E}^{*}q^{*}={\operatorname{id}}
.
In the top left
\lambda_{V_{E}}
is applied to
\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{X}
and
(\gamma^{1}F)_{\mathcal{X}}
and it is an isomorphism by induction.
Hence the top sequence is a split triangle as well. Since
\lambda_{W}
is also an isomorphism by the
n=1
case,
it remains to show that the diagram is commutative.
Let

\xi=c_{1}(\mathcal{O}_{P}(1))
,
\xi_{E}=c_{1}(\mathcal{O}_{E}(1))
and
\eta=c_{1}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{P}(W)}(1))

be the first Chern classes of the corresponding fundamental line bundles.
The commutativity of the right square follows from

i_{E}^{*}\circ c_{\eta}=c_{i_{E}^{*}\eta}\circ i_{E}^{*}=0
,
where we use Lemma
5.9
(3)
for the first equality and
i_{E}^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{P}(W)}(1)=\mathcal{O}_{E}
for the second.
The commutativity of the left square reduces by Lemma
5.9
(3)
to the equality

\rho^{*}(\xi^{i})=q^{*}(\xi_{E}^{i-1})\cdot\eta\quad\in{\operatorname{CH}}^{i}(Y),

which is well-knwon and straightforward to check.
∎

7.
The Gysin triangle

We begin with an elementary lemma on split exact triangles in an arbitrary triangulated category.

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
A\xrightarrow{a}B\xrightarrow{b}C\xrightarrow{\partial}A[1]
be an exact triangle in a triangulated category
T
.

(1)

If
\tau\colon C\to B
is a section of
b
, then there is a unique map
\sigma\colon B\to A
,
such that
a\circ\sigma={\operatorname{id}}_{B}-e
, where
e:=\tau\circ b\in\operatorname{Hom}_{T}(B,B)
. Moreover
\sigma
is a retraction of
a
.

(2)

If
\sigma:B\to A
is a retraction of
a
, then there is a unique map
\tau:C\to B
,
such that
\tau\circ b={\operatorname{id}}_{B}-\varepsilon
, where
\varepsilon:=a\circ\sigma\in\operatorname{Hom}_{T}(B,B)
.
Moreover
\tau
is a section of
b

In
(1)
(resp.
(2)
) we call
\sigma
(resp.
\tau
)
the canonical retraction defined by
\tau
(resp. the canonical section defined by
\sigma
).
Moreover, the canonical section defined by the canonical retraction of
\tau
is equal to
\tau
,
and similarly with
\sigma
.

Proof.

(1)
. By the existence of the section
\tau
the long exact sequence
stemming from applying
\operatorname{Hom}_{T}(B,-)
to the exact triangle
(a,b,\partial)

breaks up into short exact sequences; in particular
we obtain the short exact sequence

0\to\operatorname{Hom}_{T}(B,A)\xrightarrow{a\circ}\operatorname{Hom}_{T}(B,B)\xrightarrow{b\circ}\operatorname{Hom}_{T}(B,C)\to 0.

This gives a unique
\sigma
with
a\circ\sigma={\operatorname{id}}_{B}-e
.
It follows that
a\circ(\sigma\circ a)=a
. Since
also
a\circ:\operatorname{Hom}_{T}(A,A)\to\operatorname{Hom}_{T}(A,B)
is injective (by the existence of
\tau
), we see that

\sigma
is a retraction.
Similar for
(2)
; the other statements are clear.
∎

7.2
.

Having the projective bundle formula and the blow-up formula at disposal, we can construct the Gysin triangle by formally following the procedure indicated by Voevodsky in
[
Voe00b
, 3.5]
. Note that our statement is sheaf-theoretic, and therefore the arrows are reversed compared to
loc.cit.
We begin by setting the notation.

Let
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
and
\mathcal{X}=(X,D)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}_{ls}
(see Notation
2.8
).
Let
i:Z\hookrightarrow X
be a smooth closed subscheme intersecting
D
transversally (see Definition
2.11
).
Denote by
\rho\colon\tilde{X}\to X
the blow-up of
X
along
Z
, and let
\rho_{E}\colon E=\rho^{-1}(Z)\to Z
be the exceptional divisor. We define the modulus pairs

\tilde{\mathcal{X}}=(\tilde{X},D_{|\tilde{X}})
,
\mathcal{Z}=(Z,D_{|Z})
and
\mathcal{E}=(E,D_{|E})
with the obvious convention on the divisor.

Let
\rho_{1}\colon Y\to X\times\mathbf{P}^{1}
be the blow-up of
X\times\mathbf{P}^{1}
along
Z\times 0
and let
E_{1}=\rho_{1}^{-1}(Z\times 0)
be the exceptional divisor.
We obtain the following modulus pairs in
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}_{ls}

\mathcal{Y}=(Y,(D\times\mathbf{P}^{1}+X\times\infty)_{|Y}),\quad\mathcal{E}_{1}=(E_{1},(D\times 0)_{|E_{1}})

and the following obvious morphisms in
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}

i:\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{X},\quad i_{Z0}:\mathcal{Z}=\mathcal{Z}\otimes 0\to\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}},

\rho:\tilde{\mathcal{X}}\to\mathcal{X},\quad\rho_{1}:\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}},\quad i_{\tilde{X}}:\tilde{\mathcal{X}}\to\mathcal{Y},

i_{E}:\mathcal{E}\to\tilde{\mathcal{X}},\quad i_{E_{1}}:\mathcal{E}_{1}\to\mathcal{Y},\quad i_{EE_{1}}:\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}_{1},

\rho_{E}:\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{Z},\quad\rho_{E_{1}}:\mathcal{E}_{1}\to\mathcal{Z}\otimes 0=\mathcal{Z},

i_{\varepsilon}:\mathcal{X}=\mathcal{X}\otimes\varepsilon\to\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}},\quad\varepsilon\in\{0,1\},\quad\pi:\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}}\to\mathcal{X},

where all the
i
’s are induced by closed immersions of the underlying schemes, in particular
i_{\tilde{X}}
is induced by
identifying
\tilde{X}
with the strict transform of
X\times 0
in
Y
and
i_{EE_{1}}
is the pullback
of
i_{\tilde{X}}
along
i_{E_{1}}
. This gives the following commutative diagram in
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\mathcal{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Z}\otimes 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{EE_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\tilde{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{E_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{E_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{Z0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{1}}

We will denote the underlying morphisms of schemes by the same letter.
Note that
i_{1}:\mathcal{X}=\mathcal{X}\otimes 1\to\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}}
extends canonically to a morphism

i_{1,Y}:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Y}.

Finally the morphism underlying
i_{EE_{1}}
is equal to the natural inclusion

i_{EE_{1}}:\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{N}_{Z/X}^{\vee})\hookrightarrow\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{O}_{Z}\oplus\mathcal{N}_{Z/X}^{\vee}),

where
\mathcal{N}_{Z/X}^{\vee}=\mathcal{I}/\mathcal{I}^{2}
is the conormal sheaf,
\mathcal{I}
being the ideal sheaf of
Z\hookrightarrow X
. We obtain the following diagram,

(7.2.1)

\textstyle{\bigoplus_{i=0}^{j}(\gamma^{i}F)_{\mathcal{Z}}[-i]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{Z}\oplus\mathcal{N}_{Z/X}^{\vee}}}
\textstyle{R\rho_{E_{1}*}F_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{EE_{1}}^{*}}
\textstyle{\bigoplus_{i=0}^{j-1}(\gamma^{i}F)_{\mathcal{Z}}[-i]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda_{\mathcal{N}_{Z/X}^{\vee}}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{R\rho_{E*}F_{\mathcal{E}},}

where
j={\operatorname{codim}}(Z,X)
, the horizontal maps are the isomorphisms from Theorem
6.3
, and the vertical
map on the left is the projection. Using Lemma
5.9
it is direct to check that
(
7.2.1
) commutes. Thus
i_{EE_{1}}^{*}
in (
7.2.1
) has a canonical section

s:R\rho_{E*}F_{\mathcal{E}}\to R\rho_{E_{1}*}F_{\mathcal{E}_{1}},

splitting off the summand
(\gamma^{j}F)_{\mathcal{Z}}[-j]
.
Let
b_{1}
be the morphism in
D(X_{{\operatorname{Nis}}})
defined as the composition

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\pi_{*}R\rho_{1*}F_{\mathcal{Y}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{*}R\rho_{E_{1}*}F_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus_{i=0}^{j}i_{*}(\gamma^{i}F)_{\mathcal{Z}}[-i]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus_{i=1}^{j}i_{*}(\gamma^{i}F)_{\mathcal{Z}}[-i]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{E_{1}}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

where the second isomorphism is the inverse of
\lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{Z}\oplus\mathcal{N}_{Z/X}^{\vee}}
, and the rightmost arrow is the canonical projection. Similarly, we define
b
as the composition

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\rho_{*}F_{\tilde{\mathcal{X}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{*}R\rho_{E*}F_{\mathcal{E}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus_{i=0}^{j-1}i_{*}(\gamma^{i}F)_{\mathcal{Z}}[-i]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus_{i=1}^{j-1}i_{*}(\gamma^{i}F)_{\mathcal{Z}}[-i]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{E}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

where the second isomorphism is the inverse of
\lambda_{\mathcal{N}_{Z/X}^{\vee}}
and the last map is the canonical projection. This gives the following commutative diagram of solid arrows in
D(X_{{\operatorname{Nis}}})
:

(7.2.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\pi_{*}F_{\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\pi_{*}R\rho_{1*}F_{\mathcal{Y}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus_{i=1}^{j}i_{*}(\gamma^{i}F)_{\mathcal{Z}}[-i]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\mathcal{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\rho_{*}F_{\tilde{\mathcal{X}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus_{i=1}^{j-1}i_{*}(\gamma^{i}F)_{\mathcal{Z}}[-i],}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{0}^{*}=i_{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{*}_{1,Y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{*}_{\tilde{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\not\circlearrowright}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau}

where the dashed arrows are defined as follows. First, note that the bottom horizontal sequence is a distinguished triangle obtained from the distinguished blow-up triangle from Theorem
3.1
for
(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Z})
. Indeed, we have the following diagram in
D(X_{{\operatorname{Nis}}})
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\mathcal{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\rho_{*}F_{\tilde{\mathcal{X}}}\oplus i_{*}F_{\mathcal{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{*}R\rho_{E*}F_{\mathcal{E}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus_{i=0}^{j-1}(\gamma^{i}F)_{\mathcal{Z}}[-i]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\mathcal{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\rho_{*}F_{\tilde{\mathcal{X}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{*}\tau_{\geq 1}R\rho_{E*}F_{\mathcal{E}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus_{i=1}^{j-1}(\gamma^{i}F)_{\mathcal{Z}}[-i]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{*}\oplus(-i^{*})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{*}_{E}+\rho_{E}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{can.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{\mathcal{N}_{Z/X}^{\vee}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{can.}\circ(i_{E}^{*})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sum_{i=1}^{j-1}\lambda_{V}^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

where
V=\mathcal{N}_{Z/X}^{\vee}
, the unlabeled maps are the natural projections,
\mathrm{can.}
is the canonical map to the truncation
\tau_{\geq 1}
, and the composition of the second and the (inverse of the) third arrow in the bottom line is
b
. Every square is commutative, and the middle one is clearly homotopy cartesian, so that the bottom line is part of a distinguished triangle as required.
In a similar fashion, the top line of (
7.2.2
) is part of a distinguished triangle obtained from the blow-up triangle for
(\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}},Z\times 0)
after applying
R\pi_{*}
.

Going back to (
7.2.2
), note that the right square is commutative thanks to the definitions of
b
and
b_{1}
, and the commutativity of (
7.2.1
). The square on the left commutes if the left vertical arrow is
i_{0}^{*}
. By cube-invariance it is an isomorphism, with inverse
\pi^{*}
and thus it is equal to
i_{1}^{*}
. Replacing
i_{0}^{*}
with
i_{1}^{*}
we see then that the upper triangle in the left square is commutative. The lower triangle on the other hand does not commute. Set

\sigma_{1}:=\pi^{*}\circ i_{1,Y}^{*};

it is a retraction of
\rho_{1}^{*}
. We define the (dotted) map
\tau_{1}
as the canonical section
defined by
\sigma_{1}
, see Lemma
7.1
.
Set

(7.2.3)

\tau:=i_{\tilde{X}}^{*}\circ\tau_{1}\circ s;

it is a section of
b
(that we can identify with
(\mathrm{can.}\circ i_{E}^{*})
up to the isomorphism
\tau_{\geq 1}R\rho_{E*}F_{\mathcal{E}}\simeq\bigoplus_{i=1}^{j-1}(\gamma^{i}F)_{\mathcal{Z}}[-i]
).
We define
\sigma
as the canonical section defined by
\tau
(again see Lemma
7.1
). The proof of the following corollary is immediate from the previous constructions.

Corollary 7.3
.

Let
F
and
\rho:\tilde{\mathcal{X}}\to\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{Z}
be as in
7.2
and
j={\operatorname{codim}}(Z,X)
.
We have isomorphisms in
D(X_{\operatorname{Nis}})

(7.3.1)

F_{\mathcal{X}}\oplus\bigoplus_{r=1}^{j-1}i_{*}\gamma^{r}F_{\mathcal{Z}}[-r]\xrightarrow[\simeq]{\rho^{*}+\tau}R\rho_{*}F_{\tilde{\mathcal{X}}}.

7.4
.

Let
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
,
\mathcal{X}=(X,D)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}_{ls}
and let
i:Z\hookrightarrow X
be a smooth closed subscheme of
codimension
j\geq 1
intersecting
D
transversally.
Set
\mathcal{Z}=(Z,D_{|Z})
.
Following
[
Voe00b
, p. 220/21]
we define the Gysin map

(7.4.1)

g_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{X}}:i_{*}(\gamma^{j}F_{\mathcal{Z}})[-j]\to F_{\mathcal{X}}

as follows: let the notation be as in (
7.2.2
). We set

v\colon i_{*}(\gamma^{j}F)_{\mathcal{Z}}[-j]\longrightarrow R\pi_{*}R_{\rho_{1*}}F_{\mathcal{Y}}

as the composition
\tau_{1}\circ\nu^{j}
, where
\nu^{j}
is the canonical inclusion
i_{*}(\gamma^{j}F)_{\mathcal{Z}}[-j]\to\bigoplus_{i=1}^{j}i_{*}(\gamma^{i}F)_{\mathcal{Z}}[-i]
. Then the Gysin map is

g_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{X}}:=-\sigma\circ i_{\tilde{X}}^{*}\circ v.

Remark 7.5
.

We can replace the top row in (
7.2.2
) with the equivalent one

(7.5.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\pi_{*}F_{\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\pi_{*}R{\rho_{1*}}F_{\mathcal{Y}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{*}\tau_{\geq 1}R\rho_{E_{1*}}F_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{can.}\circ i_{E_{1}}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{1}^{\prime}}

where
\tau_{1}^{\prime}
is the canonical section induced by
\sigma_{1}
. Note that
\tau_{1}=\tau_{1}^{\prime}\circ\sum_{i=1}^{j}\lambda^{i}_{V_{1}}
where
V_{1}=\mathcal{O}_{Z}\oplus\mathcal{N}^{\vee}_{Z/X}
,
and
\mathrm{can.}
is the canonical map
i_{*}R\rho_{E_{1*}}F_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}\to i_{*}\tau_{\geq 1}R\rho_{E_{1*}}F_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}
. In particular the Gysin map satisfies

(7.5.2)

g_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{X}}=-\sigma\circ i_{\tilde{X}}^{*}\circ\tau_{1}^{\prime}\circ\lambda_{V_{1}}^{j}.

Remark 7.6
.

The Gysin map can be described alternatively as follows. Set

\beta:=i_{1,Y}^{*}-\sigma\circ i_{\tilde{X}}^{*}:R\pi_{*}R\rho_{1*}F_{\mathcal{Y}}\to F_{\mathcal{X}}.

We have
\beta\circ(\rho_{1}^{*})=0:R\pi_{*}F_{\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}}}\to F_{\mathcal{X}}
, and since the top row in (
7.2.2
) is split exact, there exists a unique map

\beta_{1}\colon\bigoplus_{i=1}^{j}i_{*}(\gamma^{i}F)_{\mathcal{Z}}[-i]\to F_{\mathcal{X}}

such that
\beta=\beta_{1}\circ b_{1}
. Then a diagram chase shows that

(7.6.1)

g_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{X}}=\beta_{1}\circ\nu^{j}.

Remark 7.7
.

Let
i:Z\hookrightarrow X
be as above. One can consider the class
[Z]
of
Z
in the Chow group with support
{\operatorname{CH}}^{j}_{Z}(X)
, and the cup product construction (
5.8.1
) gives a morphism

(7.7.1)

c_{[Z]}\colon(\gamma^{j}F)_{\mathcal{X}}[-j]\to R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}F_{\mathcal{X}}\to F_{\mathcal{X}}

in
D(X_{{\operatorname{Nis}}})
, where the last morphism is the forget support map. It is a natural question to compare (
7.7.1
) and (
7.4.1
): this is done in Theorem
7.12
below.

Proposition 7.8
.

Let
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
,
\mathcal{X}=(X,D)
,
\mathcal{Z}=(Z,D_{|Z})
be as in
7.4
above.
Assume
X
is equidimensional and let
\Phi
be a family of supports on
X
and
\alpha\in{\operatorname{CH}}_{\Phi}^{r}(X)
. Then the following diagram

\textstyle{i_{*}(\gamma^{j+r}F)_{\mathcal{Z}}[-j-r]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{X}}}
\scriptstyle{c_{i^{*}\alpha}}
\textstyle{\gamma^{r}F_{\mathcal{X}}[-r]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c_{\alpha}}
\textstyle{i_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z\cap\Phi}\gamma^{j}F_{\mathcal{Z}}[-j]=R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}i_{*}\gamma^{j}F_{\mathcal{Z}}[-j]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{X}}}
\textstyle{R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}F_{\mathcal{X}}}

commutes, where
i^{*}\alpha\in{\operatorname{CH}}^{r}_{\Phi\cap Z}(Z)
is the refined pullback of
\alpha
(see Lemma
5.3
).

Proof.

We use the notation from
7.2
and in particular (
7.2.2
) above.
We compute:

\displaystyle g_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{X}}\circ c_{i^{*}\alpha}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(*)}}{{=}}-\sigma\circ i_{\tilde{X}}^{*}\circ\tau_{1}^{\prime}\circ\lambda_{V_{1}}^{j}\circ c_{i^{*}\alpha}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(2*)}}{{=}}-\sigma\circ i_{\tilde{X}}^{*}\circ\tau_{1}^{\prime}\circ c_{\xi^{j}}\circ\rho_{E_{1}}^{*}\circ c_{i^{*}\alpha}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(3*)}}{{=}}-\sigma\circ i_{\tilde{X}}^{*}\circ\tau_{1}^{\prime}\circ c_{(i\rho_{E_{1}})^{*}\alpha}\circ\lambda_{V_{1}}^{j}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(4*)}}{{=}}-\sigma\circ i_{\tilde{X}}^{*}\circ\tau_{1}^{\prime}\circ c_{(i\rho_{E_{1}})^{*}\alpha}\circ((\mathrm{can.}\circ i_{E_{1}}^{*})\circ\tau_{1}^{\prime})\circ\lambda_{V_{1}}^{j}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(5*)}}{{=}}-\sigma\circ i_{\tilde{X}}^{*}\circ\tau_{1}^{\prime}\circ(\mathrm{can.}\circ i_{E_{1}}^{*})\circ c_{\rho_{1}^{*}\pi^{*}\alpha}\circ\tau_{1}^{\prime}\circ\lambda_{V_{1}}^{j}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(6*)}}{{=}}-\sigma\circ i_{\tilde{X}}^{*}\circ({\operatorname{id}}-\rho_{1}^{*}\circ\sigma_{1})\circ(c_{\rho_{1}^{*}\pi^{*}\alpha})\circ\tau_{1}^{\prime}\circ\lambda_{V_{1}}^{j}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(7*)}}{{=}}-\sigma\circ i_{\tilde{X}}^{*}\circ c_{\rho_{1}^{*}\pi^{*}\alpha}\circ({\operatorname{id}}-\rho_{1}^{*}\circ\sigma_{1})\circ\tau_{1}^{\prime}\circ\lambda_{V_{1}}^{j}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(8*)}}{{=}}-\sigma\circ i_{\tilde{X}}^{*}\circ c_{\rho_{1}^{*}\pi^{*}\alpha}\circ\tau_{1}^{\prime}\circ\lambda_{V_{1}}^{j}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(9*)}}{{=}}-\sigma\circ c_{\rho^{*}\alpha}\circ i_{\tilde{X}}^{*}\circ\tau_{1}^{\prime}\circ\lambda_{V_{1}}^{j}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(10*)}}{{=}}-\sigma\circ c_{\rho^{*}\alpha}\circ(\rho^{*}\circ\sigma+\tau\circ b)\circ i_{\tilde{X}}^{*}\circ\tau_{1}^{\prime}\circ\lambda_{V_{1}}^{j}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(11*)}}{{=}}-\sigma\circ\rho^{*}\circ c_{\alpha}\circ\sigma\circ i_{\tilde{X}}^{*}\circ\tau_{1}\circ\nu^{j}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(12*)}}{{=}}-c_{\alpha}\circ\sigma\circ i_{\tilde{X}}^{*}\circ\tau_{1}\circ\nu^{j}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(13*)}}{{=}}c_{\alpha}\circ g_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{X}},

where

(*)

holds by (
7.5.2
),

(2*)

holds by the definition of
\lambda_{V_{1}}^{j}
in
6.1
,

(3*)

holds by Lemma
5.9
(3)
,
(4)
and
the definition of
\lambda_{V}^{j}
,

(4*)

holds by
((\mathrm{can.}\circ i_{E_{1}}^{*})\circ\tau_{1}^{\prime})={\operatorname{id}}
on
i_{*}\tau_{\geq 1}R\rho_{E_{1*}}F_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}
,

(5*)

holds by
i\rho_{E_{1}}=\pi\rho_{1}i_{E_{1}}
,
and Lemma
5.9
(3)
,

(6*)

holds since
\tau_{1}^{\prime}
is the section defined by the section
\sigma_{1}
(see
7.1
),

(7*)

follows again from Lemma
5.9
(3)
and the fact that
\sigma_{1}=\pi^{*}\circ i_{1,Y}^{*}
and
\pi\rho_{1}i_{1,Y}={\operatorname{id}}
.

(8*)

holds by
({\operatorname{id}}-\rho_{1}^{*}\circ\sigma_{1})\circ\tau_{1}^{\prime}=\tau_{1}^{\prime}
,

(9*)

holds by
\pi\rho_{1}i_{\tilde{X}}=\rho
,
and Lemma
5.9
(3)
,

(10*)

holds by
{\operatorname{id}}=\rho^{*}\circ\sigma+\tau\circ b
,

(11*)

holds by Lemma
5.9
(3)
,
\tau_{1}^{\prime}\circ\lambda^{j}_{V}=\tau_{1}\circ\nu^{j}
, see Remark
7.5
, and

b\circ i_{\tilde{X}}^{*}\circ\tau_{1}^{\prime}\circ\lambda_{V}^{j}=h\circ b_{1}\circ\tau_{1}\circ\nu^{j}=h\circ\nu^{j}=0,

(12*)

holds by
\sigma\circ\rho^{*}={\operatorname{id}}
,

(13*)

holds by definition of the Gysin map in
7.4
;

whence the statement.
∎

Proposition 7.9
.

Let
\mathcal{X}=(X,D)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}_{ls}
and
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
.
Let
i:Z\hookrightarrow X
be a smooth closed subscheme of codimension
j
,
intersecting
D
transversally (see Definition
2.11
) and set
\mathcal{Z}=(Z,D_{|Z})
.
Let
X^{\prime}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
and let

f:X^{\prime}\to X
be a morphism, such that

i^{\prime}:Z^{\prime}:=X^{\prime}\times_{X}Z\hookrightarrow X^{\prime}
is a smooth closed subscheme of pure codimension
m\leq j

and
|f^{*}D|
is a simple normal crossing divisor intersecting
Z^{\prime}
transversally.
Set
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}:=(X^{\prime},f^{*}D)
and
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}:=(Z^{\prime},(f^{*}D)_{|Z^{\prime}})
. Let
f_{Z}:Z^{\prime}\to Z
be the base change of
f
and
consider the excess normal bundle

\mathcal{E}xc=f_{Z}^{*}(\mathcal{N}_{Z/X})/\mathcal{N}_{Z^{\prime}/X^{\prime}}.

Then the following diagram commutes

(7.9.1)

\textstyle{i_{*}\gamma^{j}F_{\mathcal{Z}}[-j]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{*}_{Z}}
\scriptstyle{g_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{X}}}
\textstyle{F_{\mathcal{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{*}}
\textstyle{Rf_{*}(i^{\prime}_{*}\gamma^{j}F_{\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}[-j])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c_{\beta}}
\textstyle{Rf_{*}(i^{\prime}_{*}\gamma^{m}F_{\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}[-m])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}/\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{Rf_{*}F_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}},}

where
\beta:=c_{j-m}(\mathcal{E}xc)\cap Z^{\prime}\in{\operatorname{CH}}^{j-m}(Z^{\prime})
and
c_{j-m}(\mathcal{E}xc)

is the
(j-m)
th Chern class of
\mathcal{E}xc
.
In particular, the Gysin map is compatible with smooth base change.

Proof.

First observe that given two distinguished triangles
\Delta
and
\Delta^{\prime}
with sections

\sigma
and
\sigma^{\prime}
(resp.
\tau
and
\tau^{\prime}
) as in Lemma
7.1
,
and
\varphi:\Delta\to\Delta^{\prime}
is a morphism of triangles which commutes with

\sigma
and
\sigma^{\prime}
(resp.
\tau
and
\tau^{\prime}
), then
\varphi
also commutes with the
canonical sections defined by these sections.
From this and the commutative diagram

(7.9.2)

\textstyle{{\rm Bl}_{Z^{\prime}}(X^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm Bl}_{Z}(X)\times_{X}X^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm Bl}_{Z}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm Bl}_{Z^{\prime}\times 0}(X^{\prime}\times\mathbf{P}^{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm Bl}_{Z\times 0}(X\times\mathbf{P}^{1})\times_{X}X^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm Bl}_{Z\times 0}(X\times\mathbf{P}^{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X^{\prime}\times\mathbf{P}^{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X\times\mathbf{P}^{1}}

(and all the diagrams which this diagram induces)
we see that the various pullbacks induced by
f:X^{\prime}\to X
commute with the section

\sigma_{1}
, thus with
\tau_{1}^{\prime}
from (
7.5.1
).
It is direct to check that by (
7.5.2
)
we are reduced to show the commutativity of the following diagram

(7.9.3)

\textstyle{i_{*}\gamma^{j}F_{\mathcal{Z}}[-j]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda^{j}_{V}}
\scriptstyle{f_{Z}^{*}}
\textstyle{R\rho_{E_{1}*}F_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{f}_{E_{1}}^{*}}
\textstyle{Rf_{Z*}R\rho_{E_{1}^{\prime}*}F_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{1}}}
\textstyle{Rf_{*}(i^{\prime}_{*}\gamma^{j}F_{\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}[-j])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c_{\beta}}
\textstyle{Rf_{*}(i^{\prime}_{*}\gamma^{m}F_{\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}[-m])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda^{m}_{V^{\prime}}}

where
V=\mathcal{N}_{Z/X}^{\vee}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{Z}
,
V^{\prime}=\mathcal{N}_{Z^{\prime}/X^{\prime}}^{\vee}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{Z^{\prime}}
,

E_{1}
(resp.
E_{1}^{\prime}
) is the exceptional divisor in
{\rm Bl}_{Z\times 0}(X\times\mathbf{P}^{1})
(resp. in
{\rm Bl}_{Z^{\prime}\times 0}(X^{\prime}\times\mathbf{P}^{1})
),
the maps are as in the following commutative diagram
(which is induced by (
7.9.2
) and is in general not cartesian)

\textstyle{E^{\prime}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{f}_{E_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{E_{1}^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{E_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{E_{1}}}
\textstyle{Z^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{Z}}
\textstyle{Z,}

and
\mathcal{E}_{1}=(E_{1},D_{|E_{1}})
(resp.
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{1}=(E^{\prime}_{1},D_{|E^{\prime}_{1}})
). In view of the definition of
\lambda_{V}
(see
6.1
)
and Lemma
5.9
(3)
,
(4)
we are reduced to show

(7.9.4)

\tilde{f}_{E_{1}}^{*}(\xi^{j})=\zeta^{m}\cdot\rho_{E^{\prime}_{1}}^{*}(\beta)\quad\text{in }{\operatorname{CH}}^{j}(E_{1}^{\prime}),

where
\xi=c_{1}(\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}(1))\in{\operatorname{CH}}^{1}(E_{1})
and
\zeta=c_{1}(\mathcal{O}_{E^{\prime}_{1}}(1))\in{\operatorname{CH}}^{1}(E^{\prime}_{1})
.

This follows from the excess intersection formula:
Indeed, consider the cartesian diagram

\textstyle{Z^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s^{\prime}}
\textstyle{Z\times_{X}X^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{Z}}
\textstyle{Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s}
\textstyle{E_{1}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E_{1}\times_{X}X^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E_{1},}

where the lower horizontal map is a factorization of
\tilde{f}_{E_{1}}
and
s
is the closed immersion
induced by the zero section
Z\hookrightarrow N_{Z/X}
followed by the open immersion
N_{Z/X}\hookrightarrow E_{1}

and similar with
s^{\prime}
. We obeserve

{s^{\prime}}^{*}\mathcal{N}_{E_{1}^{\prime}/E_{1}\times_{X}X^{\prime}}=\mathcal{N}_{Z^{\prime}/E_{1}\times_{X}X^{\prime}}/\mathcal{N}_{Z^{\prime}/E^{\prime}_{1}}=f_{Z}^{*}\mathcal{N}_{Z/N_{Z/X}}/\mathcal{N}_{Z^{\prime}/N_{Z^{\prime}/X^{\prime}}}=\mathcal{E}xc,

see, e.g.,
[
Ful98
, Exa 6.3.2]
for the first equality.
Furthermore, we have
\xi^{j}=s_{*}Z
and
\zeta^{m}=s^{\prime}_{*}(Z^{\prime})
.
Thus the projection formula
[
Ful98
, Exa 8.1.7]
and the above yield

\zeta^{m}\cdot\rho_{E^{\prime}_{1}}^{*}(\beta)=s^{\prime}_{*}(c_{j-m}(\mathcal{E}xc)\cap Z^{\prime})=s^{\prime}_{*}(c_{j-m}({s^{\prime}}^{*}\mathcal{N}_{E_{1}^{\prime}/E_{1}\times_{X}X^{\prime}})\cap Z^{\prime}),

which is equal to
\tilde{f}_{E_{1}}^{*}(s_{*}Z)
by excess intersection
[
Ful98
, Prop 6.6(c)]
.

∎

Lemma 7.10
.

Let the notations be as in
7.2
and
7.4
and assume
j={\operatorname{codim}}_{X}(Z)\geq 1
.
Then the pullback maps
i_{E_{1}}^{*}
and
\rho^{*}
induce the following isomorphism on cohomology with supports

(7.10.1)

i_{E_{1}}^{*}:R^{j}\pi_{*}R\rho_{1*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{E_{1}}F_{\mathcal{Y}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}i_{*}R^{j}\rho_{E_{1}*}F_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}

and

(7.10.2)

\rho^{*}:R^{j}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}F_{\mathcal{X}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}R^{j}\rho_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{E}F_{\tilde{\mathcal{X}}}.

Furthermore, if we define the local Gysin map
g_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{X},Z}^{j}
by the composition

g_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{X},Z}^{j}:i_{*}\gamma^{j}F_{\mathcal{Z}}\xrightarrow{\lambda^{j}_{V}}i_{*}R^{j}\rho_{E_{1}*}F_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}\xrightarrow{-(i_{E_{1}}^{*})^{-1}}R^{j}\pi_{*}R\rho_{1*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{E_{1}}F_{\mathcal{Y}}\\
\xrightarrow{i_{\tilde{X}}^{*}}R^{j}\rho_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{E}F_{\tilde{\mathcal{X}}}\xrightarrow{(\rho^{*})^{-1}}R^{j}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}F_{\mathcal{X}},

where
V=\mathcal{N}_{Z/X}^{\vee}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{Z}
,
then the Gysin map
g_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{X}}
is equal to the composition

i_{*}\gamma^{j}F_{\mathcal{Z}}[-j]\xrightarrow{g_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{X},Z}^{j}[-j]}R^{j}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}F_{\mathcal{X}}[-j]\cong R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}F_{\mathcal{X}}\to F_{\mathcal{X}},

where the isomorphism
R^{j}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}F_{\mathcal{X}}[-j]\cong R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}F_{\mathcal{X}}

is
[
Sai20a
, Cor 8.6(3)]
and the last arrow is the forget-supports-map.
Moreover, the Gysin map determines the local Gysin map via

g_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{X},Z}^{j}=R^{j}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}(g_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{X}})[-j].

Proof.

We will use without further notice the isomorphism

R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{A}Rf_{*}=Rf_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{f^{-1}(A)}
, for a morphism
f:V\to W
and a closed subset
A\subset W
.
For (
7.10.1
) apply
R\pi_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z\times 0}
to the blow-up sequence
of
(X\otimes{\overline{\square}},Z\times 0)
(see Theorem
3.1
) to obtain the following long exact sequence

\ldots\to R^{j}\pi_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z\times 0}F_{\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}}}\to R^{j}\pi_{*}R\rho_{1*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{E_{1}}F_{\mathcal{Y}}\xrightarrow{i_{E_{1}}^{*}}i_{*}R^{j}\rho_{E_{1}*}F_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}\to\ldots

Since
Z\times 0
has codimension
j+1
in
X\times\mathbf{P}^{1}
the term on the left vanishes by

[
Sai20a
, Cor 8.6(3)]
; by existence of the canonical section as in (
7.2
)
(for
(\mathcal{X}\times{\overline{\square}},Z\times 0)
instead of
(\mathcal{X},Z)
) we see that
i_{E_{1}}^{*}
is split surjective.
This yields the isomorphism (
7.10.1
). For (
7.10.2
) apply
R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}
to the
blow-up sequence of
(\mathcal{X},Z)
to obtain the long exact sequence

\ldots\to R^{j}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}F_{\mathcal{X}}\xrightarrow{\rho^{*}}R^{j}\rho_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{E}F_{\tilde{\mathcal{X}}}\to i_{*}R^{j}\rho_{E*}F_{\mathcal{E}}\to\ldots

By the existence of the canonical section
\sigma
as in (
7.2
) the map
\rho^{*}
is injective and
by the projective bundle formula, see Theorem
6.3
, the right hand side vanishes.
This yields the isomorphism (
7.10.2
).

Since
R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}=i_{*}Ri^{!}
and
Ri^{!}
is right adjoint to
i_{*}
,
the Gysin morphism factors via the counit
R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}F_{\mathcal{X}}\to F_{\mathcal{X}}

and by the isomorphism
R^{j}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}F_{\mathcal{X}}[-j]\cong R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}F_{\mathcal{X}}
also via

g_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{X}}:i_{*}\gamma^{j}F_{\mathcal{Z}}[-j]\xrightarrow{R^{j}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}(g_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{X}})[-j]}R^{j}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}F_{\mathcal{X}}[-j]\cong R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}F_{\mathcal{X}}\to F_{\mathcal{X}}.

Thus it remains to show

g_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{X},Z}^{j}=R^{j}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}(g_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{X}}).

By (
7.5.2
) it remains to show that

(7.10.3)

R^{j}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}(\sigma)=\eqref{lem:loc-gysin2}^{-1},

and that
\tau_{1}^{j}:=R^{j}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}(\tau_{1}^{\prime})
is equal to the composition

(7.10.4)

i_{*}R^{j}\rho_{E_{1}*}F_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}\xrightarrow{\eqref{lem:loc-gysin1}^{-1}}R^{j}\pi_{*}R\rho_{1*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\rho_{1}^{-1}(Z\times 0)}F_{\mathcal{Y}}\xrightarrow{e}R^{j}\pi_{*}R\rho_{1*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\rho_{1}^{-1}(Z\times\mathbf{P}^{1})}F_{\mathcal{Y}},

where
e
is the enlarge-support map.
(Note that
i_{\tilde{X}}^{*}
from the definition of the local Gysin map factors via
e
.)
For (
7.10.3
) we observe that
R^{j}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}(\sigma)
is by definition
a section of the isomorphism (
7.10.2
), hence has to be the inverse of that isomorphism.
To compute
\tau_{1}^{j}
we consider the following diagram:

(7.10.5)

\textstyle{R^{j}\pi_{*}R\rho_{1*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\rho_{1}^{-1}(Z\times 0)}F_{\mathcal{Y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{e}
\scriptstyle{b^{\prime\prime}}
\textstyle{R^{j}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}R\pi_{*}F_{\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{0}^{*}=i_{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{1}^{*}}
\textstyle{R^{j}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}R\pi_{*}R\rho_{1*}F_{\mathcal{Y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{1,Y}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{b_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{1}^{j}}
\textstyle{i_{*}R^{j}\rho_{E_{1}*}F_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{1}^{j}}
\textstyle{R^{j}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}F_{\mathcal{X}}.}

Here the middle line and the triangle on the left lower side are induced by applying

R^{j}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}
to the diagram (
7.2.2
)
up to the isomorphism
\bigoplus_{i=1}^{j}(\gamma^{i}F)_{\mathcal{Z}}[-i]\simeq\tau_{\geq 1}R\rho_{E_{1}*}F_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}
from (
7.2.1
),
with the obvious notation for
\sigma_{1}^{j}
and
\tau_{1}^{j}
, and
b^{\prime\prime}
is the isomorphism
(
7.10.1
). By definition of
\tau_{1}^{j}
(see
7.2
and Lemma
7.1
)
we have

\tau^{j}_{1}\circ b_{1}={\operatorname{id}}-\rho_{1}^{*}\sigma_{1}^{j}.

Thus

(7.10.6)

\tau_{1}^{j}=\tau_{1}^{j}b_{1}e(b^{\prime\prime})^{-1}=e(b^{\prime\prime})^{-1}-\rho_{1}^{*}\sigma_{1}^{j}e(b^{\prime\prime})^{-1}.

By definition
\sigma_{1}^{j}=(i_{0}^{*})^{-1}i_{1,Y}^{*}=\pi^{*}\circ i_{1,Y}^{*}
. We claim that
i_{1,Y}^{*}\circ e=0
.
Indeed
i_{1,Y}^{*}
is induced by
F_{\mathcal{Y}}\to i_{1,Y*}F_{\mathcal{X}\otimes 1}
.
Consider the natural commutative diagram

\textstyle{R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\rho_{1}^{-1}(Z\times 0)}F_{\mathcal{Y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\rho_{1}^{-1}(Z\times 0)}(i_{1,Y*}F_{\mathcal{X}\otimes 1})=0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F_{\mathcal{Y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{i_{1,Y*}F_{\mathcal{X}\otimes 1},}

where the top right corner vanishes since
\rho_{1}^{-1}(Z\times 0)\cap i_{1,Y}(X\times\{1\})=\emptyset
.
The map
i_{1,Y}^{*}\circ e
is induced by applying
R^{j}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}R\pi_{*}R\rho_{1*}
to this diagram
and going counter-clockwise starting at the top left corner; hence the vanishing
i_{1,Y}^{*}\circ e=0
.
Thus (
7.10.6
) yields
\tau_{1}^{j}=e(b^{\prime\prime})^{-1}
, which proves that
\tau^{j}_{1}
is equal
to the composition (
7.10.4
).
∎

7.11
.

We recall a general formula for the refined Gysin morphism of a blow-up.
Let
a:V\hookrightarrow W
be a regular closed immersion of quasi-projective
k
-schemes
with normal sheaf
\mathcal{N}_{V/W}
and denote by
f:\tilde{W}\to W
the blow-up of
W
along
V
. We have then the cartesian square

\textstyle{\tilde{V}:=f^{-1}(V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{\tilde{W}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a}
\textstyle{W}

Since
\tilde{V}=\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{N}_{V/W}^{\vee})
the excess normal bundle satisfies

\mathcal{E}xc=g^{*}\mathcal{N}_{V/W}/\mathcal{N}_{\tilde{V}/\tilde{W}}=g^{*}\mathcal{N}_{V/W}/\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{V}}(-1),

i.e. it is the universal quotient bundle on
\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{N}_{V/W}^{\vee})
(see e.g.
[
Ful98
, 6.7]
).

Let
W^{\prime}\to W
be a finite-type morphism and denote by
V^{\prime},\tilde{W}^{\prime},\tilde{V}^{\prime}
and
by
f^{\prime},g^{\prime},a^{\prime},b^{\prime}
the base-changes along
W^{\prime}\to W
.
The refined Gysin morphism of
f
is a map
f^{!}:{\operatorname{CH}}_{m}(W^{\prime})\to{\operatorname{CH}}_{m}(\tilde{W}^{\prime})
.
Let
T\subset W^{\prime}
be an
m
-dimensional integral closed subscheme,
and denote by
[T]\in{\operatorname{CH}}_{m}(W^{\prime})
its cycle class.
Then by
[
Ful98
, Exa 6.7.1, Prop 17.5]

(7.11.1)

f^{!}[T]=[\tilde{T}]+b^{\prime}_{*}\left\{c(\mathcal{E}xc_{|\tilde{V}^{\prime}})\cap{g^{\prime}}^{*}s(T\cap V^{\prime},T)\right\}_{m}\quad\text{in }{\operatorname{CH}}_{m}(\tilde{W}^{\prime}),

where
\tilde{T}={\rm Bl}_{T\cap V^{\prime}}(T)\subset{\rm Bl}_{V^{\prime}}(W^{\prime})\subset\tilde{W}^{\prime}
is the blow-up of
T
in
T\cap V^{\prime}
,

c(\mathcal{E}xc_{|\tilde{V}^{\prime}})
is the total Chern class of the pullback of
\mathcal{E}xc
to
\tilde{V}^{\prime}
,
and
s(T\cap V^{\prime},T)
is the Segre class of
T\cap V^{\prime}
in
T
defined in
[
Ful98
, 4.2]
.

Theorem 7.12
.

Let
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
,
\mathcal{X}=(X,D)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}_{ls}
and
i:Z\hookrightarrow X
a smooth closed subscheme of codimension
j

intersecting
D
transversally. Set
\mathcal{Z}=(Z,D_{|Z})
.
Then we have the following equality of maps of sheaves on
X_{\operatorname{Nis}}

(7.12.1)

H^{j}(c_{Z})=g_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{X},Z}^{j}\circ i^{*}:\gamma^{j}F_{\mathcal{X}}\to i_{*}\gamma^{j}F_{\mathcal{Z}}\to R^{j}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}F_{\mathcal{X}},

where
c_{Z}
is the morphism (
5.8.1
) for
Z
viewed as cycle in
{\operatorname{CH}}^{j}_{Z}(X)
.
In particular, the following diagram commutes in
D(X_{\operatorname{Nis}})

\textstyle{\gamma^{j}F_{\mathcal{X}}[-j]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{*}}
\scriptstyle{c_{Z}}
\textstyle{R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}F_{\mathcal{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{i_{*}\gamma^{j}F_{\mathcal{Z}}[-j]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{X}}}
\textstyle{F_{\mathcal{X}}.}

In particular, if
X
admits a
k
-morphism
q:X\to Z
, such that
q\circ i={\operatorname{id}}_{Z}

(locally in the Nisnevich topology this is always possible, see Lemma
7.14
below),
then

(7.12.2)

g_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{X}}=c_{Z}\circ q^{*}:i_{*}\gamma^{j}F_{\mathcal{Z}}[-j]\to R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}F_{\mathcal{X}}\to F_{\mathcal{X}}.

Proof.

The equivalence of the two statements follows from
the isomorphism
R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}F_{\mathcal{X}}\cong R^{j}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}F_{\mathcal{X}}[-j]
(see
[
Sai20a
, Cor 8.6(3)]
)
and the definition of the local Gysin map, see Lemma
7.10
.
We show the equality (
7.12.1
). This is a local question and we can therefore assume
that the normal sheaf of
Z
in
X
is trivial, i.e.,

(7.12.3)

\mathcal{N}_{Z/X}\cong\mathcal{O}_{Z}^{\oplus j}.

Let the notation be as in
7.2
. Set
\xi=c_{1}(\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}(1))\in{\operatorname{CH}}^{1}(E_{1})
.
Note that the pullback
i_{E_{1}}^{*}:{\operatorname{CH}}^{j}_{E_{1}}(Y)\to{\operatorname{CH}}^{j}_{E_{1}}(E_{1})

is by definition (see Lemma
5.3
) equal to the refined Gysin map (see
[
Ful98
, 6.2]
)

i_{E_{1}}^{!}:{\operatorname{CH}}^{j-1}(E_{1})\to{\operatorname{CH}}^{j}(E_{1})

corresponding to the diagram

\textstyle{E_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{E_{1}}}
\textstyle{E_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{E_{1}}}
\textstyle{Y.}

The normal sheaf of the immersion
i_{E_{1}}
is

\mathcal{N}_{E_{1}/Y}=\mathcal{O}_{Y}(E_{1})_{|E_{1}}=\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}(-1).

Thus by the excess intersection formula (see
[
Ful98
, Cor 6.3]
) we find

(7.12.4)

-i_{E_{1}}^{!}(\xi^{j-1})=\xi^{j}.

Set
\eta:=\xi^{j-1}
viewed as an element in
{\operatorname{CH}}^{j}_{E_{1}}(Y)
.
Consider the following diagram

\textstyle{\gamma^{j}F_{\mathcal{X}}[-j]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{*}}
\scriptstyle{(\pi\rho_{1})^{*}}
\textstyle{R(\pi\rho_{1})_{*}\gamma^{j}F_{\mathcal{Y}}[-j]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c_{\eta}}
\scriptstyle{i_{E_{1}}^{*}}
\textstyle{R(\pi\rho_{1})_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{E_{1}}F_{\mathcal{Y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{-i_{E_{1}}^{*}}
\textstyle{i_{*}\gamma^{j}F_{Z}[-j]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda^{j}_{V}}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{E_{1}}^{*}}
\textstyle{i_{*}R\rho_{E_{1}*}\gamma^{j}F_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}[-j]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c_{\xi^{j}}}
\textstyle{i_{*}R\rho_{E_{1}*}F_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}.}

The right square commutes by (
7.12.4
) and Lemma
5.9
(3)
,
the left square clearly commutes, hence so does the big outer square.
Thus by Lemma
7.10

(7.12.5)

\rho^{*}\circ g^{j}_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{X},Z}\circ i^{*}=i_{{\tilde{X}},E_{1}}^{*}\circ H^{j}(c_{\eta})\circ(\pi\rho_{1})^{*}:i_{*}\gamma^{j}F_{\mathcal{X}}\to R^{j}\rho_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{E}F_{\tilde{\mathcal{X}}},

where
i_{{\tilde{X}},E_{1}}^{*}:=R^{j}(\pi\rho_{1})_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{E_{1}}(i_{\tilde{X}}^{*})
.

Set
Z_{1}:=\rho_{1}^{-1}(Z\times\mathbf{P}^{1})
.
We have
E_{1}\subset Z_{1}
and
i_{{\tilde{X}}}^{-1}(E_{1})=i_{\tilde{X}}^{-1}(Z_{1})=E
.
Denote by the same letter
\imath
the two enlarge-support maps

\imath:R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{E_{1}}\to R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z_{1}}
and
\imath:{\operatorname{CH}}^{j}_{E_{1}}(Y)\to{\operatorname{CH}}^{j}_{Z_{1}}(Y)
.
We also denote by
i_{{\tilde{X}},Z_{1}}^{*}
the two maps induced by
i_{\tilde{X}}^{*}

i_{{\tilde{X}},Z_{1}}^{*}\,\,:\,\,R^{j}(\pi\rho_{1})_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z_{1}}F_{\mathcal{Y}}\to R^{j}\rho_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{E}F_{\tilde{\mathcal{X}}},\qquad{\operatorname{CH}}^{j}_{Z_{1}}(Y)\to{\operatorname{CH}}^{j}_{E}(\tilde{X}).

Clearly we have in both cases

i_{{\tilde{X}},E_{1}}^{*}=i_{{\tilde{X}},Z_{1}}^{*}\circ\imath.

Thus (
7.12.5
) yields

(7.12.6)

\rho^{*}\circ g^{j}_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{X},Z}\circ i^{*}=i_{{\tilde{X}},Z_{1}}^{*}\circ H^{j}(c_{\imath(\eta)})\circ(\pi\rho_{1})^{*}:i_{*}\gamma^{j}F_{\mathcal{X}}\to R^{j}\rho_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{E}F_{\tilde{\mathcal{X}}}.

The strict transform of
Z\times\mathbf{P}^{1}\subset X\times\mathbf{P}^{1}
in
Y
is the blow-up of

Z\times\mathbf{P}^{1}
in the Cartier divisor
Z\times 0
and is therefore isomorphic to
Z\times\mathbf{P}^{1}
.
We obtain

Z_{1}=(Z\times\mathbf{P}^{1})\cup E_{1}

and
E_{1}\cap(Z\times\mathbf{P}^{1})=Z\times 0
is embedded as the zero section in the normal bundle
N_{E_{1}}Y
,
which is equal to
E_{1}\setminus E
. On the other hand,
\tilde{X}\subset Y
is the strict transform of
X\times 0
and
intersects
E_{1}
in
E
, see, e.g.,
[
Ful98
, 5.1]
. Thus in
Y
we have
\tilde{X}\cap(Z\times\mathbf{P}^{1})=\emptyset
.

Claim 7.12.1
.

We claim that the following equality holds in
{\operatorname{CH}}^{j}_{Z_{1}}(Y)

(\pi\rho_{1})^{!}[Z]=\imath(\eta)+[Z\times\mathbf{P}^{1}],

where

(\pi\rho_{1})^{!}:{\operatorname{CH}}_{d-j}(Z)\to{\operatorname{CH}}_{d+1-j}(Z_{1})

is the refined Gysin map corresponding to the cartesian diagram

\textstyle{Z_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi\rho_{1}}
\textstyle{X.}

Assuming Claim
7.12.1
, we can conclude as follows.
The composition

\gamma^{j}F_{\mathcal{Y}}[-j]\xrightarrow{c_{[Z\times\mathbf{P}^{1}]}}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z_{1}}F_{\mathcal{Y}}\xrightarrow{i_{\tilde{X}}^{*}}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{E}F_{\tilde{\mathcal{X}}}

factors via

R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z\times\mathbf{P}^{1}}F_{\mathcal{Y}}\xrightarrow{i_{\tilde{X}}^{*}}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{(Z\times\mathbf{P}^{1})\cap E}F_{\tilde{\mathcal{X}}}.

Since
\tilde{X}\cap(Z\times\mathbf{P}^{1})=\emptyset
by what was said after (
7.12.6
), we have

(7.12.7)

i_{{\tilde{X}},Z_{1}}^{*}\circ c_{[Z\times\mathbf{P}^{1}]}=0.

Thus we obtain the following equality of maps
i_{*}\gamma^{j}F_{\mathcal{X}}\to R^{j}\rho_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{E}F_{\tilde{\mathcal{X}}}

\displaystyle\rho^{*}\circ g^{j}_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{X},Z}\circ i^{*}

\displaystyle=i_{{\tilde{X}},Z_{1}}^{*}\circ H^{j}(c_{\imath(\eta)})\circ(\pi\rho_{1})^{*},

\displaystyle\text{by \eqref{prop:pf-gysin3}},

\displaystyle=i_{{\tilde{X}},Z_{1}}^{*}\circ H^{j}(c_{(\pi\rho_{1})^{*}Z-[Z\times\mathbf{P}^{1}]})\circ(\pi\rho_{1})^{*},

\displaystyle\text{by \ref{prop:pf-gysin4}},

\displaystyle=i_{{\tilde{X}},Z_{1}}^{*}(\pi\rho_{1})^{*}\circ H^{j}(c_{Z}),

\displaystyle\text{by \eqref{prop:pf-gysin5}, \ref{lem:cyclecup}},

\displaystyle=\rho^{*}\circ H^{j}(c_{Z}).

The statement follows since
\rho^{*}
here is an isomorphism, see (
7.10.2
).

Proof of Claim
7.12.1
.

First note

(\pi\rho_{1})^{!}[Z]=\rho_{1}^{!}[Z\times\mathbf{P}^{1}].

To compute this expression we apply the formula (
7.11.1
)
in the case where
a=i_{Z0}:Z\times 0\hookrightarrow X\times\mathbf{P}^{1}
,
f=\rho_{1}:Y\to X\times\mathbf{P}^{1}
,

(W^{\prime}\to W)=(Z\times\mathbf{P}^{1}\hookrightarrow X\times\mathbf{P}^{1})
and
T=Z\times\mathbf{P}^{1}
and
m=d+1-j
, where
d=\dim X
.
In particular, we have

\tilde{W}^{\prime}=Z_{1},\quad V^{\prime}=V=Z\times 0,\quad\tilde{V^{\prime}}=\tilde{V}=E_{1},

and
\tilde{T}=Z\times\mathbf{P}^{1}
.
Since the conormal bundle of
Z\times 0\hookrightarrow Z\times\mathbf{P}^{1}
is trivial,
[
Ful98
, Prop 4.1(a)]
yields

\rho_{E_{1}}^{*}s(Z\times 0,Z\times\mathbf{P}^{1})=[E_{1}].

By (
7.12.3
) we have
\mathcal{N}_{Z\times 0/X\times\mathbf{P}^{1}}=\mathcal{O}_{Z}^{\oplus j+1}
.
The Whitney formula yields

c(\mathcal{E}xc_{|E_{1}})=(1-\xi)^{-1}=1+\xi+\ldots+\xi^{j},

where
\xi=c_{1}(\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}(1))
.
Thus Claim
7.12.1
follows from (
7.11.1
).
∎

Corollary 7.13
.

Let
F
,
\mathcal{X}=(X,D)
,
i:Z\hookrightarrow X
, and
\mathcal{Z}
as
in Theorem
7.12
above. Assume
D=\emptyset
(thus
\mathcal{X}=X
and
\mathcal{Z}=Z
).
Let
\Phi
be a family of supports on
Z
and

\alpha\in{\operatorname{CH}}^{r}_{\Phi}(Z)
. Then

c_{i_{*}\alpha}=g_{Z/X}\circ c_{\alpha}\circ i^{*}:\\
\gamma^{j+r}F_{X}[-j-r]\to\gamma^{j+r}F_{Z}[-j-r]\to R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}\gamma^{j}F_{Z}[-j]\to R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}F_{X},

where we view
i_{*}\alpha\in{\operatorname{CH}}^{j+r}_{\Phi}(X)
.

Proof.

It suffices to consider
\alpha=[V]
, with
V\subset Z
irreducible and of codimension
r
, and
\Phi=\Phi_{V}
.
By
[
Sai20a
, Cor 8.6(1)]
we have
R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{V}F_{X}\cong\tau_{\geq j+r}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{V}F_{X}

(here we need
D=\emptyset
). Hence we have a natural map

R^{j+r}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{V}F_{X}[-j-r]\to R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{V}F_{X}
in the derived category
and the two maps in the statement are induced by composing this map
with the two morphisms of sheaves

(7.13.1)

H^{j+r}(c_{i_{*}V}),\,\,H^{j+r}(g_{Z/X}\circ c_{V}\circ i^{*}):\gamma^{j+r}F_{X}\to R^{j+r}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{V}F_{X}.

Thus it suffices to show that the two maps in (
7.13.1
) are equal.
By
[
Sai20a
, Cor 8.6(1)]
the restriction

R^{r+j}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{V}F_{X}\to\nu_{*}R^{r+j}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{V\setminus V_{\rm sing}}F_{X\setminus V_{\rm sing}}

is injective, where
\nu:X\setminus V_{\rm sing}\hookrightarrow X
is the open immersion.
Thus we may furthermore assume that
V
is smooth.
The question is local on
X
and we can therefore assume that there exists a closed subset

W\subset X
of pure codimension
r
such that
V=i^{*}W
in
{\operatorname{CH}}^{r}_{V}(Z)={\operatorname{CH}}^{0}(V)
.
In this situation we have the following equality of maps

\gamma^{j+r}F_{X}[-j-r]\to R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{V}F_{X}

g_{Z/X}\circ c_{V}\circ i^{*}=g_{Z/X}\circ c_{i^{*}W}\circ i^{*}=g_{Z/X}\circ i^{*}\circ c_{W}=c_{Z}\circ c_{W}=c_{Z\cdot W}=c_{i_{*}V},

where the second and forth equality hold by Lemma
5.9
and the third equality
by Theorem
7.12
. This implies the statement.
∎

Lemma 7.14
.

Let
S
be an affine scheme and
Z\hookrightarrow X
a closed immersion of affine
S
-schemes.
Assume that
X
is noetherian, integral and normal and
Z
is irreducible and formally smooth and of finite type over
S
.
Then there exists a Nisnevich neighborhood
u:X^{\prime}\to X
of
Z

(i.e.,
u
is étale and induces an isomorphism
u^{-1}(Z)\xrightarrow{\simeq}Z
)
which admits an
S
-morphism
X^{\prime}\to Z
, such that the composition

Z\cong u^{-1}(Z)\hookrightarrow X^{\prime}\to Z
is the identity.

Proof.

We follow an argument in the proof of
[
Sai20a
, Lem 8.5]
.
Write
S=\operatorname{Spec}R
,
X=\operatorname{Spec}A
and
Z=\operatorname{Spec}A/I
.
Set
Z_{n}:=\operatorname{Spec}A/I^{n}
and
\hat{X}_{Z}=\operatorname{Spec}\hat{A}_{I}
, where
\hat{A}_{I}=\varprojlim_{n}(A/I^{n})
.
Since
Z
is formally smooth over
S
,
we find a compatible system of
S
-morphisms
\{Z_{n}\to Z\}
which
reduce to the identity on
Z
; it induces a morphism of
S
-schemes

\hat{\pi}:\hat{X}_{Z}\to Z
of which the natural closed immersion
Z\hookrightarrow\hat{X}_{Z}
is a section.
We can form the closed immersion

\hat{\varepsilon}:={\operatorname{id}}_{\hat{X}_{Z}}\times\hat{\pi}:\hat{X}_{Z}\hookrightarrow\hat{X}_{Z}\times_{S}Z
which restricts to the diagonal on

Z\times_{S}Z
. By
[
Elk73
, Thm 2bis]
we find therefore an
X^{h}_{Z}
-morphism

\varepsilon^{h}:X_{Z}^{h}\hookrightarrow X_{Z}^{h}\times_{S}Z
which restricts to the diagonal on
Z\times_{S}Z
,
where
X^{h}_{Z}=\operatorname{Spec}A^{h}_{I}
is the henselization of the pair
(X,Z)
.
Composing
\varepsilon^{h}
with the projection to
Z
yields an
S
-morphism

u^{h}:X_{Z}^{h}\to Z
. Since
X
is normal and noetherian, so is any affine étale scheme
Y
over
X
;
in particular such a
Y
is a disjoint union of integral normal
X
-schemes.
Since
Z
is irreducible, any Nisnevich neighborhood
Y\to X
of
Z
can be refined to a Nisnevich neighborhood

Y^{\prime}\to X
of
Z
with
Y^{\prime}
integral. It follows that we can write

A^{h}_{I}=\varinjlim B
, where the limit is over all étale maps
A\to B
inducing an isomorphism
A/I\to B/IB

with
B
integral; the transition maps
B\to B^{\prime}
in this system are automatically étale and hence
(since
B
and
B^{\prime}
are integral) also injective; thus also
B\to A^{h}_{I}
is injective.
Since
A/I
is of finite type over
R
it follows that the
R
-algebra map
{u^{h}}^{*}:A/I\to A^{h}_{I}

factors via an
R
-algebra map
A/I\to B
for some
B
as above. This yields the statement.
∎

Corollary 7.15
.

Let
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
and
\mathcal{X}=(X,D)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}_{ls}
.
Let
i:Z\hookrightarrow Z^{\prime}\xhookrightarrow{i^{\prime}}X
be closed immersion of smooth schemes
of codimension
a={\operatorname{codim}}(Z,Z^{\prime})
and
b={\operatorname{codim}}(Z^{\prime},X)
,
such that
D
intersects
Z
and
Z^{\prime}
transversally. Set
Z=(Z,D_{|Z})
and
Z^{\prime}=(Z^{\prime},D_{|Z^{\prime}})
.
We have the following equality

R^{a}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}(g^{b}_{\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}/\mathcal{X},Z^{\prime}})\circ g^{a}_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{Z}^{\prime},Z}=g^{a+b}_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{X},Z}:i_{*}\gamma^{a+b}F_{\mathcal{Z}}\to R^{a+b}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{X}}.
