In particular, the following diagram commutes

\textstyle{i_{*}\gamma^{a+b}F_{\mathcal{Z}}[-a-b]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{X}}}
\scriptstyle{g_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}[-b]}
\textstyle{F_{\mathcal{X}}}
\textstyle{i^{\prime}_{*}\gamma^{b}F_{\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}[-b].\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}/\mathcal{X}}}

Proof.

The second statement follows from the first and Lemma
7.10
.
The first statement is local in
X
and we may therefore assume that we find a smooth
closed subscheme
Z^{\prime\prime}\subset X
of codimension
a
such that
Z=Z^{\prime}\times_{X}Z^{\prime\prime}
.
Since
i^{*}:\gamma^{a+b}F_{\mathcal{X}}\to i_{*}\gamma^{a+b}F_{\mathcal{Z}}

is surjective by Lemma
7.14
it suffices to show the equality after precomposition
with
i^{*}
. Consider the following diagram

\textstyle{\gamma^{a+b}F_{\mathcal{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{*}}
\scriptstyle{{i^{\prime}}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{c_{Z^{\prime\prime}}}
\textstyle{R^{a}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z^{\prime\prime}}\gamma^{b}F_{\mathcal{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{i^{\prime}}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{c_{Z^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{i^{\prime}_{*}\gamma^{a+b}F_{\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c_{Z}}
\textstyle{i^{\prime}_{*}R^{a}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}\gamma^{b}F_{\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g^{b}_{\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}/\mathcal{X},Z^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{R^{a+b}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}F_{\mathcal{X}}}
\textstyle{i_{*}\gamma^{a+b}F_{\mathcal{Z}},\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g^{a}_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{Z}^{\prime},Z}}

where the maps
c_{Z}
,
c_{Z^{\prime}}
, and
c_{Z^{\prime\prime}}
are defined viewing
Z
,
Z^{\prime}
, and
Z^{\prime\prime}

as cycles in
{\operatorname{CH}}^{a}_{Z}(Z^{\prime})
,
{\operatorname{CH}}^{b}_{Z^{\prime}}(X)
, and
{\operatorname{CH}}^{a}_{Z^{\prime\prime}}(X)
, respectively.
The square commutes by Lemma
5.9
(3)
and the triangles commute
by Theorem
7.12
.
By definition of the refined intersection product we have
Z^{\prime}\cdot Z^{\prime\prime}=Z
in
{\operatorname{CH}}^{a+b}_{Z}(X)
.
Thus the statement follows from Lemma
5.9
(4)
and Theorem
7.12
.
∎

Theorem 7.16
.

Let
\mathcal{X}=(X,D)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}_{ls}
and
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
.
Let
i:Z\hookrightarrow X
be a smooth closed subscheme of codimension
j
intersecting
D
transversally
and set
\mathcal{Z}=(Z,D_{|Z})
.
Then there is a canonical distinguished triangle in
D(X_{{\operatorname{Nis}}})

(7.16.1)

i_{*}\gamma^{j}F_{\mathcal{Z}}[-j]\xrightarrow{g_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{X}}}F_{\mathcal{X}}\xrightarrow{\rho^{*}}R\rho_{*}F_{(\tilde{X},D_{|\tilde{X}}+E)}\xrightarrow{\partial}i_{*}\gamma^{j}F_{\mathcal{Z}}[-j+1],

where
\rho:\tilde{X}\to X
is the blow-up of
X
along
Z
and
E=\rho^{-1}(Z)
.

Proof.

We first consider the case
j=1
.
In this case
\tilde{X}=X
and
Z=E
.
Denote by
j:U=X\setminus Z\hookrightarrow X
the open immersion and set
\mathcal{U}=(U,D_{|U})
and
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}:=(X,D+Z)
.
Consider the following diagram of solid arrows of sheaves on
X_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}

(7.16.2)

\textstyle{\gamma^{1}F_{\mathcal{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(
5.10.2
)
\scriptstyle{i^{*}}
\scriptstyle{c_{Z}}
\textstyle{F_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}/F_{\mathcal{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{j_{*}F_{\mathcal{U}}/F_{\mathcal{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{R^{1}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}F_{\mathcal{X}}.}
\textstyle{i_{*}\gamma^{1}F_{\mathcal{Z}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(*)}
\scriptstyle{g_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{X},Z}^{1}}

The diagram commutes by Lemma
5.10
and Theorem
7.12
, the vertical
arrow is surjective by Lemma
7.14
.

Claim 7.16.1
.

The dotted arrow
(*)
exists, makes the diagram commute, and is an isomorphism.
(It is automatically uniquely determined).

Indeed, the question is local around the points of
Z
. We may therefore assume
that we have an étale morphism
u:X\to S[t]
, such that
S=\operatorname{Spec}K\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\}
,
with a function field
K
,
D=u^{*}\operatorname{Div}(x_{1}^{r_{1}}\cdots x_{s}^{r_{s}})
, and
u
induces an isomorphism

Z\cong u^{-1}(t=0)\xrightarrow{\simeq}S
.
In particular, we have a morphism
q:X\to S
such that the composition

(7.16.3)

q\circ i:Z\xrightarrow{\simeq}S

is an isomorphism.
Thus the arrow
(*)
exists by (
7.12.2
) as the composition

i_{*}\gamma^{1}F_{\mathcal{Z}}\xrightarrow{q^{*}}\gamma^{1}F_{\mathcal{X}}\xrightarrow{\eqref{lem:divisor-cup1}}F_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}/F_{\mathcal{X}}.

By Lemma
5.10
the map
(*)

is induced by pullback along the composition

X\xrightarrow{\Delta_{X}}X\times X\xrightarrow{u^{*}(t)\times{\operatorname{id}}_{X}}\mathbf{A}^{1}\times X\xrightarrow{{\operatorname{id}}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}}\times q}\mathbf{A}^{1}\times S;

this composition is equal to
u
. Set
\mathcal{S}:=(S,\operatorname{Div}(x_{1}^{r_{1}}\cdots x_{s}^{r_{s}}))
.
Hence the map
(*)
is on
Z
equal to (cf. Remark
4.7
)

\frac{F((\mathbf{A}^{1},0)\otimes\mathcal{Z})}{F(\mathbf{A}^{1}\otimes\mathcal{Z})}\cong\frac{F((\mathbf{A}^{1},0)\otimes\mathcal{S})}{F(\mathbf{A}^{1}\otimes\mathcal{S})}\xrightarrow{u^{*}}\frac{F(X,D+Z)}{F(X,D)}.

It remains to show that
u^{*}
becomes an isomorphism if we replace
X
by a Nisnevich neighborhood around
the point
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n},t)
. By the usual trace argument we may assume that the field
K
is infinite.
By
[
Sai20a
, Lem 6.7]
we may therefore assume that
(X,Z)
is a
V
-pair over
S

(in the sense of
[
Sai20a
, Def 2.1]
). Clearly
(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S},0_{S})
is also a
V
-pair over
S

and (
7.16.3
) gives an identification
Z\cong 0_{S}
. Thus
u^{*}
is an isomorphism
by
[
Sai20a
, Cor 2.21]
. This proves Claim
7.16.1
.

We construct the triangle from the statement in the case
j=1
.
Set

\alpha:=(*)^{-1}:F_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}/F_{\mathcal{X}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}i_{*}\gamma^{1}F_{\mathcal{Z}}.

Denote by
r:F_{\mathcal{X}}\hookrightarrow F_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}
the inclusion. For exact triangles we adopt the sign
conventions from
[
Con00
, 1.3]
. Thus the boundary map
\operatorname{cone}(r)\to F_{\mathcal{X}}[1]

of the exact triangle determined by
r
, is given by
-{\operatorname{id}}_{F_{\mathcal{X}}}
in degree
-1
.
We define the boundary map
\partial
as the composition

\partial:F_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}\to F_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}/F_{\mathcal{X}}\xrightarrow{\alpha}i_{*}\gamma^{1}F_{\mathcal{Z}}

and we define a quasi-isomorphism
\varphi
as the composition

\varphi:\operatorname{cone}(r)\xrightarrow{{\rm qis}}F_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}/F_{\mathcal{X}}\xrightarrow{\alpha}i_{*}\gamma^{1}F_{\mathcal{Z}},

where the first map is induced by the quotient map in degree 0.
It remains to show that the following diagram is commutative in
D(X_{{\operatorname{Nis}}})

(7.16.4)

\textstyle{\operatorname{cone}(r)[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi[-1]}
\textstyle{F_{\mathcal{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{r}
\textstyle{F_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{cone}(r)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{i_{*}\gamma^{1}F_{\mathcal{Z}}[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{X}}}
\textstyle{F_{\mathcal{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{r}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial}
\textstyle{i_{*}\gamma^{1}F_{\mathcal{Z}}.}

By definition the square on the right commutes;
by Lemma
7.10
the square on the left is the big outer square of the following diagram

(7.16.5)

\textstyle{\operatorname{cone}(r)[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rm qis}
\textstyle{\operatorname{cone}(F_{\mathcal{X}}\to j_{*}F_{\mathcal{U}})[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rm qis}
\textstyle{F_{\mathcal{X}}}
\textstyle{F_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}/F_{\mathcal{X}}[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{j_{*}F_{\mathcal{U}}/F_{\mathcal{X}}[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{R^{1}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}F_{\mathcal{X}}[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{i_{*}\gamma^{1}F_{\mathcal{Z}}[-1],\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g^{1}_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{X},Z}}

where the vertical arrow on the top right is the composition

R^{1}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}F_{\mathcal{X}}[-1]\cong R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}F_{\mathcal{X}}\to F_{\mathcal{X}},

where the isomorphism comes from
[
Sai20a
, Cor 8.6(3)]
.
The lower triangle in (
7.16.5
) commutes by the definition of
\alpha
,
the left top square commutes by functoriality, the right top square commutes by the definitions of the involved maps.
Thus the square on the left in (
7.16.4
) commutes.
We have constructed the canonical distinguished Gysin triangle in codimension 1.

We consider the general case
j\geq 1
.
Let
\rho:\tilde{X}\to X
be the blow-up along
Z
and
E
the exceptional divisor.
Set
\tilde{\mathcal{X}}=(\tilde{X},D_{|\tilde{X}})
and
\tilde{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}=(\tilde{X},D_{|\tilde{X}}+E)

(note that
|E+D_{|\tilde{X}}|
is a SNCD),
moreover we use the notation from
7.2
.
Set

C:=\bigoplus_{r=1}^{j-1}i_{*}\gamma^{r}F_{\mathcal{Z}}[-r]

and consider the following diagram in
D(X_{\operatorname{Nis}})

(7.16.6)

\textstyle{i_{*}\gamma^{j}F_{\mathcal{Z}}[-j]\oplus C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{X}}\oplus(-{\operatorname{id}})}
\scriptstyle{\eqref{para:pbf1}[-1]}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{R\rho_{E*}\gamma^{1}F_{\mathcal{E}}[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{\mathcal{E}/\tilde{\mathcal{X}}}}
\textstyle{F_{\mathcal{X}}\oplus C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{*}+0}
(
7.3.1
)
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{R\rho_{*}F_{\tilde{\mathcal{X}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R\rho_{*}F_{\tilde{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial_{Z}\oplus 0}
\textstyle{R\rho_{*}F_{\tilde{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial_{E}}
\textstyle{i_{*}\gamma^{j}F_{\mathcal{Z}}[-j+1]\oplus C[1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(
6.1.1
)
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{R\rho_{E*}\gamma^{1}F_{\mathcal{E}},}

where the right column is
R\rho_{*}
applied to the Gysin triangle for
E\hookrightarrow\tilde{X}
stemming from the
codimension 1 case above
and the map
\partial_{Z}
is defined so that the lower square commutes.
This defines the triangle (
7.16.1
). Note that the right column is a distinguished triangle and
the left column is the direct sum of (
7.16.1
) and
C\xrightarrow{-{\operatorname{id}}}C\to 0\to C[1]
.
If the top square commutes, then
(
7.16.1
)
is therefore a distinguished triangle,
by
[
Nee01
, Prop 1.2.3]
.
Thus it remains to show:

Claim 7.16.2
.

The top square in diagram (
7.16.6
) commutes.

This is equivalent to the commutativity of the squares resulting from precomposition
with the canonical maps
i_{*}\gamma^{r}F_{\mathcal{Z}}[-r]\to i_{*}\gamma^{j}F_{\mathcal{Z}}[-j]\oplus C
, for
r=1,\ldots,j
.
We consider two cases.

1st case:
r=1,\ldots,j-1
.
In this case we have to show the commutativity of the following diagram

(7.16.7)

\textstyle{i_{*}\gamma^{r}F_{\mathcal{Z}}[-r]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda^{r-1}_{V}}
\scriptstyle{\lambda^{r}_{V}}
\textstyle{i_{*}R\rho_{*}F_{\mathcal{E}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{-\tau}
\textstyle{i_{*}R\rho_{*}\gamma^{1}F_{\mathcal{E}}[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{\mathcal{E}/\tilde{\mathcal{X}}}}
\textstyle{R\rho_{*}F_{\tilde{\mathcal{X}}},}

with
\tau
as in (
7.2.3
) and
V=\mathcal{N}_{Z/X}^{\vee}
. Note that the two compositions

-\tau\circ\lambda_{V}^{r}
and
g_{\mathcal{E}/\tilde{\mathcal{X}}}\circ\lambda_{V}^{r-1}
factor automatically
via the forget-support map
R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}R\rho_{*}F_{\tilde{\mathcal{X}}}\to R\rho_{*}F_{\tilde{\mathcal{X}}}
.
By applying
R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}
to the second isomorphism in (
7.3.1
) and using
the isomorphism
R^{j}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}F_{\mathcal{X}}[-j]\cong R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}F_{\mathcal{X}}
from
[
Sai20a
, Cor 8.6(3)]

we obtain

R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}R\rho_{*}F_{\tilde{\mathcal{X}}}\cong R^{j}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}F_{\mathcal{X}}[-j]\oplus i_{*}\tau_{\geq 1}R\rho_{E*}F_{\mathcal{E}}.

Since
\operatorname{Hom}_{D(X_{\operatorname{Nis}})}(i_{*}\gamma^{r}F_{\mathcal{Z}}[-r],R^{j}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}F_{\mathcal{X}}[-j])=0
for
r<j
,
we see that it suffices to show the equality (
7.16.7
) after composing with

(7.16.8)

\imath_{E}:R\rho_{*}F_{\tilde{\mathcal{X}}}\xrightarrow{i_{E}^{*}}i_{*}R\rho_{E*}F_{\mathcal{E}}\xrightarrow{\rm can.}i_{*}\tau_{\geq 1}R\rho_{E*}F_{\mathcal{E}}.

Since
\imath_{E}
is a section of
\tau
we are reduced to show

R\rho_{*}(i_{E}^{*}\circ g_{\mathcal{E}/\tilde{\mathcal{X}}})\circ\lambda^{r-1}_{V}=-\lambda^{r}_{V}:i_{*}\gamma^{r}F_{\mathcal{Z}}[-r]\to i_{*}R\rho_{E*}F_{\mathcal{E}}.

By the definition of
\lambda_{V}
(see (
6.1.1
)) and Lemma
5.9
(4)

it remains to check

(7.16.9)

i_{E}^{*}\circ g_{\mathcal{E}/\tilde{\mathcal{X}},E}=-c_{\xi}:\gamma^{1}F_{\mathcal{E}}[-1]\to F_{\mathcal{E}}.

which follows from Proposition
7.9
applied to the cartesian diagram

\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{=}
\scriptstyle{=}
\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{E}}
\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{E}}
\textstyle{\tilde{X}.}

2nd case:
r=j
.

In this case we have to check the commutativity of the square

(7.16.10)

\textstyle{i_{*}\gamma^{j}F_{\mathcal{Z}}[-j]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lambda_{V}^{j-1}}
\textstyle{R\rho_{E*}\gamma^{1}F_{\mathcal{E}}[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R\rho_{*}(g_{\mathcal{E}/\tilde{X}})}
\textstyle{F_{\mathcal{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{*}}
\textstyle{R\rho_{*}F_{\tilde{\mathcal{X}}}.}

By the the second isomorphism in (
7.3.1
) we have the vanishing
\imath_{E}\circ\rho^{*}=0
,
with
\imath_{E}
the map from (
7.16.8
); hence also
\imath_{E}\circ\rho^{*}\circ g_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{X}}=0
.
On the other hand, the vanishing

\imath_{E}\circ R\rho_{*}(g_{\mathcal{E}/\tilde{\mathcal{X}}})\circ\lambda^{j-1}_{V}=0

follows from (
7.16.9
),
Lemma
5.9
(4)
, and the vanishing
\xi^{j}=0
in
{\operatorname{CH}}^{j}(E)
,
which holds since
E
has relative dimension
j-1
over
Z
.
By Corollary
7.3
and
[
Sai20a
, Cor 8.6(3)]
we have

\displaystyle R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}R\rho_{*}F_{\tilde{\mathcal{X}}}

\displaystyle\cong R^{j}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}F_{\mathcal{X}}[-j]\oplus i_{*}\tau_{\geq 1}R\rho_{E*}F_{\mathcal{E}}

\displaystyle\cong R^{j-1}\rho_{*}R^{1}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{E}F_{\tilde{\mathcal{X}}}[-j]\oplus i_{*}\tau_{\geq 1}R\rho_{E*}F_{\mathcal{E}}.

Hence it suffices to show the commutativity of the diagram of sheaves

(7.16.11)

\textstyle{i_{*}\gamma^{j}F_{\mathcal{Z}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{X},Z}^{j}}
\scriptstyle{H^{j-1}(\lambda_{V})}
\textstyle{R^{j-1}\rho_{E*}(\gamma^{1}F_{\mathcal{E}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{\mathcal{E}/\tilde{\mathcal{X}},E}^{1}}
\textstyle{R^{j}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}F_{\mathcal{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{*}}
\textstyle{R^{j-1}\rho_{*}R^{1}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{E}F_{\tilde{\mathcal{X}}}.}

This is a local question and we may therefore assume that
\mathcal{N}_{Z/X}=\mathcal{O}_{Z}^{\oplus j}
.
Thus we are back at showing the commutativity of (
7.16.10
),
under the additional assumption
\mathcal{N}_{Z/X}=\mathcal{O}_{Z}^{\oplus j}
.
Hence the statement follows from Proposition
7.9

by observing that the excess normal sheaf in question is in this case is equal to
(see
[
Ful98
, 6.7]
)

\mathcal{E}xc=\rho_{E}^{*}\mathcal{N}_{Z/X}/\mathcal{O}_{E}(-1)=\mathcal{O}_{E}^{\oplus j}/\mathcal{O}_{E}(-1)

and that the Whitney sum formula in this case yields

c_{j-1}(\mathcal{E}xc)\cap E=\xi^{j-1}.

This shows the commutativity in the second case
r=j
and hence completes the proof
of Claim
7.16.2
and the theorem.
∎

Remark 7.17
.

The reader should compare Theorem
7.16
with the classical Gysin triangle in the
\mathbf{A}^{1}
-motivic setting. Recall that for
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
and
i\colon Z\hookrightarrow X
a smooth closed subscheme of codimension
i
, there is a distinguished triangle in
\mathbf{DM}_{gm}(k)
, called the Gysin triangle (see e.g.
[
Dég12
, 2.20]
),

(7.17.1)

M(X-Z)\xrightarrow{j_{*}}M(X)\xrightarrow{i^{*}}M(Z)(i)[2i]\xrightarrow{\partial_{X,Z}}M(X-Z)[1]

which gives, after applying any realisation functor
H^{*,*}(-)
the localisation long exact sequence

(7.17.2)

\cdots\to H^{n-2i,j-i}(Z)\xrightarrow{i_{*}}H^{n,j}(X)\xrightarrow{j^{*}}H^{n,j}(X-Z)\xrightarrow{\partial_{X,Z}}H^{n+1-2i,j-i}(Z)\to\cdots.

The most significant difference between our formulation, even when
D=\emptyset
, and the formulation in the
\mathbf{A}^{1}
-setting is that the cohomology of the open complement
U=X-Z
of
Z
in
X
, which appears in (
7.17.2
) and (
7.17.1
), is replaced by the cohomology of the pair
(\tilde{X},E)
, where
\tilde{X}
is the blow-up of
Z
in
X
, and
E
is the exceptional divisor. In the modulus setting, where smooth schemes get replaced by “compactifications”
\mathcal{X}=(X,D)
, we need then to “compactify”
(X-Z)
without changing its “homotopy type”, and the pair
(\tilde{X},E)
does the job. For reduced modulus the formula in Theorem
7.16
is also witnessed in the logarithmic setting, see
[
BPØ
, 7.5]
.

8.
Pushforward

In this section we construct a pushforward for
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
along projective morphisms by
using the projective bundle formula from section
6
and the Gysin map from section
7
.
This is a classical approach which can be found,
e.g., in
[
Har66
, III]
(for coherent sheaves) and the dual version in
[
Ful98
, 6.]
(Chow groups) and

[
Dég08
, 5.]
(motives).
In fact we construct, the pushforward with proper support along quasi-projective morphisms,
which for the Kähler (resp. the de Rham-Witt) differentials was done in
[
CR11
]
(resp.
[
CR12
]
).

Definition 8.1
.

We say a family of supports
\Phi
on an
S
-scheme
X
is a
family of proper supports for
X/S
,
if
\Phi
consists of closed subsets in
X
, which are proper over
S
.

Definition 8.2
.

Let
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
and
\mathcal{X}=(X,D)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}_{ls}
. Let
V
be a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module of rank
n+1

and denote by
\pi:P=\mathbf{P}(V)\to X
the projection and set
\mathcal{P}=(P,\pi^{*}D)
.
Let
j:U\hookrightarrow P
be an open immersion and denote by
\pi_{U}:U\to X
the restriction of
\pi

and set
\mathcal{U}=(U,\pi_{U}^{*}D)
.
Let
\Phi
be a family of proper supports for
U/X
and let
\Psi
be a family of supports on
X

satisfying
\Phi\subset\pi^{-1}_{U}\Psi
.
We define the morphism in
D(X_{\operatorname{Nis}})

(8.2.1)

{\operatorname{tr}}_{(\mathcal{U},\Phi)/(\mathcal{X},\Psi)}:R\pi_{U*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}F_{\mathcal{U}}\to R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Psi}(\gamma^{n}F)_{\mathcal{X}}[-n]

as the composition

R\pi_{U*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}F_{\mathcal{U}}\cong R\pi_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}F_{\mathcal{P}}\xrightarrow{\text{enlarge supp}}R\pi_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\pi^{-1}\Psi}F_{\mathcal{P}}\\
\xrightarrow[\simeq]{\eqref{para:pbf1}^{-1}}\bigoplus_{i=0}^{n}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Psi}(\gamma^{i}F)_{\mathcal{X}}[-i]\xrightarrow{\text{proj.}}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Psi}(\gamma^{n}F)_{\mathcal{X}}[-n],

where the first isomorphism is induced from the excision isomorphism

Rj_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}F_{\mathcal{U}}=R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}Rj_{*}j^{-1}F_{\mathcal{P}}=R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}F_{\mathcal{P}}

stemming from the fact that
\Phi
is by assumption also a family of supports on
P
.
If it is clear from the context which families of support we take, we also write
{\operatorname{tr}}_{\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{X}}
instead of

{\operatorname{tr}}_{(\mathcal{U},\Phi)/(\mathcal{X},\Psi)}
.
In particular, we write (see
5.1
for notation)

{\operatorname{tr}}_{\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{X}}:={\operatorname{tr}}_{(\mathcal{P},\Phi_{P})/(\mathcal{X},\Phi_{X})}:R\pi_{*}F_{\mathcal{P}}\to(\gamma^{n}F)_{\mathcal{X}}[-n]

which is simply the projection to the
n
-th component of the inverse of the projective bundle formula (
6.1.1
).

Lemma 8.3
.

Assumptions and notations as in Definition
8.2
.

(1)

Let
\imath:R\pi_{U*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}F_{\mathcal{U}}\to R\pi_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\pi^{-1}\Psi}F_{\mathcal{P}}=R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Psi}R\pi_{*}F_{\mathcal{P}}

be the natural map (excision composed with enlarge-supports).
Then

{\operatorname{tr}}_{(\mathcal{U},\Phi)/(\mathcal{X},\Psi)}=R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Psi}({\operatorname{tr}}_{\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{X}})\circ\imath.

(2)

Let
f:Y\to X
be a morphism in
\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
such that
\mathcal{Y}:=(Y,f^{*}D)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}_{ls}
.
We obtain a diagram

\textstyle{U_{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{U}}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{U_{Y}}}
\textstyle{P_{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{Y}}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{U\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{P\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{X,}

in which the two squares are cartesian.
Set
\mathcal{U}_{Y}=(U_{Y},\pi_{Y}^{*}f^{*}D)
. The following diagram commutes

\textstyle{Rf_{*}R\pi_{U_{Y}*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{f^{-1}_{U}\Phi}F_{\mathcal{U}_{Y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{tr}}_{\mathcal{U}_{Y}/\mathcal{Y}}}
\textstyle{Rf_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{f^{-1}\Psi}(\gamma^{n}F)_{\mathcal{Y}}[-n]}
\textstyle{R\pi_{U*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}F_{\mathcal{U}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{tr}}_{\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{X}}}
\scriptstyle{f_{U}^{*}}
\textstyle{R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Psi}(\gamma^{n}F)_{\mathcal{X}}[-n].\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{*}}

(3)

Let
s:X\hookrightarrow P
be a section of
\pi
which is contained in
U
, i.e.,
s(X)\subset U
.
Then
s(X)
defines a proper family of supports for
U/X
and the following diagram commutes

\textstyle{R\pi_{U*}s_{*}\gamma^{n}F_{\mathcal{X}}[-n]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{U},s(X)}}
\textstyle{R\pi_{U*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{s(X)}F_{\mathcal{U}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{tr}}_{(\mathcal{U},s(X))/\mathcal{X}}}
\textstyle{\gamma^{n}F_{\mathcal{X}}[-n],}

where
g_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{U},s(X)}=R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{s(X)}(g_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{U}}):s_{*}\gamma^{n}F_{\mathcal{X}}[-n]\to R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{s(X)}F_{\mathcal{U}}

is induced by the Gysin map (
7.4.1
).

(4)

Let
V^{\prime}
be another locally free
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module of rank
n^{\prime}+1
and let
\pi^{\prime}:P^{\prime}:=\mathbf{P}(V^{\prime})\to X
be the projection.
Let
U^{\prime}\subset P^{\prime}
be open and
\Phi^{\prime}
be a family of proper supports for
U^{\prime}/X
.
Denote by
\pi^{\prime}_{U^{\prime}}
the restriction of
\pi^{\prime}
to
U^{\prime}
and set
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}:=(U^{\prime},{\pi^{\prime}_{U^{\prime}}}^{*}D)
.
Then
\Xi:=\Phi\times_{X}\Phi^{\prime}
is a proper family of supports for

U\times_{X}U^{\prime}/U^{\prime}
and for
U\times_{X}U^{\prime}/U
and the following
diagram commutes

\textstyle{R(\pi_{U}\times_{X}\pi^{\prime}_{U^{\prime}})_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Xi}(F_{\mathcal{U}\otimes_{\mathcal{X}}\mathcal{U}^{\prime}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{tr}}_{(\mathcal{U}\otimes_{\mathcal{X}}\mathcal{U}^{\prime},\Xi)/(\mathcal{U},\Phi)}}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{tr}}_{(\mathcal{U}\otimes_{\mathcal{X}}{\mathcal{U}^{\prime}},\Xi)/(\mathcal{U}^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime})}}
\textstyle{R\pi_{U*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}(\gamma^{n^{\prime}}F)_{\mathcal{U}}[-n^{\prime}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{tr}}_{(\mathcal{U},\Phi)/(\mathcal{X},\Psi)}}
\textstyle{R\pi^{\prime}_{U^{\prime}*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi^{\prime}}(\gamma^{n}F)_{\mathcal{U}^{\prime}}[-n]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{tr}}_{(\mathcal{U}^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime})/(\mathcal{X},\Psi)}}
\textstyle{R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Psi}(\gamma^{n+n^{\prime}}F)_{\mathcal{X}}[-(n+n^{\prime})],}

where
\mathcal{U}\otimes_{\mathcal{X}}\mathcal{U}^{\prime}=(U\times_{X}U^{\prime},(\pi_{U}\times_{X}\pi^{\prime}_{U^{\prime}})^{*}D)
.

(5)

Let
i:Z\hookrightarrow X
be a smooth closed subscheme of codimension
c

intersecting
D
transversally and set
\mathcal{Z}:=(Z,i^{*}D)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}_{ls}
.
We obtain the diagram

\textstyle{U_{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{U}}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{U_{Z}}}
\textstyle{P_{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{Z}}
\textstyle{Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\textstyle{U\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{P\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{X,}

in which the two squares are cartesian. Set
\mathcal{U}_{Z}:=(U_{Z},\pi_{U_{Z}}^{*}i^{*}D)
.
Then the following square commutes

\textstyle{R\pi_{U*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}i_{U*}(\gamma^{c}F)_{\mathcal{U}_{Z}}[-c]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{\mathcal{U}_{Z}/\mathcal{U}}}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{tr}}_{\mathcal{U}_{Z}/\mathcal{Z}}}
\textstyle{R\pi_{U*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}F_{\mathcal{U}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{tr}}_{\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{X}}}
\textstyle{R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Psi}i_{*}(\gamma^{c+n}F)_{\mathcal{Z}}[-c-n]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{X}}}
\textstyle{R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Psi}(\gamma^{n}F)_{\mathcal{X}}[-n],}

where
g_{\mathcal{U}_{Z}/\mathcal{U}}
and
g_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{X}}
are the Gysin maps.

(6)

Let
i:Z\hookrightarrow X
and
\mathcal{Z}
be as in
(5)
above
and assume that
i
factors as
Z\hookrightarrow U\xrightarrow{\pi_{U}}X
, such that
\Psi\cap Z=\Phi\cap Z
.
Then
Z\hookrightarrow U
is a closed immersion of codimension
c+n
and then following square commutes

\textstyle{R\pi_{U*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}F_{\mathcal{U}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{tr}}_{\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{X}}}
\textstyle{R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Psi}i_{*}\gamma^{n+c}F_{\mathcal{Z}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces[-c-n]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{U}}}
\scriptstyle{g_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{X}}}
\textstyle{R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Psi}\gamma^{n}F_{\mathcal{X}}[-n].}

Proof.

(1)
holds by definition. For
(2)
first observe that

f_{U}^{-1}\Phi
is a family of proper supports
for
U_{Y}/Y
; by
(1)
we are reduced to show

f^{*}\circ{\operatorname{tr}}_{\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{X}}={\operatorname{tr}}_{\mathcal{P}_{Y}/\mathcal{Y}}\circ f_{P}^{*}.

By definition and with the notation from
6.1
this follows from the equality

(8.3.1)

c_{\xi_{Y}^{i}}\circ\pi_{Y}^{*}\circ f^{*}=f_{P}^{*}\circ c_{\xi^{i}}\circ\pi^{*},\quad i=0,\ldots,n,

where
\xi_{Y}=c_{1}(\mathcal{O}_{P_{Y}}(1))\in{\operatorname{CH}}^{1}(P_{Y})
and
\xi=c_{1}(\mathcal{O}_{P}(1))\in{\operatorname{CH}}^{1}(P)
. Since

f_{P}^{*}\xi=\xi_{Y}
the equality (
8.3.1
) follows from Lemma
5.9
(3)
.
For
(3)
it suffices to show

(8.3.2)

{\operatorname{tr}}_{\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{X}}\circ g_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{P}}={\operatorname{id}}_{(\gamma^{n}F)_{\mathcal{X}}[-n]}.

Indeed this follows from the equality
R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{s(X)}F_{\mathcal{U}}=R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{s(X)}F_{\mathcal{P}}
,
the compatibility of the Gysin with restriction along open immersions (see Proposition
7.9
),
and from
(1)
. By (
7.12.2
) we have

g_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{P}}=c_{s(X)}\circ\pi^{*}
, where we view
s(X)\in{\operatorname{CH}}^{n}(P)
.
The projective bundle formula yields

s(X)=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\pi^{*}(\alpha_{i})\cdot\xi^{i},\quad\text{for certain }\alpha_{i}\in{\operatorname{CH}}^{n-i}(X).

Applying
\pi_{*}
we obtain
\alpha_{n}=X
from
[
Ful98
, Exa 3.3.3]
and the fact that
s
is a section of
\pi
.
By Lemma
5.9
(3)
,
(4)
we obtain (with the notation from
6.1
)

g_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{P}}=\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}c_{\xi^{i}}\circ\pi^{*}\circ c_{\alpha_{i}}+c_{\xi^{n}}\circ\pi^{*}=\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\lambda_{V}^{i}\circ c_{\alpha_{i}}+\lambda^{n}_{V}.

Thus equality (
8.3.2
) follows directly from the definition of
{\operatorname{tr}}_{\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{X}}
.
Next
(4)
. Note that
\Xi
is by definition the smallest family of supports
on
U\times_{X}U^{\prime}
containing all closed subsets of the form
Z\times_{X}Z^{\prime}
with
Z\in\Phi
and
Z^{\prime}\in\Phi^{\prime}
.
Thus
\Xi
is clearly a family of proper supports over
U
and
U^{\prime}
, respectively,
and we have
\Xi\subset(\pi_{U}\times_{X}\pi^{\prime}_{U^{\prime}})^{-1}(\Psi)
. Using
(1)
it is easy to see
that the commutativity of the square in
(4)
is implied by the commutativity of the following diagram

(8.3.3)

\textstyle{R(\pi\times_{X}\pi^{\prime})_{*}F_{\mathcal{P}\otimes_{\mathcal{X}}\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{tr}}_{\mathcal{P}\otimes_{\mathcal{X}}\mathcal{P}^{\prime}/\mathcal{P}}}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{tr}}_{\mathcal{P}\otimes_{\mathcal{X}}\mathcal{P}^{\prime}/\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{R\pi_{*}(\gamma^{n^{\prime}}F)_{\mathcal{P}}[-n^{\prime}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{tr}}_{\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{X}}}
\textstyle{R\pi^{\prime}_{*}(\gamma^{n}F)_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}[-n]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{tr}}_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}/\mathcal{X}}}
\textstyle{(\gamma^{n+n^{\prime}}F)_{\mathcal{X}}[-(n+n^{\prime})].}

Let
\xi=c_{1}(\mathcal{O}_{P}(1))\in{\operatorname{CH}}^{1}(P)
and
\eta=c_{1}(\mathcal{O}_{P^{\prime}}(1))\in{\operatorname{CH}}^{1}(P^{\prime})
.
Denote by
p:P\times_{X}P^{\prime}\to P
and
q:P\times_{X}P^{\prime}\to P^{\prime}
the projections.
With the notation from
6.1
we have for
i,j=0,\ldots,n
,

\displaystyle\lambda_{\pi^{*}V^{\prime}}^{j}\circ\lambda_{V}^{i}

\displaystyle=c_{q^{*}\eta^{j}}\circ p^{*}\circ c_{\xi^{i}}\circ\pi^{*},

\displaystyle\text{by defn},

\displaystyle=c_{q^{*}\eta^{j}}\circ c_{p^{*}\xi^{i}}\circ p^{*}\pi^{*},

\displaystyle\text{by \ref{lem:cyclecup}\ref{lem:cyclecup2}},

\displaystyle=c_{(q^{*}\eta^{j})\cdot(p^{*}\xi^{i})}\circ q^{*}{\pi^{\prime}}^{*},

\displaystyle\text{by \ref{lem:cyclecup}\ref{lem:cyclecup3}},

\displaystyle=c_{p^{*}\xi^{i}}\circ c_{q^{*}\eta^{j}}\circ q^{*}{\pi^{\prime}}^{*},

\displaystyle\text{by \ref{lem:cyclecup}\ref{lem:cyclecup3}},

\displaystyle=c_{p^{*}\xi^{i}}\circ q^{*}\circ c_{\eta^{j}}\circ{\pi^{\prime}}^{*},

\displaystyle\text{by \ref{lem:cyclecup}\ref{lem:cyclecup2}},

\displaystyle=\lambda_{{\pi^{\prime}}^{*}V}^{i}\circ\lambda_{V^{\prime}}^{j},

by defn.

Now the commutativity of the diagram (
8.3.3
) follows from this and the definition of
{\operatorname{tr}}
.
For
(5)
it suffices as above to show that the following diagram commutes

\textstyle{R\pi_{*}i_{P*}(\gamma^{c}F)_{\mathcal{P}_{Z}}[-c]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{\mathcal{P}_{Z}/\mathcal{P}}}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{tr}}_{\mathcal{P}_{Z}/\mathcal{Z}}}
\textstyle{R\pi_{*}F_{\mathcal{P}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{tr}}_{\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{X}}}
\textstyle{(\gamma^{c+n}F)_{\mathcal{Z}}[-c-n]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{X}}}
\textstyle{(\gamma^{n}F)_{\mathcal{X}}[-n].}

By definition of
{\operatorname{tr}}
it suffices to show for all
j=0,\ldots,n

g_{\mathcal{P}_{Z}/\mathcal{P}}\circ\lambda^{j}_{i^{*}V}=\lambda^{j}_{V}\circ g_{\mathcal{Z}/\mathcal{X}}:(\gamma^{c+j}F)_{\mathcal{Z}}[-c-j]\to R\pi_{*}F_{\mathcal{P}}.

Since
\lambda_{V}^{j}=c_{\xi^{j}}\circ\pi^{*}
and
\lambda_{i^{*}V}^{j}=c_{i_{P}^{*}\xi^{j}}\circ\pi_{Z}^{*}

the above equality follows from the Propositions
7.8
and
7.9
.
Finally
(6)
.
By considering the diagram

\textstyle{Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{U\times_{X}Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{U\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{U}}
\textstyle{Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\textstyle{X}

with cartesian square, we see that the statement follows from
(5)
,
(3)
, and
the functoriality of the Gysin map, see Corollary
7.15
.
∎

8.4
.

Recall from
[
TT90
, Exa 2.1.2(d) and Lem 2.1.3]
that a morphism
f:Y\to X
in
\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
is quasi-projective in
the sense of
[
Gro61
, Def (5.3.1)]
if and only if there is a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module of finite rank
V
such that

f
factors as an immersion
Y\hookrightarrow\mathbf{P}(V)
followed by the projection
\mathbf{P}(V)\to X
.

We say such a morphism
f
has
relative dimension
r
, if
r=\dim Y_{i}-\dim X_{j}
is constant,
for
Y_{i}
ranging through the connected components of
Y
mapping to the connected component
X_{j}
of
X
.

Definition 8.5
.

Let
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
.
Let
\mathcal{X}=(X,D)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}_{ls}
and let
f:Y\to X
be a quasi-projective morphism in
\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}

of relative dimension
r\in\mathbb{Z}
, which is transversal to
D
(see Definition
2.11
).
Let
\Phi
be a family of proper supports for
Y/X
and let
\Psi
be a family of supports on

X
such that
\Phi\subset f^{-1}\Psi
.
Choose a factorization

(8.5.1)

f:Y\xrightarrow{i}U\xrightarrow{\pi}X,

where
i
is a closed immersion of codimension
c
and
\pi
is the composition
of an open immersion into a projective bundle over
X
,
U\hookrightarrow P
, followed by the projection
P\to X
.
Let
n
be the relative dimension of
\pi
, so that
r=n-c
.
Set
\mathcal{Y}:=(Y,f^{*}D)
and
\mathcal{U}=(U,\pi_{U}^{*}D)
.

For
e\geq c={\operatorname{codim}}(Y,U)
we define the map

(8.5.2)

(i,\pi)^{e}_{*}:Rf_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}\gamma^{e}F_{\mathcal{Y}}[-e]\to R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Psi}\gamma^{e+r}F_{\mathcal{X}}[-e-r]

as the following composition:

Rf_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}\gamma^{e}F_{\mathcal{Y}}[-e]\xrightarrow{g_{\mathcal{Y}/\mathcal{U}}}R\pi_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}\gamma^{e-c}F_{\mathcal{U}}[-e+c]\xrightarrow{{\operatorname{tr}}_{\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{X}}}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Psi}\gamma^{e+r}F_{\mathcal{X}}[-e-r].

Proposition 8.6
.

Assumptions as in Definition
8.5
.

(1)

Let
Y\xrightarrow{i^{\prime}}U^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\pi^{\prime}}X
be another factorization as in (
8.5.1
).
Then

(i,\pi)^{e}_{*}=(i^{\prime},\pi^{\prime})^{e}_{*},\quad\text{for all }e\geq{\operatorname{codim}}(Y,U\times_{X}U^{\prime}).

(2)

Let
g:Z\to Y
be a quasi-projective morphism in
\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
of relative dimension
s
, which is transversal to
f^{*}D
.
Let
\Xi
be a family of proper supports for
Z/Y
such that
\Xi\subset g^{-1}\Phi
.
Let

be a factorization of

as in (
8.5.1
) with
{\operatorname{codim}}(Z,U^{\prime})=c^{\prime}
.
Set
U^{\prime}_{Y}=U^{\prime}\times_{X}Y
and

and

\pi^{\prime}_{Y}:=p_{Y}:U^{\prime}_{Y}\to Y
. Set
\mathcal{Z}:=(Z,g^{*}f^{*}D)
etc.

Then we have
{\operatorname{codim}}(Z,U^{\prime}_{Y})=c^{\prime}+r
,

\Xi
is also a proper family with supports for
Z/X

and for

we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{R(fg)_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Xi}\gamma^{e}F_{\mathcal{Z}}[-e]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(i^{\prime},\pi^{\prime})^{e}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{(i^{\prime}_{Y},\pi^{\prime}_{Y})^{e}_{*}}
\textstyle{R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Psi}\gamma^{e+r+s}F_{\mathcal{X}}[-e-r-s]}
\textstyle{Rf_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}\gamma^{e+s}F_{\mathcal{Y}}[-e-s].\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(i,\pi)^{e+s}_{*}}

Proof.

(1)
.
We obtain the following diagram in which
ST=S\times_{X}T
and all maps are the obvious ones

\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{YU^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\scriptstyle{i^{\prime}}

\textstyle{X.}

We form the modulus pairs

.
We can view

\textstyle{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}\gamma^{e}_{\mathcal{Y},\Phi}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

. We have the commutative diagram

,

8.7
.

Recall from
1.6
that the functor
\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}:{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}\to\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
is right adjoint to
\underline{\omega}_{!}
.
Let
F\in{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
and set
{\widetilde{F}}:=\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F
.
By the weak cancellation theorem
[
MS
, Cor 3.6]
the natural map from
4.8

(8.7.1)

\kappa_{e}:{\widetilde{F}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\gamma^{e}({\widetilde{F}}(e)),\quad e\geq 0.

is an isomorphism.

Let
\mathcal{X}
,
f:Y\to X
and
\Phi,\Psi
be as in Definition
8.5
and assume the relative dimension of
f

is
r=0
.
We define

f_{*}:=\kappa_{e}^{-1}\circ(i,\pi)^{e}_{*}\circ\kappa_{e}:Rf_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}{\widetilde{F}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\to R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Psi}{\widetilde{F}}_{\mathcal{X}},

where
e\gg 0
. It follows from Proposition
8.6
, that
f_{*}
is independent of the choice of a factorization
(
8.5.2
) and it follows from the commutativity of (
4.8.3
) that it is independent of the choice of
e
.

Proposition 8.8
.

Let
F\in{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
and set
{\widetilde{F}}:=\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
.
Let
\mathcal{X}=(X,D)
,
f:Y\to X
,
\mathcal{Y}
,
\Psi
and
\Phi
be as in
8.5
above and
assume that
f
is of relative dimension
r=0
.

(1)

Let
g:Z\to Y
be a quasi-projective morphism of relative dimension
0
in
\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
and
assume that
g
is transversal
f^{*}D
. Set
\mathcal{Z}:=(Z,g^{*}f^{*}D)
.
Let
\Xi
be a family of proper supports
for
Z/Y
such that
\Xi\subset g^{-1}\Phi
. Then
\Xi
is also a family of proper supports for

Z/X
and we have

(f\circ g)_{*}=f_{*}g_{*}:Rg_{*}Rf_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Xi}{\widetilde{F}}_{\mathcal{Z}}\to Rf_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}{\widetilde{F}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\to R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Psi}{\widetilde{F}}_{\mathcal{X}}.

(2)

Assume
X
and
Y
are connected and
\Phi=f^{-1}\Psi
. Then

\deg(Y/X)\cdot=f_{*}\circ f^{*}:R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Psi}{\widetilde{F}}_{\mathcal{X}}\to Rf_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}{\widetilde{F}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\to R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Psi}{\widetilde{F}}_{\mathcal{X}},

where

\deg(Y/X):=\begin{cases}[k(Y):k(X)]&\text{if $f$ is dominant}\\
0&\text{else.}\end{cases}

(3)

Assume
X
and
Y
are connected and
f
is proper and its restriction

f_{|Y\setminus|f^{*}D|}:Y\setminus|f^{*}D|\to X\setminus|D|
is finite and surjective. Then

H^{0}(f_{*})=(\Gamma^{t}_{f})^{*}:f_{*}{\widetilde{F}}_{\mathcal{Y}}\to{\widetilde{F}}_{\mathcal{X}},

where
\Gamma^{t}_{f}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y})
is the transpose of the graph of
f
.

Proof.

(1)
. This follows from Proposition
8.6
(2)
.

(2)
. Choose a factorization (
8.5.1
) and
e\geq c=n
, then
f_{*}\circ f^{*}

is by Theorem
7.12
equal to the composition

\displaystyle R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Psi}{\widetilde{F}}_{\mathcal{X}}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\kappa_{e}}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Psi}\gamma^{e}({\widetilde{F}}(e))_{\mathcal{X}}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\pi_{U}^{*}}R\pi_{U*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}\gamma^{e}({\widetilde{F}}(e))_{\mathcal{U}}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{c_{Y}}R\pi_{U*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}\gamma^{e-n}({\widetilde{F}}(e))_{\mathcal{U}}[n]

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{{\operatorname{tr}}_{\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{X}}}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Psi}\gamma^{e}({\widetilde{F}}(e))_{\mathcal{U}}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[\simeq]{\kappa_{e}^{-1}}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Psi}{\widetilde{F}}_{\mathcal{X}}.

Let
\overline{Y}\subset P
be the closure of
Y
; it induces a cycle in
{\operatorname{CH}}^{n}(P)
.
It remains to show:

Claim 8.8.1
.

For
G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
the composition

\gamma^{n}G_{\mathcal{X}}\xrightarrow{\pi^{*}}R\pi_{*}\gamma^{n}G_{\mathcal{P}}\xrightarrow{c_{\overline{Y}}}R\pi_{*}G_{\mathcal{P}}[n]\xrightarrow{{\operatorname{tr}}_{\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{X}}}\gamma^{n}G_{\mathcal{X}},

is equal to the multiplication with
\deg(Y/X)
.

To this end, let
\xi=c_{1}(\mathcal{O}_{P}(1))\in{\operatorname{CH}}^{1}(P)
.
By the projective bundle formula there exist cycles
\alpha_{i}\in{\operatorname{CH}}^{n-i}(X)
, such that

\overline{Y}=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\pi^{*}\alpha_{i}\cdot\xi^{i},\quad\text{in }{\operatorname{CH}}^{n}(P).

Applying
\pi_{*}:{\operatorname{CH}}^{n}(P)\to{\operatorname{CH}}^{0}(X)=\mathbb{Z}
we find

\deg(Y/X)=\alpha_{n}
and hence Claim
8.8.1
follows from
Lemma
5.9
(1)
,
(3)
,
(4)

and the definition of
{\operatorname{tr}}_{\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{X}}
.

(3)
. By semipurity we can assume
D=\emptyset
. Since restriction to a dense open subset
is injective for
F\in{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
(e.g.,
[
Sai20a
, Thm 3.1]
) we can reduce to the case where

X
and
Y
are points and
f
is induced by a finite field extension; since both sides of the equality in

(3)
are transitive we can assume that this field extension is simple, so that
f
factors as
a closed immersion
Y\hookrightarrow\mathbf{P}^{1}_{X}
followed by the projection
\mathbf{P}^{1}_{X}\to X
.
In this situation
H^{0}(f_{*})
is equal to the composition

F(Y)\xrightarrow{\kappa_{1}}(\gamma^{1}{\widetilde{F}}(1))(Y)\xrightarrow{g_{Y/\mathbf{P}^{1}_{X}}}H^{1}(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{Y},{\widetilde{F}}(1)_{(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{Y},\emptyset)})\xrightarrow{{\operatorname{tr}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}_{X}/X}}(\gamma^{1}{\widetilde{F}}(1))(X)\xrightarrow{\kappa_{1}^{-1}}F(X).

In the following we set

\operatorname{Tr}_{h}:=(\Gamma_{h}^{t})^{*}:h_{*}F_{U^{\prime}}\to F_{U},

for a finite surjective morphism
h:U^{\prime}\to U
in
\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
.

Claim 8.8.2
.

Let
V
be a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{U}
-module of rank
n+1
. Set
P=\mathbf{P}(V)
and
P^{\prime}=\mathbf{P}(h^{*}V)

and denote by
h^{\prime}:P^{\prime}\to P
the base change of
h:U^{\prime}\to U
, so that we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{P^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{\prime}}
\textstyle{P\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{U^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h}
\textstyle{U.}

Let
G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
.
Then

\operatorname{Tr}_{h}\circ{\operatorname{tr}}_{P^{\prime}/U^{\prime}}={\operatorname{tr}}_{P/U}\circ\operatorname{Tr}_{h^{\prime}}:H^{i}(P^{\prime},G_{P^{\prime}})\to H^{i-n}(U,(\gamma^{n}G))_{U}).

for all
i
.

We prove the claim. Let
\lambda_{V}^{i}=c_{\xi^{i}}\circ\pi^{*}
be as in (
6.1
), where

\xi=c_{1}(\mathcal{O}_{P}(1))\in{\operatorname{CH}}^{1}(P)
and
\pi:P\to U
is the projection.
Then by the definition of
{\operatorname{tr}}_{P/U}
it suffices to show

\operatorname{Tr}_{h^{\prime}}\circ\lambda^{i}_{h^{*}V}=\lambda^{i}_{V}\circ\operatorname{Tr}_{h}:h_{*}(\gamma^{i}G)_{U^{\prime}}[-i]\to R\pi_{*}G_{P},\quad\text{for all }i.

We know
\pi^{*}\circ\operatorname{Tr}_{h}=\operatorname{Tr}_{h^{\prime}}\circ{\pi^{\prime}}^{*}
, since the pullback is compatible with the composition of finite correspondences.
Thus we are left to show the commutativity of

(8.8.1)

\textstyle{(\gamma^{i}F)_{P}[-i]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c_{\xi^{i}}}
\textstyle{F_{P}}
\textstyle{h^{\prime}_{*}(\gamma^{i}F)_{P^{\prime}}[-i]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Tr}_{h^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{c_{{h^{\prime}}^{*}\xi^{i}}}
\textstyle{h^{\prime}_{*}F_{P^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Tr}_{h^{\prime}}}

Using the definition of
c_{\xi^{i}}
in
5.8

and the explicit description (
5.5.2
) of the map (
5.5.1
)
we see that (
8.8.1
) follows from the projection formula

(\operatorname{Tr}_{h^{\prime}}(a)\otimes\beta\otimes\Delta_{P})=\operatorname{Tr}_{h^{\prime}}(a\otimes{h^{\prime}}^{*}\beta\otimes\Delta_{P^{\prime}})\quad\text{in }(G\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}\underline{\omega}^{*}K^{M}_{i})(P),

where
a\in G(P^{\prime})
,
\beta\in K^{M}_{i}(P)
,
\Delta_{P}
and
\Delta_{P^{\prime}}
are the respective diagonals.
The projection formula follows from the description of
\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}
, e.g.,
[
RSY22
, Lem 4.3]
,
and the equality of finite correspondences

(8.8.2)

({\operatorname{id}}_{P^{\prime}}\times\Gamma_{h^{\prime}})\circ\Gamma_{\Delta_{P^{\prime}}}\circ\Gamma^{t}_{h^{\prime}}=(\Gamma_{h^{\prime}}^{t}\times{\operatorname{id}}_{P})\circ\Gamma_{\Delta_{P}}\in\mathbf{Cor}(P,P^{\prime}\times P),

which can be deduced from the cartesian diagram

\textstyle{P^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{({\operatorname{id}}_{P^{\prime}}\times h^{\prime})\circ\Delta_{P^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{h^{\prime}}
\textstyle{P^{\prime}\times P\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h^{\prime}\times{\operatorname{id}}_{P}}
\textstyle{P\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Delta_{P}}
\textstyle{P\times P.}

This completes the proof of the claim.

We come back to the proof
(3)
. Consider the following commutative diagram

(8.8.3)

\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{P}^{1}_{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{1}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{P}^{1}_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{X,}

in which the vertical maps are the projections and the square is cartesian.
Clearly it suffices to show for
G={\widetilde{F}}(1)
and with the notation from above

(8.8.4)

\operatorname{Tr}_{f}={\operatorname{tr}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}_{X}/X}\circ g_{Y/\mathbf{P}^{1}_{X}}:(\gamma^{1}G)(Y)\to(\gamma^{1}G)(X).

We compute using (
8.8.3
)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Tr}_{f}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Tr}_{f}\circ({\operatorname{tr}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}_{Y}/Y}\circ g_{Y/\mathbf{P}^{1}_{Y}})

by Lem
8.3
(3)

\displaystyle={\operatorname{tr}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}_{X}/X}\circ Tr_{f_{1}}\circ g_{Y/\mathbf{P}^{1}_{Y}}

by Claim
8.8.2

\displaystyle={\operatorname{tr}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}_{X}/X}\circ g_{Y/\mathbf{P}^{1}_{X}}

by Lem
8.9
below.

This proves (
8.8.4
) and finishes the proof of the proposition.
∎

Lemma 8.9
.

Let
F\in{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
and
{\widetilde{F}}=\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
.
Let
f:X_{1}\to X
be a finite and surjective morphism in
\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
and let
Z
be a smooth

k
-scheme which comes with two closed immersions
i:Z\hookrightarrow X
and
i_{1}:Z\hookrightarrow X_{1}
both of codimension 1
such that
i=f\circ i_{1}
. Then the following diagram commutes in
D(X_{{\operatorname{Nis}}})

\textstyle{f_{*}i_{1*}(\gamma^{1}{\widetilde{F}})_{Z}[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{Z/X_{1}}}
\textstyle{f_{*}{\widetilde{F}}_{X_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\Gamma_{f}^{t})^{*}}
\textstyle{i_{*}(\gamma^{1}{\widetilde{F}})_{Z}[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{Z/X}}
\textstyle{{\widetilde{F}}_{X},}

where
\Gamma_{f}^{t}
is the transpose of the graph of
f
which we view in

\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}((X,\emptyset),(X_{1},\emptyset))=\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(X_{1},X)
.

Proof.

Note that
(\Gamma_{f}^{t})^{*}
also induces a morphism on the cohomology with supports

(8.9.1)

f_{*}R^{1}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z_{1}}F_{X_{1}}\to f_{*}R^{1}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{f^{-1}(Z)}F_{X_{1}}=R^{1}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}(f_{*}F_{X_{1}})\xrightarrow{(\Gamma_{f}^{t})^{*}}R^{1}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}F_{X},

where we use
R^{i}f_{*}=0
for all
i>0
, which holds by the finiteness of
f
. Here,
Z_{1}=i_{1}(Z)
, and the first arrow is the enlarge support map.
Using this map and the local Gysin map from Lemma
7.10
we see that the statement is local around

Z
. By Lemma
7.14
we can replace
X
by a Nisnevich neighborhood of
Z

to find a morphism
q:X\to Z
such that
q\circ i={\operatorname{id}}_{Z}
.
Set
q_{1}:=q\circ f:X_{1}\to Z
; it satisfies
q_{1}\circ i_{1}={\operatorname{id}}_{Z}
.
Whence Theorem
7.12
yields

g_{Z/X}=\imath\circ c_{Z}\circ q^{*}:i_{*}(\gamma^{1}F)_{Z}[-1]\to R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}F_{X}\to F_{X},

(\Gamma_{f}^{t})^{*}\circ g_{Z/X_{1}}=(\Gamma_{f}^{t})^{*}\circ\imath\circ c_{Z_{1}}\circ q_{1}^{*}:f_{*}i_{1*}(\gamma^{1}F)_{Z_{1}}[-1]\to f_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z_{1}}F_{X_{1}}\to f_{*}F_{X_{1}},

where
c_{Z}
(resp.
c_{Z_{1}}
) is defined viewing
Z\in{\operatorname{CH}}^{1}_{Z}(X)
(resp.
Z_{1}\in{\operatorname{CH}}^{1}_{Z_{1}}(X_{1})
)
and
\imath
is the forget support map in both cases.
Thus it suffices to show the equality

(8.9.2)

c_{Z}=\eqref{lem:gysinTRco11}\circ c_{Z_{1}}\circ f^{*}:(\gamma^{1}F)_{X}[-1]\to R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{Z}F_{X},

since clearly we also have the commutativity

(\Gamma^{t}_{f})^{*}\circ\iota=\iota\circ\eqref{lem:gysinTRco11}

where
\iota
is again the forget support map.

Again this statement is local in
Z
and we can therefore assume that there are global functions

d\in H^{0}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})
and
d_{1}\in H^{0}(X_{1},\mathcal{O}_{X_{1}})
with

Z=\operatorname{Div}_{X}(d)\quad\text{and}\quad Z_{1}=\operatorname{Div}_{X_{1}}(d_{1}).

Since as cycles we have
f_{*}Z_{1}=Z
we can (by
[
Ful98
, Prop 1.4]
) additionally choose
d
and
d_{1}
such that

(8.9.3)

\operatorname{Nm}_{X_{1}/X}(d_{1})=d.

Note that
\Gamma_{f}^{t}
also defines an element in
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}((X,Z),(X_{1},Z_{1}))
and thus Lemma
5.10

together with Remark
4.7
show that (
8.9.2
) is implied by
the commutativity of the following diagram

\textstyle{{\widetilde{F}}((\mathbf{A}^{1},0)\otimes X_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(
5.10.2
)
\textstyle{{\widetilde{F}}(X_{1},Z_{1})/F(X_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\Gamma_{f}^{t})^{*}}
\textstyle{{\widetilde{F}}((\mathbf{A}^{1},0)\otimes X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{({\operatorname{id}}\times f)^{*}}
(
5.10.2
)
\textstyle{{\widetilde{F}}(X,Z)/F(X).}

We prove the commutativity of the above diagram.
By (
5.10.3
) it suffices to show

(8.9.4)

(\Gamma_{f}^{t})^{*}\Delta_{X_{1}}^{*}(d_{1}\times{\operatorname{id}}_{X_{1}})^{*}({\operatorname{id}}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}}\times f)^{*}=\Delta_{X}^{*}(d\times{\operatorname{id}}_{X})^{*}:{\widetilde{F}}((\mathbf{A}^{1},0)\times X)\to{\widetilde{F}}(X,Z)/F(X),

where by abuse of notation we denote by
d:(X,Z)\to(\mathbf{A}^{1},0)

(resp.
d_{1}:(X_{1},Z)\to(\mathbf{A}^{1},0)
) the morphisms of modulus pairs induced by
d
(resp.
d_{1}
)
and by
\Delta_{X}:(X,Z)\to(X,Z)\otimes X
(resp
\Delta_{X_{1}}:(X_{1},Z)\to(X_{1},Z)\otimes X_{1}
) the diagonal map.
We have

(8.9.5)

\displaystyle(\Gamma_{f}^{t})^{*}\Delta_{X_{1}}^{*}(d_{1}\times{\operatorname{id}}_{X_{1}})^{*}({\operatorname{id}}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}}\times f)^{*}

\displaystyle=(\Gamma_{f}^{t})^{*}\Delta_{X_{1}}^{*}({\operatorname{id}}_{X_{1}}\times f)^{*}(d_{1}\times{\operatorname{id}}_{X})^{*}

\displaystyle=\Delta_{X}^{*}(\Gamma_{f}^{t}\times{\operatorname{id}}_{X})^{*}(d_{1}\times{\operatorname{id}}_{X})^{*},

where the second equality is induced by (
8.8.2
).
Note that the graph of
d_{1}
in
X_{1}\times\mathbf{A}^{1}
is given by
V(t-d_{1})
, where
t
is the coordinate of
\mathbf{A}^{1}
.
As in
[
Ful98
, Prop 16.1.1, Prop 1.4]
we find

(8.9.6)

(\Gamma_{f}^{t}\times{\operatorname{id}}_{X})^{*}(d_{1}\times{\operatorname{id}}_{X})^{*}=((f\times{\operatorname{id}}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}})_{*}(\Gamma_{d_{1}})\times{\operatorname{id}}_{X})^{*}=(\operatorname{Div}_{X\times\mathbf{A}^{1}}(P(t))\times{\operatorname{id}}_{X})^{*},

where
P(t)=\operatorname{Nm}_{X_{1}[t]/X[t]}(t-d_{1})\in\mathcal{O}(X)[t]
is the minimal polynomial of
d_{1}
over
k(X)
.
Note that by (
8.9.3
) we have

P(t)=t^{n}-\operatorname{Tr}_{X_{1}/X}(d_{1})t^{n-1}+\ldots+(-1)^{n}d,

where
n=\deg(X_{1}/X)
.
Putting (
8.9.5
) and (
8.9.6
) together and setting

G=\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\operatorname{\mathbb{Z}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}}}(X),{\widetilde{F}})\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
we find that (
8.9.4
) is implied by

(8.9.7)

\operatorname{Div}_{X\times\mathbf{A}^{1}}(t-d)^{*}=\operatorname{Div}_{X\times\mathbf{A}^{1}}(P(t))^{*}:G(\mathbf{A}^{1},0)\to G(X,Z)/G(X),

where we view
\operatorname{Div}_{X\times\mathbf{A}^{1}}(t-d),\operatorname{Div}_{X\times\mathbf{A}^{1}}(P(t))\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}((X,Z),(\mathbf{A}^{1},0))
.
To show this we can shrink
X
around the generic point of
Z
(by purity, see
[
Sai20a
, Cor 8.6(1)]
).
Thus in the following we assume
Z\in X
is a point with residue
K=k(Z)
.
The map
q:X\to Z
from the beginning of the proof induces a morphism

X\to\operatorname{Spec}K[d]
which is étale.
By
[
Sai20a
, Rmk 2.2(1), Lem 4.2, Lem 4.3]

we see that
(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{K}=\operatorname{Spec}K[t],0_{K})
and
(X,Z)
are
V
-pairs over
K
in the sense of

[
Sai20a
, Def 2.1]
. There is a canonical identification
0_{K}\cong Z
.
We claim that
g\in\{\frac{t-d}{t-1},\frac{P(t)}{(t-1)^{n}}\}
is admissible for the pair

((\mathbf{A}^{1}_{K},0_{K}),(X,Z))
in the sense of
[
Sai20a
, Def 2.3]
, i.e., we have to show

(1)

g
is regular in a neighborhood of
X\times_{K}0_{K}
;

(2)

\operatorname{Div}_{X\times\mathbf{A}^{1}}(g)\times_{\mathbf{A}^{1}}0_{K}=\Delta_{0_{K}}
, where

\Delta_{0_{K}}:0_{K}\hookrightarrow X\times_{K}0_{K}
is the diagonal (via the identification
0_{K}=Z
fixed above);

(3)

g
extends to an invertible function in a neighborhood of
X\times_{K}\infty_{K}

in
X\times_{K}\mathbf{P}^{1}_{K}
.

All points are immediate to check.
Therefore
[
Sai20a
, Thm 2.10(2)]
yields

\operatorname{Div}_{X\times_{K}\mathbf{A}^{1}_{K}}(\tfrac{t-d}{t-1})^{*}=\operatorname{Div}_{X\times_{K}\mathbf{A}^{1}_{K}}(\tfrac{P(t)}{(t-1)^{n}})^{*}:G(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{K},0_{K})/G(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{K})\to G(X,Z)/G(X).

Since
\operatorname{Div}_{X\times\mathbf{A}^{1}}(t-1)^{*}(G(\mathbf{A}^{1},0))\subset G(X)
,
this implies (
8.9.7
) and completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

9.
Proper correspondence action on reciprocity sheaves

In this section we fix a reciprocity sheaf
F\in{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
and
set
{\widetilde{F}}:=\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
, see (
1.6.1
).

9.1.
Pushforward and cycle cupping for reciprocity sheaves

9.1
.

Recall the twists of reciprocity sheaves from
[
RSY22
, 5e]
:
For
n\geq 1
we define recursively

(9.1.1)

F\langle 0\rangle:=F,\quad F\langle n\rangle:=\underline{\omega}_{!}(\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}(F\langle n-1\rangle)(1)),

where
(-)(1)
denotes the twist from Definition
4.4
.
Thus
F\langle n\rangle\in{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
and
F\langle m+n\rangle=F\langle m\rangle\langle n\rangle
, for all
m,n\geq 0
.
There exists a natural surjective map

(9.1.2)

\underline{\omega}_{!}({\widetilde{F}}(n))\rightarrow\!\!\!\!\!\rightarrow F\langle n\rangle,

which is defined as follows:
Let
G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
; twisting the adjunction map
G\to\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\underline{\omega}_{!}G
by
(1)
and applying

\underline{\omega}_{!}
, yields a map
\underline{\omega}_{!}(G(1))\to(\underline{\omega}_{!}G)\langle 1\rangle
.
For
G=\tilde{F}
this yields (
9.1.2
) for
n=1
, and in general we define it recursively by

\underline{\omega}_{!}({\widetilde{F}}(n))=\underline{\omega}_{!}({\widetilde{F}}(n-1)(1))\to\underline{\omega}_{!}({\widetilde{F}}(n-1))\langle 1\rangle\to F\langle n-1\rangle\langle 1\rangle=F\langle n\rangle.

The surjectivity holds by
[
RSY22
, 5e(4)]
.
It is not known in general whether this is an isomorphism.

We also define recursively for
n\geq 0

(9.1.3)

\gamma^{0}F:=F,\quad\gamma^{n}F:=\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},\gamma^{n-1}F).

It follows from
[
MS
, Prop 2.10]
that for
G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
we have

(9.1.4)

\gamma^{n}\underline{\omega}_{!}G=\underline{\omega}_{!}\gamma^{n}G\in{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}.

We obtain an isomorphism

(9.1.5)

\gamma^{n}F\langle n\rangle\cong\gamma^{n-1}\underline{\omega}_{!}\gamma^{1}(\widetilde{(F\langle n-1\rangle)}(1))\cong\gamma^{n-1}\underline{\omega}_{!}\widetilde{(F\langle n-1\rangle)}\\
\cong\gamma^{n-1}F\langle n-1\rangle\cong F,

where the first isomorphism holds by definition and (
9.1.4
),
the second by the weak cancellation theorem
[
MS
, Cor 3.6]
,
the third by
\underline{\omega}_{!}\tau_{!}\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}={\operatorname{id}}
, and the forth by induction.
It is direct to check that the composition

(9.1.6)

F=\underline{\omega}_{!}{\widetilde{F}}\xrightarrow[\simeq]{\kappa_{n}}\underline{\omega}_{!}(\gamma^{n}{\widetilde{F}}(n))\xrightarrow[\simeq]{\eqref{para:RSCtwist4}}\gamma^{n}\underline{\omega}_{!}{\widetilde{F}}(n)\xrightarrow{\eqref{para:RSC:twist2}}\gamma^{n}F\langle n\rangle\xrightarrow[\simeq]{\eqref{para:RSCtwist6}}F

is the identity.

Lemma 9.2
.

The functor
\gamma^{n}:{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}\to{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
is exact, for all
n\geq 0
.
Furthermore, if char
(k)=0
, and

0\to G_{1}\to G_{2}\to G_{3}\to 0

is an exact sequence in
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}NST}}
with
G_{i}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
, then so is

0\to\gamma^{n}(G_{1})\to\gamma^{n}(G_{2})\to\gamma^{n}(G_{3})\to 0.

Proof.

First recall that
{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
is an abelian category, by
[
Sai20a
, Thm 0.1]
, and
that a sequence
0\to F_{1}\to F_{2}\to F_{3}\to 0
in
{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
is exact if and only if
the sequence
0\to(F_{1})_{X}\to(F_{2})_{X}\to(F_{3})_{X}\to 0
of sheaves on
X_{\operatorname{Nis}}
is exact, for any
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
.
It suffices to consider the case
n=1
.
Given a short exact sequence in
{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
as above we obtain for
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}

a short exact sequence on
\mathbf{P}^{1}_{X,{\operatorname{Nis}}}

0\to({\widetilde{F}}_{1})_{(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{X},\emptyset)}\to({\widetilde{F}}_{2})_{(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{X},\emptyset)}\to({\widetilde{F}}_{3})_{(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{X},\emptyset)}\to 0.

Applying
R\pi_{*}
, with
\pi:\mathbf{P}^{1}_{X}\to X
the structure map we get a short exact sequence

0\to R^{1}\pi_{*}({\widetilde{F}}_{1})_{(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{X},\emptyset)}\to R^{1}\pi_{*}({\widetilde{F}}_{2})_{(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{X},\emptyset)}\to R^{1}\pi_{*}({\widetilde{F}}_{3})_{(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{X},\emptyset)}\to 0

using the fact that
\pi_{*}({\widetilde{F}}_{3})_{(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{X},\emptyset)}=({\widetilde{F}}_{3})_{(X,\emptyset)}
.
Applying the projective bundle formula (see Theorem
6.3
) yields an exact sequence

0\to(\underline{\omega}_{!}\gamma^{1}{\widetilde{F}}_{1})_{X}\to(\underline{\omega}_{!}\gamma^{1}{\widetilde{F}}_{2})_{X}\to(\underline{\omega}_{!}\gamma^{1}{\widetilde{F}}_{3})_{X}\to 0.

The first statement follows from (
9.1.4
).

Now assume char
(k)=0
. We show the second statement. Since
\gamma
is left exact on
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}

it suffices to show the surjectivity. By Lemma
1.3
, Corollary
4.5
,
and resolution of singularities it suffices to show
\gamma(G_{2})_{\mathcal{X}}\to\gamma(G_{3})_{\mathcal{X}}

is surjective for all
\mathcal{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}_{ls}
. This follows from the projective bundle formula, Theorem
6.3
, as above.
∎

Proposition 9.3
.

For
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
, we have
{\underline{\omega}}_{!}\gamma^{n}F=\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathcal{K}^{M}_{n},{\underline{\omega}}_{!}F)
. In particular, for
F\in{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
, we have
\gamma^{n}F=\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathcal{K}^{M}_{n},F)
. (This is
[
MS
, Prop 2.10]
for
n=1
.)

Proof.

Thanks to (
9.1.4
) and (
4.5.1
), we have to show that the natural morphism

(9.3.1)

{\underline{\omega}}_{!}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathcal{K}^{M}_{n},F)\to\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\underline{\omega}_{!}\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathcal{K}^{M}_{n},\underline{\omega}_{!}F)=\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathcal{K}^{M}_{n},\underline{\omega}_{!}F)

is an isomorphism for every
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
. Evaluating both sides of (
9.3.1
) on
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
, we see that we can replace
F
by
F^{X}=\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{tr}(X),F)
and are left to show that

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathcal{K}^{M}_{n},\underline{\omega}_{!}F)=(\underline{\omega}_{!}\gamma^{n}F)(k)=(\gamma^{n}F)(k).

Indeed, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathcal{K}^{M}_{n},\underline{\omega}_{!}F)

\displaystyle\cong^{(1)}\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathcal{K}^{M}_{n},\underline{\omega}^{*}\underline{\omega}_{!}F)

\displaystyle\cong^{(2)}\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathcal{K}^{M}_{n},\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\underline{\omega}_{!}F)

\displaystyle\cong^{(3)}(\gamma^{n}\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\underline{\omega}_{!}F)(k)

\displaystyle\cong^{(4)}H^{n}(\mathbf{P}^{n}_{k},(\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\underline{\omega}_{!}F)_{(\mathbf{P}^{n}_{k},\emptyset)})

\displaystyle\cong^{(5)}H^{n}(\mathbf{P}^{n}_{k},F_{(\mathbf{P}^{n}_{k},\emptyset)})\cong^{(6)}(\gamma^{n}F)(k)

where the isomorphism
(1)
follows from the fact that
\underline{\omega}^{*}
is fully faithful,
(2)
follows from the fact that

\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathcal{K}^{M}_{n}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}
, by adjunction and definition of
\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}
,
(3)
is (
4.5.1
),
the isomorphisms
(4)
and
(6)
follow from Theorem
6.3
and isomorphism
(5)
by

\underline{\omega}_{!}\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}={\operatorname{id}}
.
∎

9.4
.

Let
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
, let
\Phi
be a family of supports on
X
, and let
\alpha\in{\operatorname{CH}}^{r}_{\Phi}(X)
.
Then we define

C_{\alpha}:F_{X}\to R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}F\langle r\rangle_{X}[r]

as the composition

F_{X}={\widetilde{F}}_{(X,\emptyset)}\xrightarrow{\kappa_{r}}\gamma^{r}{\widetilde{F}}(r)_{(X,\emptyset)}\xrightarrow{c_{\alpha}}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}{\widetilde{F}}(r)_{(X,\emptyset)}[r]\xrightarrow{\eqref{para:RSC:twist2}}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}F\langle r\rangle_{X}[r].

We note that
C_{\alpha}
satisfies the analogous properties of
c_{\alpha}
listed in Lemma
5.9
.
This is immediate for
5.9
(1)
-
(3)
.
We give the argument for the analogous property of Lemma
5.9
(4)
:
Let
\alpha\in{\operatorname{CH}}^{r}_{\Phi}(X)
and
\beta\in{\operatorname{CH}}^{s}_{\Psi}(X)
.
Set
m=i+j
and consider the following diagram in which we omit all the supports for readability

\textstyle{F_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\kappa_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\kappa_{m}}
\textstyle{\gamma^{i}{\widetilde{F}}(i)_{(X,\emptyset)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma^{i}(\kappa_{j})}
\scriptstyle{c_{\alpha}}
\textstyle{{\widetilde{F}}(i)_{(X,\emptyset)}[i]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(
9.1.2
)
\scriptstyle{\kappa_{j}}
\textstyle{F\langle i\rangle_{X}[i]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\kappa_{j}}
\textstyle{\gamma^{m}{\widetilde{F}}(m)_{(X,\emptyset)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c_{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{c_{\alpha\cdot\beta}}
\textstyle{\gamma^{j}{\widetilde{F}}(m)_{(X,\emptyset)}[i]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c_{\beta}}
\scriptstyle{\eqref{para:RSC:twist2}^{a}}
\textstyle{\gamma^{j}\widetilde{F\langle i\rangle}(j)_{(X,\emptyset)}[i]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c_{\beta}}
\textstyle{{\widetilde{F}}(m)_{(X,\emptyset)}[m]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eqref{para:RSC:twist2}^{a}}
(
9.1.2
)
\textstyle{\widetilde{F\langle i\rangle}(j)_{(X,\emptyset)}[m]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(
9.1.2
)
\textstyle{F\langle m\rangle_{X}[m],}

where
\eqref{para:RSC:twist2}^{a}:{\widetilde{G}}(n)\to\widetilde{G\langle n\rangle}
,
G\in{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
, is induced
by adjunction from (
9.1.2
). All the squares in the diagram commute by functoriality,
the top triangle commutes by (
4.8.3
), the triangle in the middle commutes by
Lemma
5.9
(4)
, and the commutativity of the bottom triangle follows from
the definition of the map (
9.1.2
). Hence the whole diagram commutes and we obtain

C_{\alpha\cdot\beta}=C_{\beta}\circ C_{\alpha}
.

9.5
.

Let
f:Y\to X
be quasi-projective in
\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
, let
\Phi
be a family of proper supports for
Y/X

and
\Psi
a family of supports on
X
such that
\Phi\subset f^{-1}\Psi
.
Set
r:=\dim Y-\dim X\in\mathbb{Z}
. For
a\geq 0
with
a+r\geq 0
and
b\geq 0
we define

f_{*}:Rf_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}\gamma^{b}(F\langle a+r\rangle)_{Y}[r]\to R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Psi}\gamma^{b}(F\langle a\rangle)_{X}

as follows: let
Y\xrightarrow{i}U\xrightarrow{\pi}X
be a factorization as in (
8.5.1
) with
c={\operatorname{codim}}(Y,U)
and
n=\dim(U/X)
,
so that
r=n-c
; let
e\geq c
, then we define
f_{*}
as the composition

\displaystyle Rf_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}\gamma^{b}(F\langle a+r\rangle)_{Y}[r]

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\eqref{para:RSCtwist4}}Rf_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}\gamma^{b}(\widetilde{F\langle a+r\rangle})_{(Y,\emptyset)}[r]

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\kappa_{e}}Rf_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}\gamma^{b+e}(\widetilde{F\langle a+r\rangle}(e))_{(Y,\emptyset)}[r]

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{(i,\pi)_{*}^{e}}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Psi}\gamma^{b+e+r}(\widetilde{F\langle a+r\rangle}(e))_{(X,\emptyset)}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\eqref{para:RSCtwist4}}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Psi}\gamma^{b+e+r}\underline{\omega}_{!}(\widetilde{F\langle a+r\rangle}(e))_{X}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\eqref{para:RSC:twist2}}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Psi}\gamma^{b+e+r}(F\langle a+e+r\rangle)_{X}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\eqref{para:RSCtwist6}}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Psi}\gamma^{b}(F\langle a\rangle)_{X}.

It follows from Proposition
8.6
(1)
that
f_{*}
is independent of the choice of
the factorization (
8.5.1
) and it follows from the commutativity of (
4.8.3
)
that it is independent of the choice of
e
.

Remark 9.6
.

By (
9.1.5
) we have

\gamma^{b}(F\langle a\rangle)=\begin{cases}F\langle a-b\rangle&a\geq b\\
\gamma^{b-a}F&a\leq b.\end{cases}

Above we work with
\gamma^{b}(F\langle a\rangle)
so that we don’t have to distinguish the two cases.

Theorem 9.7
.

Let
f:Y\to X
,
\Phi,\Psi
,
a,r,b
be as in
9.5
.

(1)

{\operatorname{id}}_{X*}:F_{X}\to F_{X}
is the identity.

(2)

Let
g:Z\to Y
be another quasi-projective morphism in
\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
of relative dimension
s=\dim Z-\dim Y
and
let
\Xi
be a family of proper supports for
Z/Y
with
\Xi\subset g^{-1}\Phi
, then
for
a\geq 0
with
a+r+s\geq 0
and
a+r\geq 0
and
b\geq 0
we have

(f\circ g)_{*}=f_{*}\circ g_{*}:R(f\circ g)_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Xi}\gamma^{b}(F\langle a+r+s\rangle)_{Z}[r+s]\to R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Psi}\gamma^{b}(F\langle a\rangle)_{X}.

(3)

Let
h:X^{\prime}\to X
be a smooth morphism. We form the base change diagram

\textstyle{Y^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{X^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h}
\textstyle{X.}

Set
\Psi^{\prime}=h^{-1}\Psi
,
\Phi^{\prime}={h^{\prime}}^{-1}\Phi
.
The following diagram commutes

\textstyle{Rf_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}\gamma^{b}(F\langle a+r\rangle)_{Y}[r]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{*}}
\scriptstyle{{h^{\prime}}^{*}}
\textstyle{R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Psi}\gamma^{b}(F\langle a\rangle)_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h^{*}}
\textstyle{R(fh^{\prime})_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi^{\prime}}\gamma^{b}(F\langle a+r\rangle)_{Y^{\prime}}[r]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}_{*}}
\textstyle{Rh_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Psi^{\prime}}\gamma^{b}(F\langle a\rangle)_{X^{\prime}}.}

(4)

Let
\Xi
be some family of supports and
\alpha\in{\operatorname{CH}}^{s}_{\Xi}(X)
.
Then
f^{*}\alpha\in{\operatorname{CH}}^{s}_{f^{-1}\Xi}(Y)
and the following diagram commutes

\textstyle{Rf_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}\gamma^{b}(F\langle a+r\rangle)_{Y}[r]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{C_{f^{*}\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{f_{*}}
\textstyle{Rf_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi\cap f^{-1}\Xi}\gamma^{b}(F\langle a+r+s\rangle)_{Y}[r+s]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{*}}
\textstyle{R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Psi}\gamma^{b}(F\langle a\rangle)_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{C_{\alpha}}
\textstyle{R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Psi\cap\Xi}\gamma^{b}(F\langle a+s\rangle)_{X}[s].}

(5)

Let
\beta\in{\operatorname{CH}}^{s}_{\Phi}(Y)
.
Then
f_{*}\beta\in{\operatorname{CH}}^{s-r}_{\Psi}(X)
and the following diagram commutes

\textstyle{Rf_{*}F_{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{C_{\beta}}
\textstyle{Rf_{*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi}F\langle s\rangle_{Y}[s]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{*}}
\textstyle{F_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{C_{f_{*}\beta}}
\textstyle{R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Psi}F\langle s-r\rangle_{X}[s-r].}

Proof.

(1)
follows from Lemma
8.3
(3)
and from (
9.1.6
)
being the identity.

(2)
follows from Proposition
8.6
(2)
and (
4.8.3
).

(3)
follows from Proposition
7.9
and Lemma
8.3
(2)
.

(4)
follows from Proposition
7.8
and by the definition of
{\operatorname{tr}}_{\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{X}}

from the following equality for a projective bundle
\pi:P=\mathbf{P}(V)\to X

c_{\pi^{*}\alpha}\circ\lambda^{i}_{V}=c_{\pi^{*}\alpha}\circ c_{\xi^{i}}\circ\pi^{*}=\lambda_{V}^{i}\circ c_{\alpha},

which follows from Lemma
5.9
.

(5)
By Corollary
7.13
we are reduced to the case where

f=\pi:\mathbf{P}(V)\to X
is a projective bundle with
V
locally free of rank
r+1
and
\Phi=Y
,

\Psi=X
. In this case we can write
\beta=\sum_{i=0}^{r}\xi^{i}\cdot\pi^{*}\alpha_{i}
, with

\alpha_{i}\in{\operatorname{CH}}^{i}(X)
and
\xi^{i}=c_{1}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{P}}(1))^{i}
. Thus
\pi_{*}\beta=\alpha_{r}
and hence
the commutativity of the diagram follows in this case from the definition of
{\operatorname{tr}}_{P/X}
and the equality

c_{\beta}\circ\pi^{*}=\sum_{i=0}^{r}\lambda^{i}_{V}\circ c_{\alpha_{i}},

which holds by Lemma
5.9
(1)
,
(3)
,
(4)
.
∎

9.2.
Proper correspondence action

In this subsection we fix a scheme
S
separated and of finite type over
k
.

9.8
.

We denote by
C_{S}
the category with objects the
S
-schemes
X\to S
with the property that
the induced map
X\to\operatorname{Spec}k
is smooth and quasi-projective; the morphisms are given by

C_{S}(X,Y)=\bigoplus_{i}{\operatorname{CH}}^{\dim Y_{i}}_{\Phi^{\rm prop}_{X\times_{S}Y_{i}}}(X\times Y_{i}),

where for simplicity we write
X
instead of
X\to S
,
where
Y=\sqcup_{i}Y_{i}
is the decomposition into connected components,
and where
\Phi^{\rm prop}_{X\times_{S}Y_{i}}
is the family of supports on
X\times Y_{i}
consisting of those
closed subsets which are contained in
X\times_{S}Y_{i}
and are proper over
X
;
the composition is defined by

C_{S}(X_{1},X_{2})\times C_{S}(X_{2},X_{3})\to C_{S}(X_{1},X_{3}),

(\alpha,\beta)\mapsto\beta\circ\alpha:=p_{13*}(p_{12}^{*}\alpha\cdot p_{23}^{*}\beta),

where
p_{i,j}:X_{1}\times X_{2}\times X_{3}\to X_{i}\times X_{j}
are the projections, the pullbacks

p_{ij}^{*}
are induced by flat pullback,
the intersection product is given by (
5.4.8
), and the pushforward is well-defined
since
p_{13}
is proper along

(\Phi^{\rm prop}_{X_{1}\times_{S}X_{2}}\times_{k}X_{3})\cap(X_{1}\times_{k}\Phi^{\rm prop}_{X_{2}\times_{S}X_{3}})

and maps this family of supports into
\Phi^{\rm prop}_{X_{1}\times_{S}X_{3}}
.
It follows from
[
CR11
, Prop 1.1.34, Prop 1.3.10]
that
C_{S}
is a category
and the identity in
C_{S}(X,X)
is induced by the diagonal
\Delta\subset X\times_{S}X

(cf. also
[
Ful98
, Prop 16.1.1]
).

Note that for
S=\operatorname{Spec}k
and
X,Y\in C_{S}
we have a natural map
\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(X,Y)\to C_{S}(X,Y)

which is compatible with composition.

9.9
.

Let
F\in{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
.
Let
S
be a
k
-scheme, let
(f:X\to S)
,
(g:Y\to S)\in C_{S}
.
For
\alpha\in C_{S}(X,Y)
we define a morphism in
D^{+}(S_{\operatorname{Nis}})

(9.9.1)

\alpha^{*}:Rg_{*}F_{Y}\to Rf_{*}F_{X}

as follows: set
{\widetilde{F}}:=\tau_{!}\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F
; it suffices to consider the case that
Y
is of pure dimension
d
;
then (
9.9.1
) is defined to be the composition

\displaystyle Rg_{*}F_{Y}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{p_{Y}^{*}}Rg_{*}Rp_{Y*}F_{X\times Y}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{C_{\alpha}}Rg_{*}Rp_{Y*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi^{\rm prop}_{X\times_{S}Y}}F\langle d\rangle_{X\times Y}[d]

\displaystyle\cong Rf_{*}Rp_{X*}R{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi^{\rm prop}_{X\times_{S}Y}}F\langle d\rangle_{X\times Y}[d]

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{p_{X*}}Rf_{*}F_{X},

where
C_{\alpha}
is defined in
9.4
,
p_{X},p_{Y}:X\times Y\to X,Y
denote the projections,

p_{X*}
is the pushforward from
9.5
, and
the isomorphism in the third line follows from the equality

g_{*}p_{Y*}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi^{\rm prop}_{X\times_{S}Y}}(G)=f_{*}p_{X*}{\underline{\Gamma}}_{\Phi^{\rm prop}_{X\times_{S}Y}}(G),

for any sheaf
G
on
X\times Y
.

Proposition 9.10
.

Let the assumptions be as in
9.9
above.

(1)

Let
